# Z3 technique inventory

**One flat, checkable list of what Z3 actually implements** — decision
procedures, preprocessing passes, and search techniques — organised so it can be
diffed against another solver. A sibling lane does the diff; this file is one
complete side of it, and deliberately does not rank by importance or mention
axeyum.

## What was read

| | |
|---|---|
| Source | `references/z3` — shallow clone of `https://github.com/Z3Prover/z3` |
| Commit | `e18d63bda04fcab8240eb55314d567db3e43d540` ("Remove build_z3.bat (#10754)") |
| Commit date | 2026-09-08, i.e. one day before this inventory |
| Read on | 2026-09-09 |
| Method | C++ source read directly. Nothing was built or run. |

`references/` is gitignored; `scripts/fetch-references.sh` repopulates it. If
you re-run any citation below, check the commit first — line numbers move.

## Entry format

Every entry is:

```
### <name>
- **Where**: path:line
- **Logics**: which SMT-LIB logics reach it
- **Default**: on by default / behind param <p> / behind tactic <t> / dead / did not verify
- **Does**: one line
- **Provenance**: [C] read from source | [P] from a paper | [I] inference
```

**Exhaustive beats deep.** A name plus a path plus one line is a complete entry.
There are no treatises here on purpose.

## Honesty rules this file was written under

- **"did not verify" is a complete answer** and appears throughout. It means
  the field was not confirmed in source, not that the answer is no.
- **Every `path:line` came from a real `grep -n` / `sed -n`** against the commit
  above. None were guessed or recalled.
- **No technique is listed because a paper describes it.** Z3's papers describe
  things the shipping solver does not enable. Where a paper is the source of a
  characterisation it carries `[P]` and says whether the code was checked.
- **Dead code is a first-class finding**, not an omission. Code behind
  `if (false && …)`, `#if 0`, a parameter that no caller ever sets, a file
  absent from its `CMakeLists.txt`, or a plugin never constructed is called out
  in a `Dead or unreachable` section per area and indexed at the end.

## How this was produced, and where it is thin

Eight parallel read-only passes over disjoint directories, each returning a flat
inventory with source citations, plus a coordinating pass that did the dispatch
layer, the second SMT core, optimization, and the glue.

**Every citation in this file was machine-audited.** The 1,167 distinct
`src/…:line` references, spanning 612 distinct files, were each checked against
the commit above for file existence and line range: 0 name a file that does not
exist, and 0 point past the end of their file. Two errors were found and fixed
in that audit (`theory_arith.h` for `theory_arith_core.h`, and an off-by-one),
which is the extent of the citation defects found. Content correctness at each
line was spot-checked, not exhaustively re-derived — the audit proves the
pointers resolve, not that every one-line description is right.

Known thin spots, stated up front:

- **Per-logic reachability is the least reliable column.** Z3 has three
  independent dispatch layers (logic → tactic, logic → incremental core, and
  `static_features` → configuration) and a technique can be reachable through
  one and not the others. Where a lane could not trace all three it says "did
  not verify". Treat "Logics: all" as a claim about the layer that lane read.
- **`src/api/` was surveyed for entry points, not implementations.** The
  API-only list names what exists; whether each is reachable from `(check-sat)`
  was not traced.
- **One lane's dead-code conclusion is corrected below** (see *Corrections*);
  others were spot-checked but not exhaustively re-derived.

## Corrections applied to lane findings

- **The classic `theory_arith` (Simplex + Gomory cuts + the old ast-based
  Gröbner basis in `src/math/grobner/grobner.cpp`) is NOT dead code.** A lane
  reported it as unreachable on the evidence that `grep -rn "alloc(smt::theory_arith"`
  returns zero hits. That grep is looking for the template name; what is
  allocated is its typedefs. `src/smt/smt_setup.cpp` registers
  `smt::theory_mi_arith` and `smt::theory_i_arith` at lines 292, 304, 360, 378,
  405, 420, 425, 594, 603, 617, 683, 690 — all of them `theory_arith<Ext>`
  instantiations, all guarded by `m_arith_mode == AS_OLD_ARITH`, i.e.
  `smt.arith.solver=2` (`src/params/theory_arith_params.cpp:27`,
  `theory_arith_params.h:28`). The default is `AS_NEW_ARITH`
  (`theory_arith_params.h:57`, from `smt.arith.solver=6`), so the correct
  statement is **"not reachable at the default `arith.solver`, reachable at
  `smt.arith.solver=2`"** — a very different thing from dead. Note also that
  `src/math/grobner/` holds *two* engines: the old ast-based `grobner.cpp` used
  by `theory_arith`, and the PDD-based `pdd_solver.cpp` / `pdd_simplifier.cpp`
  used by `nla_grobner` and `sat_anf_simplifier`. Do not conflate them. `[C]`

- **`euf::ac_plugin` is not dead.** A lane reported it as the strongest
  dead-code candidate in `src/ast/euf/` on the evidence that no `alloc(ac_plugin,
  …)` exists outside the directory. It is used by *composition*, not
  construction: `euf::arith_plugin` holds two of them as members
  (`src/ast/euf/euf_arith_plugin.h:31`, `ac_plugin m_add, m_mul`) and
  `euf::specrel_plugin` allocates them per declaration
  (`src/ast/euf/euf_specrel_plugin.cpp:37`), and `specrel_plugin` has a
  production caller (`src/sat/smt/specrel_solver.cpp:32`). `[C]`

- **`euf::arith_plugin` and `euf::bv_plugin` have production construction
  sites**, at `src/ast/simplifiers/euf_completion.cpp:96` and `:97`. The same
  lane reported them as test-only. They are, however, reachable only through the
  `euf-completion` simplifier — which is not in the default preprocessing
  pipeline (`src/solver/solver_preprocess.cpp:85` still carries
  `// add: euf_completion?`). So "not in a default path" is right; "no
  production caller" is not. `[C]`

- **General shape of both corrections**: grepping for a direct construction site
  under-reports reachability, because C++ subsystems are wired by member
  composition as often as by allocation. Where a `Default: dead / no caller`
  row below rests on a construction-site grep alone, treat it as "no direct
  construction site found", not as proof of deadness.

# Part 1 — Dispatch, default preprocessing, logic classification

## Headline findings

Not a ranking of importance — these are the entries most likely to be *wrong* in
someone's mental model of Z3, and the dead code, because a technique that is
present but unreachable is worth as much to a diff as one that is absent.

1. **Z3 has three SMT cores, and the newest one is off for every logic.**
   `src/smt/` (classic `smt_context`), `src/sat/smt/` (the `sat.euf` engine: 87
   files, ~29k lines, a full second implementation of arithmetic, BV, arrays,
   datatypes, FPA, quantifiers, recfun, PB), and `sat_smt_solver`. Both
   `sat.euf` and `sat.smt` default **False**, and the `euf` parameter's own doc
   string says *"this feature is preliminary and not ready for general
   consumption"* (`src/params/sat_params.pyg:63`). No SMT-LIB logic selects it.
   Meanwhile the EUF core has **no sequence/string theory at all** —
   `euf_solver.cpp:138`–`:161` has no seq branch — so strings exist only in the
   classic core.

2. **Every query runs two solvers, and which one answers depends on whether you
   pushed.** `mk_smt_strategic_solver_factory` builds
   `mk_combined_solver(tactic_pipeline, incremental_core)`
   (`smt_strategic_solver.cpp:186`). Solver1 (the tactic pipeline) handles the
   non-incremental case; the first `push`, or an assertion after a `check-sat`,
   switches permanently to solver2. For `QF_BV`, solver2 is the **bit-blasting
   SAT solver**, not the SMT core, because `rewriter.hi_div0` defaults true
   (`smt_strategic_solver.cpp:144`).

3. **The default arithmetic engine is `theory_lra` (`arith.solver=6`), which
   makes the entire classic `theory_arith` — Simplex, Gomory cuts, the old
   ast-based Gröbner module, all of difference logic, UTVPI — reachable only at
   non-default `smt.arith.solver` values.** It is not dead (see *Corrections*),
   but no default configuration touches it. The live nonlinear machinery is
   `src/math/lp/nla_*`, driven by 41 distinct `arith.nl.*` parameters, each
   naming a separate lemma family or heuristic.

4. **A user-settable parameter value whose only implementation is a throw.**
   `smt.bv.solver=1` is documented as "polysat" in
   `src/params/smt_params_helper.pyg:63`; `src/sat/smt/euf_solver.cpp:143` reads
   `throw default_exception("polysat solver is not integrated")`. Similarly
   `opt.maxsat_engine=core_maxsat` is advertised in `opt_params.pyg`'s help
   string and has no branch in the dispatch (`src/opt/maxsmt.cpp:183`–`:211`),
   and `sat.local_search_mode=qsat` is documented in `sat_params.pyg` while the
   enum (`sat_config.h:82`) holds only `gsat` and `wsat` — setting it silently
   gives you the default.

5. **A registered tactic that runs a different tactic.** `ADD_TACTIC` strings
   are not compiled C++ — `scripts/mk_genfile_common.py` text-scans and
   Python-`eval`s them out of `.h` files, including from inside `/* */` comments.
   `src/tactic/ufbv/ufbv_rewriter_tactic.h:28` registers `"ufbv-rewriter"` with
   the body `"mk_quasi_macros_tactic(m, p)"`. Running `(apply ufbv-rewriter)`
   silently runs quasi-macros.

6. **Whole implemented techniques wired to nothing.** `src/sat/sat_bcd.cpp`
   (blocked-clause decomposition, Biere & Heule LPAR-19) is compiled and never
   instantiated — the only reference in the tree is a `friend class bcd;`
   declaration. `src/sat/smt/xor_solver.cpp` (XOR/Gaussian elimination) is not
   in its directory's `CMakeLists.txt` at all, has a stale `.d` artefact beside
   it, and `sat_config.cpp:251` hardcodes `m_xor_solver = false; // prevent
   users from playing with this option`. `sat_npn3_finder.cpp` is reachable only
   from a unit test. Induction is dead **twice over**: `smt_induction.cpp` and
   `.h` are renamed to `.disabled` and absent from `src/smt/CMakeLists.txt`, AND
   their only call site (`smt_context.cpp:1761`, `induction::should_try` /
   `get_induction()()`) sits inside an `#if 0` block (`:1760`–`:1764`) — while
   `smt.induction` remains a live, documented parameter
   (`smt_params_helper.pyg:57`) with a backing field
   (`smt_params.h:116`). Setting it does nothing at all.

7. **`src/ast/sls/` is a second solver architecture, not a heuristic.** A full
   stochastic-local-search engine with its own per-theory plugins (arith, BV,
   array, datatype, EUF, seq, basic), its own DDFW core, and a bidirectional
   phase/value/unit exchange with the CDCL(T) search
   (`sls_smt_plugin.cpp:210`–`:373`). It is off by default (`smt.sls.enable`
   False) and reachable through the `qfbv-sls` / `sls-smt` tactics, the
   `sat.euf` core, the classic core's `theory_sls`, the parallel worker pool,
   and `opt`'s `enable_sls`. Five independent integration points for a
   subsystem no default configuration runs.

8. **The default preprocessing pipeline is 20 passes long and its ordering is
   hardcoded in one function** (`src/solver/solver_preprocess.cpp:48`), with a
   live `// add: euf_completion?` TODO at `:85` — congruence-closure-modulo
   simplification is implemented and registered but not in the default path.

9. **Parallelism is a first-class subsystem that is off.** `smt.threads`
   defaults to 1, but behind it sit cube-and-conquer workers with unit sharing
   (`smt_parallel.cpp:745`, `:976`), Janota-style backbone-computation threads
   (`:109`, `parallel.num_bb_threads` default 2), and a dedicated unsat-core
   minimization thread pool (`:620`, `parallel.core_minimize` default True).

10. **Six semantically inert logic-string suffixes.** `AUFLIA+`, `AUFLIA-`,
    `AUFLIRA+`, `AUFLIRA-`, `AUFNIRA+`, `AUFNIRA-` route to exactly the same
    setup as their base logic (`smt_setup.cpp:113`–`:124`).

11. **The fixedpoint engine registry has seven engines and auto-config can pick
    two.** `src/muz/base/dl_engine_base.h:25` declares `DATALOG`, `SPACER`,
    `BMC`, `QBMC`, `TAB`, `CLP`, `DDNF`; all seven are constructed in
    `src/muz/fp/dl_register_engine.cpp:30`–`:48` and all build. Auto-config only
    ever selects `datalog` or `spacer`, and `fp.engine`'s own help text lists
    only "auto-config, datalog, bmc, spacer" — so `tab`, `clp`, `ddnf` and
    `qbmc` are live code reachable only by a parameter value the documentation
    does not mention.

12. **A transform header that was never in the build.**
    `src/muz/transforms/dl_mk_different.h` has no `.cpp` and does not appear in
    its directory's `CMakeLists.txt`. And `dl_mk_synchronize.cpp` — a 300-line
    rule-merging transform — compiles with zero `alloc()` sites anywhere.

## Dispatch: what actually runs for a given query

This is the part of Z3 an outsider most reliably gets wrong. Z3 does not have
"a solver". A `(check-sat)` from SMT-LIB goes through **two** solvers wrapped in
a chooser, and a third engine exists that almost nothing reaches by default.

### `smt_strategic_solver_factory` — the top-level entry
- **Where**: `src/tactic/portfolio/smt_strategic_solver.cpp:153` (factory), `:186` (the wrap)
- **Logics**: all, whenever a solver is created from a logic name
- **Default**: on by default
- **Does**: builds `mk_combined_solver(tactic2solver(<per-logic tactic>), <per-logic incremental solver>)` — every query gets BOTH a tactic pipeline and an incremental core.
- **Provenance**: `[C]`

### `combined_solver` — the non-incremental / incremental switch
- **Where**: `src/solver/combined_solver.cpp:46`; mode switch `:69`; unknown-fallback policy `:106`
- **Logics**: all
- **Default**: on by default
- **Does**: runs solver1 (the tactic pipeline) while the problem is non-incremental; switches permanently to solver2 (the incremental core) on the first `push`, or on an assertion after a `check-sat`, or if `ignore_solver1=true`.
- **Provenance**: `[C]`

### `combined_solver` parameters
- **Where**: `src/solver/combined_solver_params.pyg:4`
- **Does**: `solver2_timeout` (UINT_MAX) fall back to solver1 after timeout even in incremental mode; `ignore_solver1` (False) always use solver2; `solver2_unknown` (1) on solver2 `unknown`: 0 = return unknown, 1 = run solver1 if quantifier-free, 2 = always run solver1.
- **Provenance**: `[C]`

### `mk_tactic_for_logic` — logic → tactic table
- **Where**: `src/tactic/portfolio/smt_strategic_solver.cpp:100`–`:119` (tail; the table starts earlier in the function)
- **Default**: on by default
- **Does**: maps a logic string to its tactic (`LRA`→`mk_lra_tactic`, `NRA`→`mk_nra_tactic`, `LIA`→`mk_lia_tactic`, `UFBV`/`BV`→`mk_ufbv_tactic`, `QF_FP`→`mk_qffp_tactic`, `QF_FPBV`/`QF_BVFP`→`mk_qffpbv_tactic`, `HORN`→`mk_horn_tactic`, `QF_FD`/`SAT`→`mk_fd_tactic` when proofs are off, else `mk_default_tactic`). Full QF_* table is in the tactic-library section.
- **Provenance**: `[C]`

### `mk_solver_for_logic` — logic → incremental core
- **Where**: `src/tactic/portfolio/smt_strategic_solver.cpp:140`
- **Default**: on by default
- **Does**: picks solver2. `QF_FD`/`SAT` → `mk_fd_solver`; `SMTFD` → `mk_smtfd_solver`; **`QF_BV` → `mk_inc_sat_solver` whenever `rewriter.hi_div0` is true, and its default IS true (`src/params/bv_rewriter_params.pyg:8`)** — so QF_BV's incremental path is the bit-blasting SAT solver, not `src/smt/`; `tactic.default_tactic == "sat"` → `mk_inc_sat_solver`; otherwise `mk_smt2_solver`.
- **Provenance**: `[C]`

### `mk_smt2_solver` — the three-way core choice
- **Where**: `src/tactic/portfolio/smt_strategic_solver.cpp:131`
- **Default**: on by default
- **Does**: `sat.smt=true` → `mk_sat_smt_solver` (the newest core); else `sat.euf=true` → `mk_inc_sat_solver` with the EUF extension; else `mk_smt_solver` (the classic `src/smt/` core). **Both switches default False** (`src/params/sat_params.pyg:50`, `:63`), so the classic core is what almost every logic gets.
- **Provenance**: `[C]`

### `mk_special_solver_for_logic` — `QF_FD` and `SMTFD` bypass
- **Where**: `src/tactic/portfolio/smt_strategic_solver.cpp:122`
- **Default**: on by default when proofs are disabled and `parallel.enable` is false
- **Does**: routes `QF_FD`/`SAT` to the finite-domain solver and `SMTFD` to `mk_smtfd_solver`, skipping both the tactic pipeline and the SMT core.
- **Provenance**: `[C]`

### `tactic.default_tactic` — a whole-strategy override
- **Where**: `src/params/tactic_params.pyg` (`default_tactic`, SYMBOL, `''`); consumed at `src/tactic/portfolio/smt_strategic_solver.cpp:167`
- **Default**: empty, i.e. off
- **Does**: if set to a tactic s-expression, it is parsed with `parse_sexpr` and REPLACES the per-logic tactic entirely for solver1.
- **Provenance**: `[C]`

## The default preprocessing pipeline (`init_preprocess`)

Every solver built through the `then_simplifier` path installs exactly this
ordered list. Read this as "what Z3 does to your formula before any search".
Defaults come from `src/params/preprocessor_params.h` (line cited per row).

- **Where**: `src/solver/solver_preprocess.cpp:48`

| # | line | simplifier | gated by | default |
|---|------|-----------|----------|---------|
| 1 | `:62` | `rewriter_simplifier` (th_rewriter) | — | always |
| 2 | `:63` | `propagate_values` | `m_propagate_values` | **on** (`preprocessor_params.h:38`) |
| 3 | `:64` | `euf::solve_eqs` (Gaussian / variable solving) | `m_solve_eqs` | **on** (`:40`) |
| 4 | `:65` | `elim_unconstrained` | `m_elim_unconstrained` | **on** (`:39`) |
| 5 | `:66` | `cnf_nnf_simplifier` | `m_nnf_cnf` | **on** (`:44`) |
| 6 | `:67` | `eliminate_predicates` (macros/quasi-macros) | `m_macro_finder \|\| m_quasi_macros` | off/off (`:37`,`:47`) |
| 7 | `:68` | `qe_lite` simplifier | `m_qe_lite` | did not verify default |
| 8 | `:69` | `pull_nested_quantifiers` | `m_pull_nested_quantifiers` | off (`:35`) |
| 9 | `:70` | `max_bv_sharing` | `m_max_bv_sharing` | **on** (`:49`) |
| 10 | `:71` | `refine_inj_axiom` | `m_refine_inj_axiom` | **on** (`:41`) |
| 11 | `:72` | `bv::slice` (bit-vector size reduction) | `m_bv_size_reduce` | off (`smt.bv.size_reduce`, `smt_params_helper.pyg:62`) |
| 12 | `:73` | `distribute_forall` | `m_distribute_forall` | off (`:45`) |
| 13 | `:74` | `bound_simplifier` wrapped in `if_change_simplifier` with an inner `rewriter → propagate_values → solve_eqs` loop (`:50`–`:60`) | `m_bound_simplifier` | **on** (`:52`) |
| 14 | `:75` | `elim_bounds` | `m_eliminate_bounds` | off (`:42`) |
| 15 | `:76` | `bit2int` | `m_simplify_bit2int` | off (`:43`) |
| 16 | `:77` | `bv::elim_simplifier` (bit-blast quantified BV) | `m_bb_quantifiers` | did not verify default |
| 17 | `:78` | `elim_term_ite` | `m_eliminate_term_ite && lift_ite != FULL` | off (`:36`) |
| 18 | `:79` | `push_ite_simplifier` | `m_lift_ite != NONE` | NONE (`:56`) |
| 19 | `:80` | `ng_push_ite_simplifier` (non-ground) | `m_ng_lift_ite != NONE` | NONE (`:57`) |
| 20 | `:81` | `flatten_clauses` | — | always |

**Not installed, and the source says so.** `src/solver/solver_preprocess.cpp:83`–`:88`
carries a live TODO: `// add: euf_completion?` and `// add: make it externally
programmable`. `euf-completion` is a registered simplifier (congruence-closure
modulo simplification) that the default pipeline does not run. `[C]`

## Logic classification table (`smt_logics.cpp`)

Z3 classifies a logic by **substring search on its name**, not by a table of
known logics. This is why unusual names still get sensible theories, and why a
few classifications look wrong.

- **Where**: `src/solver/smt_logics.cpp`

| predicate | line | accepts |
|---|---|---|
| `supported_logic` | `:24` | union of all the below |
| `logic_has_reals_only` | `:31` | name contains `LRA`, `NRA`, or `RDL` |
| `logic_has_arith` | `:40` | contains `LRA`/`LIRA`/`LIA`/`NRA`/`NIRA`/`NIA`/`IDL`/`RDL`; or `QF_BVRE`, `QF_S`, `QF_FD`, `HORN`; or is FPA; or is `ALL` |
| `logic_has_bv` | `:60` | contains `BV`; or `FP`, `QF_FD`, `SMTFD`, `HORN`; or `ALL` |
| `logic_has_finite_sets` | `:71` | contains `FS`; or `ALL` |
| `logic_has_array` | `:78` | starts with `QF_A` or `A`; or `SMTFD`, `HORN`; or `ALL` |
| `logic_has_seq` | `:88` | `QF_BVRE`, or has_str |
| `logic_has_str` | `:92` | `QF_S`, `QF_SLIA`, `QF_SNIA`; or `ALL` |
| `logic_has_fpa` | `:100` | contains `FP`; or `ALL` |
| `logic_has_uf` | `:106` | contains `UF`; or `SMTFD` |
| `logic_has_horn` | `:113` | exactly `HORN` |
| `logic_has_pb` | `:117` | `QF_FD`, `ALL`, or `HORN` |
| `logic_has_datatype` | `:121` | contains `DT`; or `QF_FD`, `ALL`, `HORN` |

Two observations that are visible in the source, not inferred: `logic_has_arith`
lists `LRA` twice (`:43` and `:46`), as does `logic_has_reals_only` (`:34`,
`:35`) — harmless, but it means these were hand-edited rather than generated.
And `logic_has_bv` is true for `FP`, so a pure floating-point logic is
classified as a bit-vector logic — consistent with FPA being bit-blasted. `[C]`

# Part 2 — `src/smt/`: the classic SMT core


Repo: `/home/mjbommar/projects/personal/axeyum/references/z3`, HEAD `e18d63b`. Read-only
inventory; nothing built. Every `Where` line was obtained with `grep -n` / `sed -n` against
this checkout.

## Logic -> configuration map

Source: `src/smt/smt_setup.cpp`. `setup::setup_default()` (`src/smt/smt_setup.cpp:76`)
dispatches on the SMT-LIB `m_logic` string to one `setup_<LOGIC>()` member; `setup_auto_config()`
(`src/smt/smt_setup.cpp:143`) is the `(auto_config true)` path that additionally collects
`static_features` over the asserted formulas before choosing a configuration (skipped for
`QF_BV`/`QF_AUFBV`/`QF_ABV`/`QF_UFBV` — comment at `smt_setup.cpp:147` says these logics "do not
use them"). `setup::setup_unknown(static_features&)` (`smt_setup.cpp:841`) is the
no-recognized-logic fallback: it pattern-matches on counted feature classes (num theories,
diff-logic shape, has_bv, has_fpa, has_arrays, quantifier count) to pick the same per-logic
functions reflectively.

| `setup_*` function | `smt_setup.cpp:line` | Theory plugin(s) registered | Notable param changes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `setup_QF_UF` | 219 | (params only, no explicit register here — UF is the base congruence closure, always present) | `m_params.setup_QF_UF()` (delegates to `smt_params`) |
| `setup_QF_DT` | 223 | `theory_datatype` (`setup_datatypes`, 735), `theory_recfun` (`setup_recfuns`, 740) | on top of QF_UF |
| `setup_QF_BVRE` | 229 | BV (`setup_bv`) + LIA (`setup_lra_arith`) + `theory_seq`/`theory_char` (`setup_seq`, 783) | combines QF_BV, QF_LIA, and seq setup — regex-over-BV logic |
| `setup_QF_RDL` | 243 (no static_features) / 267 (with st) | `theory_mi_arith` (proofs on) else `theory_mi_arith`/`theory_rdl`/`theory_frdl` depending on density + rationals (see `smt_setup.cpp:291-316`) | `m_relevancy_lvl=0`, `arith_eq2ineq=true`, `arith_reflect=false`, restart/phase tuned by `st.is_dense()` |
| `setup_QF_IDL` | 319 / 325 | `theory_mi_arith` (proofs) else `theory_dense_si`/`theory_dense_i` (dense) else `theory_i_arith` | relevancy=0, `arith_small_lemma_size=30`, phase selection `PS_CACHING`/`PS_CACHING_CONSERVATIVE2` by CNF density, `random_initial_activity=IA_RANDOM` for pure-conjunction inputs |
| `setup_QF_UFIDL` | 383 / 389 | `theory_mi_arith` (proofs) else `theory_dense_si`/`theory_dense_i` (dense, no UF) else `theory_i_arith` | `arith_eq_bounds=true`, `restart_strategy=RS_GEOMETRIC`, `restart_factor=1.5` |
| `setup_QF_LRA` | 428 / 434 | `theory_lra` (default) or `theory_mi_arith` if `arith_mode==AS_OLD_ARITH` (`setup_lra_arith`, 601) | — |
| `setup_QF_LIA` | 444 / 450 | same as QF_LRA via `setup_lra_arith` | — |
| `setup_QF_LIRA` | 440 | `setup_mi_arith()` (608) — `theory_inf_arith`/`theory_lra`/`theory_mi_arith` by `arith_mode` | mixed int/real linear arith |
| `setup_QF_UFLIA` | 457 / 462 | `setup_lra_arith` + (if `st.m_has_bv`) `setup_bv()` | throws if benchmark has reals |
| `setup_QF_UFLRA` | 470 | `setup_lra_arith` | — |
| `setup_QF_BV` | 475 | `setup_bv()` -> `theory_bv` (`BS_BLASTER`) or `theory_dummy` (`BS_NO_BV`) or `theory_intblast` (`m_bv_solver==2`, plus `setup_lra_arith`) | bit-blast is the classic path |
| `setup_QF_AUFBV` | 481 | `setup_bv()` + `setup_arrays()` | — |
| `QF_ABV`, `QF_UFBV` | routed to `setup_QF_AUFBV` at `smt_setup.cpp:102,104` | same as above | identical config, distinct logic string |
| `setup_QF_AX` | 487 / 493 | `setup_arrays()` -> `theory_array`/`theory_array_full`/`theory_dummy` per `m_array_mode` (718) | `AR_MODEL_BASED` throws `default_exception` — deprecated (727) |
| `setup_QF_AUFLIA` | 498 / 505 | `setup_i_arith()` + `setup_arrays()` | `setup_i_arith` (592) picks `theory_i_arith` if `AS_OLD_ARITH` else `setup_lra_arith` |
| `setup_AUFLIA` | 511 / 519 | `setup_i_arith()` + `setup_arrays()` | throws if benchmark has reals (from the `static_features` overload) |
| `AUFLIA+/-`, `AUFLIRA+/-`, `AUFNIRA+/-` | routed at `smt_setup.cpp:113-124` | aliases to `setup_AUFLIA`/`setup_AUFLIRA` | the `+`/`-` suffixes are treated identically to the base logic — no differentiation in code |
| `setup_AUFLIRA` | 526 | `setup_mi_arith()` + `setup_arrays()` | — |
| `setup_UFNIA` | 533 | delegates to `setup_AUFLIA()` | — |
| `setup_UFLRA` | 537 | delegates to `setup_AUFLIRA()` | — |
| `setup_AUFNIRA` | 545 | delegates to `setup_AUFLIRA()` | nonlinear real/int arith gets no distinct config from AUFLIRA at this layer |
| `setup_LRA` | 549 | `setup_mi_arith()` | — |
| `setup_QF_FP` | 554 | `setup_QF_BV()` + `theory_fpa` | FP is bit-blasted through BV plus a dedicated FPA theory |
| `setup_QF_FPBV` | 559 | identical body to `setup_QF_FP` (both call `setup_QF_BV()` + register `theory_fpa`) | `QF_BVFP` aliases here too (`smt_setup.cpp:133`) |
| `setup_QF_S` / `QF_SLIA` | 564 | dispatch on `smt.string_solver` param: `"seq"`/`"auto"` -> `setup_unknown()`; `"char"` -> `setup_QF_BV()`+`setup_char()`; `"empty"` -> `setup_seq()`; `"none"` -> nothing registered | `"seq"` and `"auto"` route through the generic feature-driven `setup_unknown()`, not a seq-specific path, at this call site |
| `setup_unknown()` (no `st`) | 805 | `setup_arith()` + `setup_arrays()` + `setup_bv()` + `setup_datatypes()` + `setup_recfuns()` + `setup_dl()` + `setup_seq_str(st)` + `setup_fpa()` (registers BOTH bv and fpa again — `setup_fpa` at 778 also calls `setup_bv()`) + `setup_finite_set()` + `setup_special_relations()` + `setup_polymorphism()` + `setup_relevancy(st)` | the "install everything" fallback for CFG_BASIC / unrecognized logic |
| `setup_unknown(static_features&)` | 841 | reflective dispatch to the specific `setup_*` above based on counted feature classes | quantifiers present -> `setup_AUFLIRA`/`setup_AUFLIA` + datatypes/bv/dl/seq/finite_set/fpa/recfun/special_relations/polymorphism (843-857) |
| `setup_card()` (always run, `setup.cpp:72`) | 769 | `theory_pb` | registered unconditionally for EVERY config mode, not logic-gated |
| `setup_sls()` (always run, `setup.cpp:73`) | 773 | `theory_sls`, only if `m_params.m_sls_enable` | off by default (see Theory Solvers section) |

## Theory solvers, quantifier machinery, and core search entries

The exhaustive per-technique entries for theory solvers (`theory_*.cpp/h`) and the core CDCL(T)
search loop (`smt_context.cpp`, conflict resolution, case-split queues, congruence closure,
watch lists, justifications) were produced by two parallel research passes over this same
checkout and are appended below verbatim (each entry independently carries its own
`Where`/`Logics`/`Default`/`Does`/`Provenance`, gathered the same way as this section).

### Simplex core (theory_arith<Ext>)
- **Where**: `src/smt/theory_arith_core.h:1` (module header), tableau/variable-kind doc `src/smt/theory_arith.h:59`, pivot at `src/smt/theory_arith_core.h:1955` (`theory_arith<Ext>::pivot`)
- **Logics**: reached only when `arith.solver=2` (see `src/params/smt_params_helper.pyg:65`); registered via `smt_setup.cpp:292,304,360,378,405,420,594,603,617,637,681,683,690` for the QF_LRA/QF_LIA/QF_UFLIA/etc "old arith" path
- **Default**: behind param `arith.solver=2` (default is 6 = `theory_lra`)
- **Does**: simplex tableau over base/quasi-base/non-base variables with lazy pivoting and eager/lazy bound propagation
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Bland's rule / anti-cycling
- **Where**: `src/smt/theory_arith_core.h:2119` (`select_blands_pivot_core`), dispatch at `theory_arith_core.h:2216` (`select_pivot`), flag `theory_arith_core.h:1652` (`m_blands_rule`)
- **Logics**: same as Simplex core (theory_arith family, `arith.solver=2`)
- **Default**: `on by default` within that engine (activated automatically to break cycling; the engine itself is behind `arith.solver=2`)
- **Does**: switches pivot-selection strategy to Bland's least-index rule to guarantee simplex termination when normal heuristic pivoting cycles
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Branch-and-bound for integers (theory_arith)
- **Where**: `src/smt/theory_arith_int.h:206` (`branch_infeasible_int_var`), driver at `theory_arith_int.h:956,1073-1097`
- **Logics**: theory_arith family (`arith.solver=2`), integer variables
- **Default**: behind param `arith.solver=2`
- **Does**: creates a case-split (branch) lemma `x <= floor` / `x >= ceil` on a fractional-valued integer tableau variable
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Gomory cuts (theory_arith)
- **Where**: `src/smt/theory_arith_int.h:439` (`is_gomory_cut_target`), `theory_arith_int.h:500` (`mk_gomory_cut`), ratio-gated call at `theory_arith_int.h:1076` (`m_arith_branch_cut_ratio`)
- **Logics**: theory_arith family (`arith.solver=2`), integer arithmetic
- **Default**: behind param `arith.solver=2`; internally gated further by `arith_branch_cut_ratio`
- **Does**: derives a valid cutting-plane inequality from an infeasible integer tableau row to strengthen the LP relaxation before branching
- **Provenance**: [C]

### GCD test for integer rows
- **Where**: `src/smt/theory_arith_core.h:1955` (`pivot<...>(..., apply_gcd_test)`); stats counters `m_gcd_tests`/`m_gcd_conflicts` declared `src/smt/theory_arith.h:44`
- **Logics**: theory_arith family (`arith.solver=2`)
- **Default**: behind param `arith.solver=2`
- **Does**: rejects integer rows whose coefficients admit no integer solution via a GCD divisibility check, pruning infeasible branches early
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Nonlinear monomial bound propagation (interval arithmetic)
- **Where**: `src/smt/theory_arith_nl.h:447` (`propagate_nl_upward`), `:475` (`propagate_nl_downward`), `:532` (`propagate_nl_bounds`)
- **Logics**: theory_arith family (`arith.solver=2`), nonlinear terms
- **Default**: behind param `arith.solver=2` and `arith.nl` (doc at `smt_params_helper.pyg:66`: "relevant only if smt.arith.solver=2")
- **Does**: propagates interval bounds up/down through multiplication monomials using `old_interval` arithmetic
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Cross-nested-form consistency check
- **Where**: `src/smt/theory_arith_nl.h:1437` (`cross_nested`), `:1528` (`is_cross_nested_consistent`)
- **Logics**: theory_arith family (`arith.solver=2`), nonlinear terms
- **Default**: behind param `arith.solver=2`
- **Does**: rewrites a nonlinear row into a nested nonlinear expression and interval-evaluates it for a tighter consistency check than pure interval propagation
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Gröbner basis nonlinear propagation
- **Where**: `src/smt/theory_arith_nl.h:1669` (`init_grobner_var_order`), `:1743` (`add_row_to_gb`), uses `math/grobner/grobner.h` included at `src/smt/theory_arith.h:38`
- **Logics**: theory_arith family (`arith.solver=2`), nonlinear terms
- **Default**: behind param `arith.solver=2` + `arith.nl`
- **Does**: builds and simplifies a Gröbner basis of the nonlinear cluster to derive new equalities/bounds or detect a polynomial-level conflict
- **Provenance**: [P] (Gröbner-basis polynomial completion; checked in code, algorithm identity itself not independently verified against a paper)

### Offset-equality propagation (theory_arith)
- **Where**: `src/smt/theory_arith_eq.h:101` (`is_offset_row`), `:215` (`propagate_cheap_eq`)
- **Logics**: theory_arith family (`arith.solver=2`)
- **Default**: behind param `arith.solver=2`
- **Does**: detects tableau rows of the shape `x - y = k` and propagates equalities to the core when two such rows chain to the same offset
- **Provenance**: [C]

### theory_arith_inv.h / theory_arith_pp.h
- **Where**: `src/smt/theory_arith_inv.h:1` (module), `src/smt/theory_arith_pp.h:27` (`collect_statistics`), `:58` (`display`)
- **Logics**: n/a (debug/instrumentation, not a decision procedure)
- **Default**: n/a
- **Does**: invariant-checking (`theory_arith_inv.h`) and pretty-printing/statistics (`theory_arith_pp.h`) helpers for theory_arith — not separate algorithms
- **Provenance**: [C]

### theory_lra (LP/Simplex via math/lp)
- **Where**: `src/smt/theory_lra.h:1`, delegate construction `src/smt/theory_lra.cpp:936` (`m_lia = alloc(lp::int_solver, ...)`), `theory_lra.cpp:3632` (`make_feasible`)
- **Logics**: default arithmetic engine for essentially all logics with arithmetic (`arith.solver` default 6, `smt_params_helper.pyg:65`); explicit registration `smt_setup.cpp:605,681(setup_lra_arith path),690`
- **Default**: `on by default`
- **Does**: thin `theory` plugin wrapping `math/lp`'s dual-simplex LP core (`lar_solver`) for LRA/LIA
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Integer branch/cut dispatch in theory_lra
- **Where**: `src/smt/theory_lra.cpp:2020` (`check_lia`), branch case `theory_lra.cpp:2034`, cut case `theory_lra.cpp:2056`
- **Logics**: same as theory_lra (default arithmetic engine)
- **Default**: `on by default`
- **Does**: consumes `lp::lia_move` results (branch / cut / conflict) from `math/lp::int_solver` and turns them into SMT case-splits or lemmas
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Nonlinear arithmetic dispatch in theory_lra (nla_solver)
- **Where**: `src/smt/theory_lra.cpp:2182` (`check_nla`), `:2166` (`check_nla_continue`), include `src/smt/theory_lra.cpp:24` (`math/lp/nla_solver.h`)
- **Logics**: default arithmetic engine, nonlinear terms
- **Default**: `on by default` for nonlinear terms when `arith.nl.nra=true` (doc: "relevant only if smt.arith.solver=6", `smt_params_helper.pyg:67`)
- **Does**: hands nonlinear constraints to `math/lp`'s incremental-linearization NLA solver, falling back to an `nra_solver` call when linearization stalls
- **Provenance**: [C] (dispatch site only; internals live in `math/lp`, outside my scope)

### Difference-logic Bellman–Ford propagation (theory_diff_logic<Ext>)
- **Where**: `src/smt/theory_diff_logic.h:1` (module), `theory_diff_logic_def.h:371` (`final_check_eh`), `:567` (`propagate_core`)
- **Logics**: reached at `arith.solver=1` (bellman-ford) or `=3` (floyd-warshall) per `smt_params_helper.pyg:65`; registered `smt_setup.cpp:312,314,643,645,649,651`
- **Default**: behind param `arith.solver=1|3` (non-default vs. `theory_lra`)
- **Does**: shortest-path/negative-cycle-based decision procedure for IDL/RDL over a difference-constraint graph (graph structure itself lives in `smt/diff_logic.h`, outside my file scope)
- **Provenance**: [C] for dispatch; graph algorithm body not directly read (out of scope file)

### Difference-logic optimization via simplex
- **Where**: `src/smt/theory_diff_logic.h:66` (`typedef simplex::simplex<simplex::mpq_ext> Simplex`), conversion helpers `theory_diff_logic.h:369-375` (`node2simplex`, `update_simplex`)
- **Logics**: same registration as above (IDL/RDL, `arith.solver=1|3`)
- **Default**: behind param `arith.solver=1|3`
- **Does**: builds an auxiliary `math/simplex` instance from the DL graph to compute MAX-SMT-style objective optimization for difference logic
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Dense difference logic (theory_dense_diff_logic<Ext>)
- **Where**: `src/smt/theory_dense_diff_logic.h:1`, typedefs `theory_dense_diff_logic.h:291-294`
- **Logics**: `arith.solver=... AS_DENSE_DIFF_LOGIC` branch; registered `smt_setup.cpp:366,368,658,660,664,666`
- **Default**: behind param arith mode `AS_DENSE_DIFF_LOGIC` (non-default)
- **Does**: adjacency-matrix (dense) variant of the difference-logic decision procedure, chosen for small dense DL problems instead of the sparse-graph `theory_diff_logic`
- **Provenance**: [C]

### UTVPI theory (theory_utvpi<Ext>)
- **Where**: `src/smt/theory_utvpi.h:11` ("use Bellman Ford traversal algorithm for UTVPI"), typedefs `theory_utvpi.h:358-359`, propagate loop `theory_utvpi_def.h:496`
- **Logics**: `arith.solver=4` (AS_UTVPI); registered `smt_setup.cpp:672,674`
- **Default**: behind param `arith.solver=4` (non-default)
- **Does**: encodes `±x ± y <= k` constraints via the `x⁺/x⁻` splitting trick (doc `theory_utvpi_def.h:17-24`) and reuses Bellman-Ford-style propagation from the diff-logic base
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Array read-over-write axiom
- **Where**: `src/smt/theory_array_base.cpp:143` (doc comment on select-store axiom), `theory_array_base.h:1`
- **Logics**: any logic with arrays (QF_A*, AUFLIA, etc.); registered `smt_setup.cpp:724` (`theory_array`) / `:730` (`theory_array_full`)
- **Default**: `on by default` when arrays occur (`array.mode` default not independently verified — "did not verify")
- **Does**: lazily instantiates `i≠j => select(store(a,i,v),j) = select(a,j)` (and the `i=j` case) on demand
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Array extensionality
- **Where**: `src/smt/theory_array_base.cpp:331` (`assert_extensionality`), `:358` (`assert_extensionality_core`)
- **Logics**: same as above (array theory)
- **Default**: `on by default` when two array-sorted terms need disequality justification
- **Does**: introduces a fresh witness index `k` and asserts `a=b <=> select(a,k)=select(b,k)` to justify/refute array disequalities
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Array weak-equivalence / select-to-store propagation
- **Where**: `src/smt/theory_array_base.cpp:917` (`propagate_select_to_store_parents`), `:953` (`propagate_selects_to_store_parents`), weak-var trail `theory_array_base.h:29-33`
- **Logics**: array theory
- **Default**: `on by default`
- **Does**: pushes `select` congruence information up through `store` parents in the union-find without fully instantiating axioms, reducing axiom blow-up
- **Provenance**: [C]

### theory_array vs theory_array_full
- **Where**: `src/smt/theory_array.h:1`, `src/smt/theory_array_full.h:24` (adds maps/consts/as-array/lambdas)
- **Logics**: `theory_array` for `array.mode=AR_SIMPLE`, `theory_array_full` for `AR_FULL`; both registered `smt_setup.cpp:724,730`
- **Default**: `did not verify` which `array.mode` value is the global default; `theory_array_full` is the superset used when `map`/`const`/`as-array`/lambda array terms appear
- **Does**: `theory_array_full` extends the base select/store axiomatization with `map`, constant-array, `as-array`, and array-lambda handling
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Bit-blasting (theory_bv)
- **Where**: `src/smt/theory_bv.h:22` (`#include ".../bit_blaster.h"`), `src/smt/theory_bv.cpp:48` (`mk_bits`), `:567` (`internalize_num`)
- **Logics**: QF_BV/BV in the classic (non-`sat.smt`) core; registered `smt_setup.cpp:713`, guarded by `bv.solver` switch at `smt_setup.cpp:699-714`
- **Default**: `on by default` (`bv.solver=0`, doc `smt_params_helper.pyg:63`: "0 - bit-blasting")
- **Does**: eagerly expands each bit-vector term into its per-bit Boolean circuit and reuses the SAT engine's propagation/congruence over individual bits
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Bit-level propagation / equivalence-class bit merge
- **Where**: `src/smt/theory_bv.cpp:1283` (`propagate_bits`), fixed-eq justification `theory_bv.cpp:367`
- **Logics**: same as theory_bv
- **Default**: `on by default`
- **Does**: after bit-blasting, propagates a fixed bit value across an enode's equivalence class and detects bit-level conflicts before falling through to the SAT engine
- **Provenance**: [C]

### theory_intblast (integer-blasting BV alternative)
- **Where**: `src/smt/theory_intblast.h:8` ("Intblast version of bit-vector solver"), registration `src/smt/smt_setup.cpp:701-703`
- **Logics**: `bv.solver=2`; also forces `setup_lra_arith()` (`smt_setup.cpp:702`)
- **Default**: behind param `bv.solver=2` (default is 0 = bit-blasting)
- **Does**: translates bit-vector constraints into integer arithmetic (`bv2int_translator`) and hands them to the arithmetic theory instead of blasting to bits; falls back to an SLS engine (`ast/sls/sat_ddfw.h`, included `theory_intblast.h:24`) per `final_check_eh` (`theory_intblast.cpp:39`)
- **Provenance**: [C]

### theory_char
- **Where**: `src/smt/theory_char.h:14` ("Solver for characters"), stats `theory_char.h:28-33` (`m_num_ackerman`, `m_num_bounds`, `m_num_blast`)
- **Logics**: any logic using `Char`/strings; registered unconditionally from `setup_seq` at `smt_setup.cpp:785,789`
- **Default**: `on by default` whenever the seq/string solver is active (default `string_solver=seq`, `smt_params_helper.pyg:141`)
- **Does**: decides character equalities/orderings by a mix of Ackermannization, bound propagation, and (as last resort) bit-blasting the character code
- **Provenance**: [C]

### theory_datatype + occurs-check
- **Where**: `src/smt/theory_datatype.h:1`, `theory_datatype.cpp:754` (`final_check_eh`), `:869` (`occurs_check_enter`), `:1009` (`occurs_check`)
- **Logics**: any logic with algebraic datatypes; registered `smt_setup.cpp:737` and via `setup_unknown`/general path (`smt_setup.cpp:814,848`)
- **Default**: `on by default`
- **Does**: decides constructor/selector/recognizer congruences and performs a DFS occurs-check over recursive datatype terms to reject infinite (cyclic) values
- **Provenance**: [C]

### theory_dl (finite-domain DL)
- **Where**: `src/smt/theory_dl.h:9` ("Basic theory solver for DL finite domains"), factory `mk_theory_dl`, registered `smt_setup.cpp:747` (`setup_dl`)
- **Logics**: `did not verify` which logic string routes here beyond the general/unknown path (`smt_setup.cpp:816,850`); header only exposes a factory function, no class body in this file
- **Default**: `on by default` in the general/unknown-logic setup path
- **Does**: decision procedure for finite-domain "DL" (datalog-style enumerated) sorts — body lives in `theory_dl.cpp`, not further inspected in depth
- **Provenance**: [C] for registration; [I] for behavior (only class-doc + registration read)

### theory_dummy ("no theory" placeholder)
- **Where**: `src/smt/theory_dummy.h:24` ("Do nothing theory... returns FC_GIVEUP if theory expressions were internalized"), used at `smt_setup.cpp:637,710,721`
- **Logics**: fallback when a theory is explicitly disabled (`arith.solver=AS_NO_ARITH`, `bv.solver=BS_NO_BV`, `array.mode=AR_NO_ARRAY`)
- **Default**: behind explicit disable params; not the default engine
- **Does**: accepts theory terms without deciding them, forcing `unknown` (`FC_GIVEUP`) if any were actually used
- **Provenance**: [C]

### theory_finite_set (axiom saturation)
- **Where**: `src/smt/theory_finite_set.h:12-16` (class doc: "relies on instantiating axiom schemas... saturating constraints"), callback wiring `theory_finite_set.cpp:34-38`
- **Logics**: any logic using the `FiniteSet` sort; registered `smt_setup.cpp:793` and general path `smt_setup.cpp:819,852`
- **Default**: `on by default` in the general setup path
- **Does**: saturates congruence-closure-driven axiom instances for `set.union/intersect/filter/map/in/empty` down to arithmetic + UF
- **Provenance**: [C]

### theory_finite_set_size (cardinality sub-solver)
- **Where**: `src/smt/theory_finite_set_size.h:9` ("sub-solver for cardinality constraints of finite sets"), field `theory_finite_set.h:139` (`m_cardinality_solver`)
- **Logics**: `FiniteSet` sort with cardinality (`set.card`) constraints
- **Default**: `on by default` whenever `theory_finite_set` is active (embedded, not separately registered)
- **Does**: separate reasoning module inside `theory_finite_set` specifically for `|S|` cardinality equalities/disequalities
- **Provenance**: [C]

### theory_fpa (IEEE 754 floating point)
- **Where**: `src/smt/theory_fpa.h:9` ("Floating-Point Theory Plugin"), registered `smt_setup.cpp:556,561,780`
- **Logics**: QF_FP/FP and any logic using floats
- **Default**: `on by default` for FP sorts
- **Does**: bit-blasts FP operations to bit-vectors via `fpa2bv_converter`/`fpa2bv_rewriter` and manages a dedicated FP model-value procedure
- **Provenance**: [C]

### theory_opt (interface, not a solver)
- **Where**: `src/smt/theory_opt.h:9-11` ("Interface utilities used by optimization providing theory solvers")

- **Default**: default bool value not verified; call sites gate on `m_scope_lvl == m_base_lvl`
- **Does**: strips literals falsified at the base level out of stored clauses and marks satisfied clauses for removal, shrinking clause storage between search rounds
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Congruence-closure merge (`add_eq`)
- **Where**: `src/smt/smt_context.cpp:481`
- **Logics**: all (any theory using enodes/E-graph — effectively all except pure SAT)
- **Default**: on by default
- **Does**: union-find style merge of two equivalence classes, choosing the new root by class size (union-by-size) with an invariant that an interpreted-value root always wins, then propagates relevancy and re-inserts affected parents into the congruence table
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Congruence table (`cg_table`) — specialized unary/binary/commutative/n-ary hash tables
- **Where**: `src/smt/smt_cg_table.h:34` (class), `:35` unary, `:53` binary, `:73` commutative (`cg_comm_hash`/`cg_comm_eq`, order-independent hash + swap-aware equality), `:108` general n-ary
- **Logics**: all
- **Default**: on by default
- **Does**: finds/creates the "same function, congruent-root arguments" bucket for a new term in O(1) amortized, with a dedicated fast path for arity-1/2 and for commutative binary operators
- **Provenance**: [C]

### `almost_cg_table` / extended-disequality propagation (`is_ext_diseq`)
- **Where**: header explanation `src/smt/smt_almost_cg_table.h:28`, caller `src/smt/smt_context.cpp:1335-1345`
- **Logics**: caller theory not verified (likely array/datatype territory); table lives in `smt_context.h:189`
- **Default**: appears to be an on-demand utility, not a background pass (not verified further)
- **Does**: checks whether two parent terms would become congruent "if" two given roots were merged (congruent everywhere except at the pair being tested), used to justify a derived disequality
- **Provenance**: [C], shallow

### Interface equalities / theory combination via case split (`assume_eq`)
- **Where**: `theory::assume_eq`/`mk_preferred_eq` in `src/smt/smt_theory.cpp:127,142`; core logic `context::assume_eq` at `src/smt/smt_context.cpp:4695`
- **Logics**: all (Nelson-Oppen-style combination between any two theories sharing a sort, e.g. arithmetic+arrays, arithmetic+UF)
- **Default**: on by default
- **Does**: when two theories disagree over whether two shared terms are equal, creates an explicit "interface equality" atom, marks it `try_true_first`, and lets the case-split queue decide it like any other literal — this is how Z3 does theory combination for non-convex theories
- **Provenance**: [C], recognized as the standard delayed/interface-equality Nelson-Oppen-style combination technique; no specific paper citation checked

### Dynamic ackermannization (core-loop wiring)
- **Where**: call site `src/smt/smt_context.cpp:4146` (`m_dyn_ack_manager.propagate_eh()`, inside `bounded_search`), manager declared `src/smt/smt_context.h:238`
- **Logics**: all (heuristic, function-symbol agnostic)
- **Default**: manager always constructed; whether it does anything depends on its own params (default on/off not verified)
- **Does**: heuristically instantiates the Ackermann axiom `a=b -> f(a)=f(b)` for congruent-looking function applications the congruence closure hasn't yet been told are equal, cutting down redundant case splits over UF equalities
- **Provenance**: [C], shallow — only the core-loop call site read, not `dyn_ack.cpp` internals

### Justification kinds — Boolean propagation (`b_justification`)
- **Where**: `src/smt/smt_b_justification.h:23` (kinds `CLAUSE`, `BIN_CLAUSE`, `AXIOM`, `JUSTIFICATION`)
- **Logics**: all
- **Default**: on by default
- **Does**: tagged-pointer encoding that avoids allocating a full justification object for the two hottest propagation reasons (ordinary clause / binary clause), falling back to a general `justification*` only when needed
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Justification kinds — equality propagation (`eq_justification`)
- **Where**: `src/smt/smt_eq_justification.h:22` (kinds `AXIOM`, `CONGRUENCE`, `EQUATION`, `JUSTIFICATION`)
- **Logics**: all
- **Default**: on by default
- **Does**: same tagged-pointer trick specialized to equality propagation reasons (asserted equation vs. congruence-closure-derived)
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Clause representation with inline activity/lemma bit
- **Where**: `src/smt/smt_clause.h:55` (class `clause`), `:48` (`is_lemma` free function), `:87-97` (`get_activity_addr`)
- **Logics**: all
- **Default**: on by default
- **Does**: a single variable-length allocation storing literal array + an activity counter only for learned clauses (`CLS_LEARNED`/`CLS_TH_LEMMA`), avoiding the field for input clauses
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Clausal proof trail (`clause_proof`)
- **Where**: header rationale `src/smt/smt_clause_proof.h:9-16`, class `:39`
- **Logics**: all (infrastructure, logic-agnostic)
- **Default**: whether logging is compiled/enabled by default vs. only under proof-production mode was not verified
- **Does**: records a trail of added/removed clauses (input, theory lemmas, axioms, resolution-derived lemmas) intended as a trusted-checking / replay / interpolation interface, separate from the DRAT-style resolution proof
- **Provenance**: [C]

### `checker` — quick syntactic sat/unsat evaluator
- **Where**: `src/smt/smt_checker.h:39` (class), used by the quantifier instantiation queue at `src/smt/qi_queue.cpp:167,218`
- **Logics**: all (used wherever quantifier instantiation happens)
- **Default**: on by default
- **Does**: evaluates whether a formula (typically a candidate quantifier instantiation) is already forced true/false by the current partial boolean+enode assignment, without going through full internalization — a cheap pre-filter

### Fingerprint dedup table
- **Where**: `src/smt/fingerprints.h:26` (`fingerprint`), `:47` (`fingerprint_set`, `insert`/`contains`)
- **Logics**: quantified logics only
- **Default**: on by default
- **Does**: keyed by (quantifier, argument enode roots) to avoid re-instantiating the same quantifier with the same (up to congruence) binding twice
- **Provenance**: [C], shallow

### `farkas_util` — Farkas-certificate lemma combination
- **Where**: `src/smt/smt_farkas_util.h:19` (module comment: "specialized to proofs generated by the arithmetic solvers"), class `:28`
- **Logics**: linear-arithmetic-bearing logics (QF_LRA/LIA/UFLRA/etc.) — exact caller set not verified
- **Default**: on by default within arithmetic theory lemma generation (not itself a param)
- **Does**: combines a set of asserted/derived inequalities with rational coefficients (a Farkas combination) into one contradiction/consequence, used to build small arithmetic conflict/propagation lemmas
- **Provenance**: [C], shallow — didn't trace call sites in `theory_arith`/`theory_lra`

### `arith_value` / `implied_equalities` — model/value extraction utilities
- **Where**: `src/smt/smt_arith_value.h:9` ("Utility to extract arithmetic values from context"), `src/smt/smt_implied_equalities.h:9` ("Procedure for obtaining implied equalities relative to the state of a solver")
- **Logics**: arithmetic logics for `arith_value`; all logics with a congruence/theory backend for `implied_equalities`
- **Default**: on-demand utilities, not part of the main loop
- **Does**: `arith_value` reads a concrete rational value for a term out of the current theory state; `implied_equalities` computes which asserted/derivable terms are forced equal — both consumer-facing query APIs
- **Provenance**: [C], shallow

## Quantifier machinery

### Matching Abstract Machine (MAM) — compiled code-tree interpreter
- **Where**: `src/smt/mam.cpp:1859` (`class interpreter`), opcode enum at `src/smt/mam.cpp:112-119`
- **Logics**: quantified logics reached via `smt_quantifier.cpp`/`qi_queue.cpp` — AUFLIA, AUFLIRA, AUFNIRA, UFNIA, UFLRA, LRA, and any logic where a formula happens to contain a quantifier (`did not verify` whether QF_* logics can still hit this through an inline `exists`/`forall`)
- **Default**: on by default — `mk_mam` (`src/smt/mam.cpp:4071`) is the only constructor (`src/smt/mam.h:71`); there is no alternate/naive matcher left in this tree (`grep -n "class mam"` finds only `mam_impl`)
- **Does**: interprets a compiled "code tree" of matching instructions against the E-graph to find pattern-quantifier instantiation candidates (E-matching), instead of walking patterns naively each round
- **Provenance**: [C] — module header at `src/smt/mam.h:6-10` names it "Matching Abstract Machine"; the WAM-like code-tree design is a published de Moura/Bjørner technique but I did not cross-check a specific citation, so treat the paper attribution as [I] only

### MAM opcode family: INIT1..INITN (term-head initialization)
- **Where**: `src/smt/mam.cpp:113` (`INIT1=0 ... INITN`), `struct initn` at `src/smt/mam.cpp:133`
- **Logics**: same as MAM entry above
- **Default**: on by default (compiled unconditionally into every code tree)
- **Does**: seeds a matching register from the root pattern application (fixed arity INIT1-6, `INITN` for n-ary associative ops like `+`/`*`)
- **Provenance**: [C]

### MAM opcode family: BIND1..BINDN / `struct bind` (child-position binding)
- **Where**: `src/smt/mam.cpp:114`, `struct bind` at `src/smt/mam.cpp:213`
- **Logics**: same as MAM entry above
- **Default**: on by default
- **Does**: descends into an argument of the currently-bound enode and binds a new register to it
- **Provenance**: [C]

### MAM opcode family: YIELD1..YIELDN / `struct yield` (successful match emission)
- **Where**: `src/smt/mam.cpp:115`, `struct yield` at `src/smt/mam.cpp:228`
- **Logics**: same as MAM entry above
- **Default**: on by default
- **Does**: emits a completed variable binding (quantifier + pattern + bound register values) to the instantiation queue once a code-tree path fully matches
- **Provenance**: [C]

### MAM instructions: COMPARE / CHECK / FILTER / CFILTER / PFILTER / CHOOSE / NOOP / CONTINUE
- **Where**: `src/smt/mam.cpp:116-117`; `struct compare:141`, `struct check:146`, `struct filter:151`, `struct pcheck:156`, `struct choose:169`, `struct cont:213`
- **Logics**: same as MAM entry above
- **Default**: on by default
- **Does**: COMPARE tests two bound registers for equality; CHECK/FILTER/PFILTER prune candidates by approximate "label set" (function-symbol bit signature); CHOOSE forks the interpreter over alternative code-tree branches; CONTINUE resumes matching at a saved 2-deep "joint" position (depth-2 nested-variable optimization, per comment at `mam.cpp:172-179`, Z3 explicitly does not support depth-3 joints)
- **Provenance**: [C]

### MAM instructions: GET_ENODE / GET_CGR1..CGRN / IS_CGR (congruence-aware lookups)
- **Where**: `src/smt/mam.cpp:118-119`; `struct get_enode_instr:164`, `struct get_cgr:220`, `struct is_cgr:235`
- **Logics**: same as MAM entry above
- **Default**: on by default
- **Does**: GET_ENODE copies a known enode into a register; GET_CGR* looks up the congruence-closure representative of an application built from bound registers (reuses existing E-graph structure sharing instead of allocating); IS_CGR checks whether such a representative already exists without creating one
- **Provenance**: [C]

### `code_tree` / `code_tree_manager` / `compiler` (pattern -> code-tree compilation)
- **Where**: `class code_tree` `src/smt/mam.cpp:381`, `class code_tree_manager` `src/smt/mam.cpp:606`, `class compiler` `src/smt/mam.cpp:790`
- **Logics**: same as MAM entry above
- **Default**: on by default
- **Does**: compiles a quantifier's chosen multi-pattern into a shared, mergeable code tree (patterns with common prefixes share instruction paths), then owns tree lifetime/registration for the interpreter
- **Provenance**: [C]

### `code_tree_map` — per-root-label dispatch of code trees
- **Where**: `src/smt/mam.cpp:2897`
- **Logics**: same as MAM entry above
- **Default**: on by default
- **Does**: indexes compiled code trees by their pattern's root function/label so a new enode only triggers the code trees whose root matches it
- **Provenance**: [C]

### `quantifier_manager` — quantifier lifecycle owner
- **Where**: `src/smt/smt_quantifier.h:37`, `add`/`del`/`propagate` at `src/smt/smt_quantifier.cpp:468,476,544`
- **Logics**: any logic in which quantifiers are internalized (did not verify an exhaustive list beyond the AUF*/UFNIA/UFLRA/LRA family)
- **Default**: on by default (constructed whenever `context` is built)
- **Does**: tracks live quantifiers, forwards `push`/`pop`/`restart`/`assign_eh`/`relevant_eh`/`add_eq_eh` events to the active `quantifier_manager_plugin`, and drives `propagate()` each round
- **Provenance**: [C]

### `default_qm_plugin` — the shipped quantifier_manager_plugin
- **Where**: `src/smt/smt_quantifier.cpp:620`
- **Logics**: did not verify beyond "whatever calls quantifier_manager with the default plugin" (no evidence of an alternate plugin implementation in this file — `grep`ped only this one class deriving `quantifier_manager_plugin`)
- **Default**: on by default
- **Does**: owns the MAM instance, the qi_queue, the model_finder/model_checker/quick_checker/ho_qsolver, and decides per-quantifier whether MBQI applies (`model_based()` returns `m_fparams->m_mbqi`, `src/smt/smt_quantifier.cpp:768` i.e. offset 148 from class start = line 620+148=768)
- **Provenance**: [C]

### `mbqi_enabled` per-quantifier ID filter
- **Where**: `src/smt/smt_quantifier.cpp:779` (class-relative 620+159)
- **Logics**: quantified logics, gated further by `smt.mbqi_id`
- **Default**: `behind param mbqi_id` — empty by default so every quantifier is MBQI-eligible unless the user restricts it (`if (!m_fparams->m_mbqi_id) return true;`)
- **Does**: restricts MBQI activation to quantifiers whose `:qid` symbol has a given string prefix
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Model-Based Quantifier Instantiation (MBQI) driver
- **Where**: `src/smt/smt_model_finder.h:70` (`class model_finder`), `class auf_solver` at `src/smt/smt_model_finder.cpp:384`
- **Logics**: quantified logics; gated by `smt.mbqi` (default true, `src/params/smt_params.cpp:427`)
- **Default**: `on by default` (`m_mbqi = true`, `src/params/smt_params.cpp:427` and `:397`)
- **Does**: given a candidate ground model, tries to complete uninterpreted-function interpretations so all quantified formulas are also satisfied, using the "Almost Uninterpreted Fragment" (AUF) technique
- **Provenance**: [P]+[C] — header comment at `src/smt/smt_model_finder.h:24-29` cites "Complete instantiation for quantified SMT formulas", Yeting Ge and Leonardo de Moura, CAV 2009; `auf_solver`'s own doc comment at `src/smt/smt_model_finder.cpp:379` repeats the citation, and the `auf_solver` class exists in code, confirmed by `sed`

### AUF-solver constraint shapes: `f_var`, `f_var_plus_offset`, `ho_var`, `select_var`
- **Where**: `src/smt/smt_model_finder.cpp:1185,1265,1383,1506`
- **Logics**: same as MBQI entry above
- **Default**: on whenever MBQI runs
- **Does**: each is a distinct "qinfo" subclass recording one shape of constraint MBQI must satisfy when completing a function's interpretation — a bare function-of-variable read, a function-of-(variable+constant-offset) read (arithmetic special case), a higher-order variable occurrence, and an array/select occurrence, respectively
- **Provenance**: [C]

### AUF-solver constraint shapes: `x_eq_y`/`x_neq_y`/`x_leq_y`/`x_sleq_y`/`x_eq_t`/`x_neq_t`/`x_gle_t`
- **Where**: `src/smt/smt_model_finder.cpp:1610,1624,1636,1650,1685,1712,1729`
- **Logics**: same as MBQI entry above
- **Default**: on whenever MBQI runs
- **Does**: records pairwise variable/variable or variable/term relational constraints (equality, disequality, ≤, strict ≤, ≥/≤ combined) discovered while analyzing a quantifier body, consumed later to pick a satisfying witness domain per variable
- **Provenance**: [C]

### `quantifier_analyzer` — per-quantifier body analysis for MBQI
- **Where**: `src/smt/smt_model_finder.cpp:1888`, `quantifier_info` at `src/smt/smt_model_finder.cpp:1762`
- **Logics**: same as MBQI entry above
- **Default**: on whenever MBQI runs
- **Does**: walks a quantifier's body once to classify every subterm into the `qinfo` shapes above, building the `quantifier_info` MBQI later consumes
- **Provenance**: [C]

### `model_checker` — candidate-model quantifier verification
- **Where**: `src/smt/smt_model_checker.h:38`; module header explicitly distinguishes "Model checker AND Model-based quantifier instantiation" (`src/smt/smt_model_checker.h:10-11`)
- **Logics**: quantified logics
- **Default**: on by default whenever a quantified formula reaches final check
- **Does**: checks whether the model `model_finder` produced actually satisfies each quantifier, and if not, feeds counterexamples back into instantiation (distinct role from `model_finder`, which *constructs* candidate completions — `model_checker` *verifies* and drives refinement)
- **Provenance**: [C]

### `qi_queue` — instantiation candidate queue with cost model
- **Where**: `src/smt/qi_queue.h:42`, `get_cost` at `src/smt/qi_queue.cpp:120`, `instantiate()` at `src/smt/qi_queue.cpp:150`
- **Logics**: quantified logics
- **Default**: on by default; cost formula configurable via `smt.qi.cost` (default `"(+ weight generation)"`, `src/params/qi_params.h:81`)
- **Does**: scores every E-matching-produced candidate instantiation with a user-configurable cost expression (parsed and evaluated per-candidate) and defers/admits it against `qi_eager_threshold`/`qi_lazy_threshold`
- **Provenance**: [C]

### `qi_queue` generation-bump function (`m_qi_new_gen`)
- **Where**: `src/smt/qi_queue.cpp:37,57` (`m_new_gen_function`, default parsed from `m_params.m_qi_new_gen`)
- **Logics**: quantified logics
- **Default**: `behind param qi.new_gen` (falls back to the literal string `"cost"` if the configured expression fails to parse, `src/smt/qi_queue.cpp:61`)
- **Does**: computes the "generation" (matching-loop depth counter) assigned to instances produced FROM this instantiation, used to bound/detect runaway matching loops
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Lazy instantiation deferral queue
- **Where**: `src/smt/qi_queue.cpp:405-447` (`display_delayed_instances_stats`, the `m_qi_lazy_threshold` gated loop)
- **Logics**: quantified logics
- **Default**: `behind param qi_lazy_instantiation` (`m_qi_lazy_instantiation = false` by default, `src/params/qi_params.h:43`)
- **Does**: when enabled, delays cheap-enough instantiations across search rounds instead of committing them immediately, replaying only those under the lazy-cost threshold
- **Provenance**: [C]

### `quick_checker` — cheap pre-check before full E-matching
- **Where**: `src/smt/smt_quick_checker.h`/`src/smt/smt_quick_checker.cpp:26` (module header: "Incomplete model checker", `smt_quick_checker.cpp:10`); invoked at `src/smt/smt_quantifier.cpp:406-411`
- **Logics**: quantified logics
- **Default**: `behind param qi_quick_checker` — base `smt_params` default is `MC_NO` (`src/params/qi_params.h:39`) but the shipped SMT config sets `m_qi_quick_checker = MC_UNSAT` (`src/params/smt_params.cpp:395,415`), so it is effectively ON by default for the normal solver configuration
- **Does**: an intentionally incomplete/unsound-for-completeness quick scan for candidate quantifier violations, used to short-circuit expensive full E-matching when a quantifier is quickly seen to be already satisfied or violated
- **Provenance**: [C]

### `ho_qsolver` — higher-order pattern quantifier solving
- **Where**: `src/smt/smt_ho_qsolver.h:21`, `final_check` at `src/smt/smt_ho_qsolver.cpp:257`, driven from `src/smt/smt_quantifier.cpp:150,428`
- **Logics**: quantified logics containing higher-order patterns (did not verify a specific SMT-LIB logic gate beyond "wired unconditionally into `default_qm_plugin`")
- **Default**: on by default — constructed unconditionally in `default_qm_plugin`'s init (`smt_quantifier.cpp:150`) and consulted at final check (`smt_quantifier.cpp:428`); NOT dead, confirmed live via `ast/euf/ho_matcher.h`
- **Does**: wraps `euf::ho_matcher` to find and check higher-order pattern instantiations that plain first-order E-matching (MAM) cannot express
- **Provenance**: [C]

### `euf::ho_matcher` integration inside `quantifier_manager`
- **Where**: `src/smt/smt_quantifier.cpp:628,673-678,736,761,853` (class-relative offsets inside `default_qm_plugin`)
- **Logics**: same as ho_qsolver entry
- **Default**: `behind param ho_matching_bound` for iteration cap (`m_ho_matching_bound`, `smt_quantifier.cpp:674`); otherwise on whenever a pattern is detected as higher-order (`is_ho_pattern`, `smt_quantifier.cpp:736`)
- **Does**: compiles higher-order patterns (`compile_ho_pattern`) and refines higher-order matches (`refine_ho_match`) as a second matching pass alongside MAM
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Relevancy propagation event handlers (Boolean connective specializations)
- **Where**: `src/smt/smt_relevancy.h:27,47,57`; connective-specific subclasses `and_relevancy_eh`/`or_relevancy_eh`/`implies_relevancy_eh`/`ite_relevancy_eh`/`ite_term_relevancy_eh` at `src/smt/smt_relevancy.cpp:51,58,65,72,79`
- **Logics**: any logic using relevancy propagation (gated by `m_relevancy_lvl`, see core-search section for the general engine; `setup_relevancy` forces `m_relevancy_lvl=0` for quantifier-free BV, `src/smt/smt_setup.cpp:829-839`)
- **Default**: `behind param relevancy` (`m_relevancy_lvl`), non-zero levels are the default in many `setup_*` paths except QF_RDL/QF_IDL/QF_UFIDL/quantifier-free-BV which force it to 0

- **Provenance**: [C]

### `smt_induction.cpp.disabled` / `smt_induction.h.disabled` — induction lemma generator (NOT COMPILED)
- **Where**: `src/smt/smt_induction.cpp.disabled:1-24` (module header), confirmed absent from the build: `grep -n "smt_induction" src/smt/CMakeLists.txt` returns nothing

- **Does**: (as designed, not running) adds induction lemmas to the context by generalizing over recursively-defined structure
- **Provenance**: [C] for the dead-code determination; [I] for what it would do since it never runs

- **Logics**: all
- **Default**: on by default when the `get-consequences` SMT-LIB command / API entry point is invoked (not part of ordinary `check-sat`)
- **Does**: computes all fixed/implied literal values over a set of "variables of interest" given a set of assumptions, using incremental core extraction (`extract_fixed_consequences`, `extract_cores`, `smt_consequences.cpp:49,139,462`)
- **Provenance**: [C]

### `context::preferred_sat` / `find_mutexes`
- **Where**: `src/smt/smt_consequences.cpp:493,533,600`
- **Logics**: all
- **Default**: on by default when invoked via the API (not part of ordinary `check-sat`)
- **Does**: `preferred_sat` searches for a satisfying assignment preferring given literals and returns unsat cores per rejected preference; `find_mutexes` finds mutually-exclusive-literal groups among a set of Boolean variables
- **Provenance**: [C]

### `get_implied_equalities` — external implied-equality query
- **Where**: `src/smt/smt_implied_equalities.cpp:35` (`class get_implied_equalities_impl`), three strategies at `:74,113,147`
- **Logics**: all; module header "Procedure for obtaining implied equalities relative to the state of a solver" (`smt_implied_equalities.cpp:6-11`)

- **Does**: three distinct strategies — `get_implied_equalities_filter_basic` (syntactic filtering), `get_implied_equalities_basic` (direct enode-equivalence read), `get_implied_equalities_model_based` (uses a computed model to cluster terms by value) — to partition a term set into implied-equal classes
- **Provenance**: [C]

### `dyn_ack_manager` — dynamic Ackermannization
- **Where**: `src/smt/dyn_ack.h:32`; justification classes `dyn_ack_cc_justification`/`dyn_ack_eq_justification` at `src/smt/dyn_ack.cpp:28,103`; module header "Dynamic Ackermann's reduction" (`dyn_ack.cpp:6-9`)

- **Default**: `behind param dack` (`m_params.m_dack`) — did not verify the compiled-in default value from this file alone; cross-referenced name only, not the params default
- **Does**: lazily adds Ackermann-reduction axioms (`f(a)=f(b)` congruence lemmas) only for function-application pairs that repeatedly conflict, instead of eagerly Ackermannizing the whole formula
- **Provenance**: [C]

### `arith_eq_adapter` — arithmetic-equality-to-other-theories bridge
- **Where**: `src/smt/arith_eq_adapter.h:46`, `mk_axioms` at `src/smt/arith_eq_adapter.h:85`
- **Logics**: any logic combining arithmetic with another theory over shared terms
- **Default**: did not verify whether this runs unconditionally or behind a param
- **Does**: generates bridging axioms when an arithmetic equality needs to be reflected into another theory's congruence view (theory combination glue)
- **Provenance**: [C]

### `arith_eq_solver` integer-equation elimination family
- **Where**: `src/smt/arith_eq_solver.h:37,41,46,53,58,79,102` (`gcd_test`, `solve_integer_equations_units`, `solve_integer_equations_omega`, `solve_integer_equations_hermite`, `solve_integer_equations_gcd`, `solve_integer_equation`, `solve_integer_equations`)
- **Logics**: integer arithmetic logics (QF_LIA/QF_UFLIA/AUFLIA family) — did not verify the exact caller/gating param
- **Default**: did not verify
- **Does**: FOUR distinct linear Diophantine elimination strategies exposed as separate entry points — GCD feasibility test, unit-coefficient elimination, the Omega-test style projection, and Hermite Normal Form elimination — selected per-equation-set shape
- **Provenance**: [C] for existence of each named function; [I] that "Omega test"/"Hermite normal form" here match the textbook algorithms of the same name (plausible from the function names, not confirmed by reading the bodies)

### `is_value_sort` — sort-is-a-value-domain check
- **Where**: `src/smt/smt_value_sort.cpp:29,70`; module header "Determine if elements of a given sort can be values" (`smt_value_sort.cpp:6-9`)
- **Logics**: all
- **Default**: on by default, called wherever model/value construction needs to know if a sort has a canonical value representation
- **Does**: a structural sort classifier deciding whether Z3 can directly represent values of a given sort (vs. needing an uninterpreted/opaque domain)
- **Provenance**: [C]

### `expr_context_simplifier` / `expr_strong_context_simplifier`
- **Where**: `src/smt/expr_context_simplifier.h:28,57`
- **Logics**: all
- **Default**: `behind tactic ctx-solver-simplify` for the strong variant (see `ctx_solver_simplify_tactic` below); did not verify whether the plain `expr_context_simplifier` runs anywhere unconditionally
- **Does**: simplifies an expression using the current context's already-known truth assignments to prune unreachable/contradictory subterms (weak: syntactic context; strong: uses a full sub-solver call per node)
- **Provenance**: [C]

### `uses_theory` — theory-membership term scan
- **Where**: `src/smt/uses_theory.h:26,33`, `src/smt/uses_theory.cpp:23,39`
- **Logics**: all
- **Default**: on by default, a plain utility predicate
- **Does**: walks a term (with memoized visited-set) to check whether any subterm belongs to a given theory family id
- **Provenance**: [C]

### `for_each_relevant_expr` — relevancy-aware term traversal
- **Where**: `src/smt/smt_for_each_relevant_expr.cpp:123`, connective handling at `:226,240,254` (`process_and`,`process_or`,`process_ite`)
- **Logics**: all, only meaningful when relevancy propagation is active
- **Default**: on by default as a utility, but only visits nodes the relevancy engine has marked relevant
- **Does**: traverses a formula skipping subterms deemed irrelevant by the current relevancy assignment, with special-cased short-circuiting for and/or/ite so only the "deciding" children are visited

### `install_smt2_extra_cmds` — SMT-LIB2 front-end additions
- **Where**: `src/smt/smt2_extra_cmds.cpp:45`, `include_cmd` at `src/smt/smt2_extra_cmds.cpp:23`

### `proto_model` — pre-finalization model container
- **Where**: `src/smt/proto_model/proto_model.h:40`
- **Logics**: all

### `smt_tactic` — tactic wrapper exposing `smt::kernel` as a `tactic`

## Dead or unreachable

### From the theory-solver pass
- **`theory_wmaxsat`**: no `alloc(theory_wmaxsat...)` or `register_plugin` call anywhere under
  `src/smt/` — the only constructor definition is its own `.cpp`; live construction happens from
  `src/opt/wmax.cpp` / `src/opt/maxsmt.cpp`, i.e. reachable only through the `opt`/MaxSMT module,
  never through `smt_setup.cpp`'s plugin registration that every other theory goes through. `[C]`
- **`theory_seq_empty`**: no `register_plugin(alloc(smt::theory_seq_empty...))` call site found
  anywhere in `src/` (search scoped to this pass, not a full-repo confirmation — `did not verify`
  exhaustively). `theory_seq` (the real engine) is what `setup_seq()` registers
  (`smt_setup.cpp:784`). Possibly unreachable/legacy placeholder. `[C]` for the negative grep,
  `[I]` for "legacy" characterization.
- **`theory_dense_diff_logic` / `theory_utvpi` / `theory_diff_logic` (idl/rdl/fidl/frdl) /
  `theory_mi_arith`/`theory_i_arith`/`theory_inf_arith` (the old `theory_arith<Ext>` template)**:
  not dead, but reachable ONLY via non-default `arith.solver` values (1/3/4/2/5); the shipped
  default is `theory_lra` (`arith.solver=6`). A differential fuzz that only exercises "the
  arithmetic theory" at default settings will silently miss this entire family. `[C]`

### From the core-search pass
- **`src/smt/smt_context.cpp:1761`** — `#if 0` block calling `induction::should_try(*this)` /
  `get_induction()()` inside `propagate_theories`; induction-based reasoning is compiled out of
  the core propagate loop entirely (consistent with `smt_induction.cpp.disabled` being unbuilt,
  see below).
- **`src/smt/smt_context.cpp:3639`** — `#if 0` model-validation self-check block (walks
  `m_assigned_literals` looking for assignments contradicting the built model) inside the
  post-search validation path; compiled out.
- **`src/smt/smt_context.cpp:4495`** — `#if 0` block (a static counter/total/max instrumentation
  snippet) right after proof-justification construction; compiled out, reads as abandoned
  profiling code.
- **`src/smt/smt_case_split_queue.cpp:699-705`** — `GOAL_START()`/`GOAL_STOP()` timing macros in
  `rel_goal_case_split_queue` are defined as no-ops; the `#if 0` branch that would enable
  `m_goal_time` timing is permanently dead, so goal-queue timing instrumentation never runs even
  when `CS_RELEVANCY_GOAL` is selected.
- **`CS_RELEVANCY` / `CS_RELEVANCY_ACTIVITY` / `CS_RELEVANCY_GOAL` case-split strategies are
  effectively gated off in most configurations.** `mk_case_split_queue`
  (`src/smt/smt_case_split_queue.cpp:1251-1259`) silently downgrades any of these three to
  `CS_ACTIVITY` whenever `relevancy_lvl() < 2` or `m_auto_config` is on — so a user can set
  `smt.case_split=3/4/5` and, under auto-config (the default) or default relevancy, silently get
  plain activity-based splitting instead. A param that is easy to request and structurally hard
  to actually get.
- **`PS_THEORY` phase selection** (`src/smt/smt_context.cpp:1913`) has its one candidate call
  site commented out (`src/smt/smt_setup.cpp:415`: `// m_params.m_phase_selection = PS_THEORY;`)
  — no logic setup currently turns it on; only reachable via an explicit user override of
  `smt.phase_selection=7`.



- **`theory_si_arith` / `theory_smi_arith` — entirely commented out, not just unused.** The
  typedefs themselves are comments: `src/smt/theory_arith.h:1276-1277`
  (`// typedef theory_arith<si_ext> theory_si_arith;` / `// typedef theory_arith<smi_ext>
  theory_smi_arith;`). Every call site that would construct them is also commented, e.g.
  `src/smt/smt_setup.cpp:298-299,372-374`. These are not merely "no caller" — the *type does not
  exist* in compiled code. `[C]`
- **`smt_induction.cpp.disabled` / `smt_induction.h.disabled` — not compiled at all.** File
  extension is `.disabled`, and `grep -n "smt_induction" src/smt/CMakeLists.txt` returns nothing
  (confirmed empty match). See the Quantifier machinery entry above for what it would do
  (automated induction lemmas). `[C]`
- **`AR_MODEL_BASED` array mode throws unconditionally.** `src/smt/smt_setup.cpp:726-728`:
  `case AR_MODEL_BASED: throw default_exception("The model-based array theory solver is
  deprecated"); break;` — the enum value is reachable (any code path setting
  `m_array_mode=AR_MODEL_BASED` will hit this), but the moment it is reached the solver always
  aborts with a fixed error; there is no live model-based array theory behind it anymore. `[C]`
- **`AUFLIA+/-`, `AUFLIRA+/-`, `AUFNIRA+/-` logic-string suffixes are semantically inert.**
  `src/smt/smt_setup.cpp:113-124` routes every one of these six suffixed logic names to the exact
  same `setup_AUFLIA()`/`setup_AUFLIRA()` call as their unsuffixed base logic — the `+`/`-` never
  changes configuration at this layer. `[I]` (the suffixes may matter to feature/other code
  outside `src/smt/` — did not verify).
- **`setup_unknown()` (no static_features) double-registers BV.** `src/smt/smt_setup.cpp:805-823`
  calls `setup_bv()` directly (813) AND `setup_fpa()` (818), and `setup_fpa` itself
  (`smt_setup.cpp:778-781`) calls `setup_bv()` again. `setup::setup_bv()`
  (`smt_setup.cpp:696-699`) guards against double registration with `if
  (m_context.get_theory(bv_fid)) return;`, so this is harmless in practice, not a bug — but it
  means reading `setup_unknown()` as "BV is configured once" is wrong; flagging so nobody
  attributes BV-specific setup ordering effects to a single call site. `[C]`
- **`if (false && ...)` / `#if 0` blocks scattered across the theory solvers** (not exhaustively
  entered as individual inventory rows — listed here as a pointer list since each is a small
  disabled branch, not a distinct procedure): `src/smt/theory_arith_nl.h:1138`,
  `src/smt/theory_array_base.cpp:502,870`, `src/smt/theory_sls.cpp:127,256`,
  `src/smt/theory_arith_aux.h:1660,2017,2267`, `src/smt/smt_case_split_queue.cpp:699`,
  `src/smt/seq_ne_solver.cpp:259`, `src/smt/theory_diff_logic_def.h:666,1391`,
  `src/smt/smt_context_inv.cpp:35`, `src/smt/theory_arith_core.h:91`, `src/smt/qi_queue.cpp:253,516`,
  `src/smt/theory_lra.cpp:1084,1370,1389,1414,1428,1437,2439,2622,3343`,
  `src/smt/theory_arith_int.h:1062`, `src/smt/seq_eq_solver.cpp:1216`, `src/smt/seq_axioms.cpp:83`,
  `src/smt/theory_seq.cpp:780,1590,2208,2329,2347`, `src/smt/smt_implied_equalities.cpp:388`,
  `src/smt/smt_induction.cpp.disabled:484` (moot, whole file is dead), `src/smt/smt_context_pp.cpp:218`,
  `src/smt/theory_recfun.cpp:119`, `src/smt/theory_pb.cpp:1363,1602`, `src/smt/mam.cpp:971`.
  Obtained via `grep -rn "^#if 0\|if (false &&\|if(false &&" src/smt/`. Each is a genuinely
  disabled branch at that exact line; I did NOT read every one to characterize what it would do —
  treat only the line numbers as verified, not the semantics. `[C]` for existence, `[I]`/not
  characterized for intent.
- **`theory_wmaxsat` is never registered inside `src/smt/`.** No `setup_*` function in
  `smt_setup.cpp` touches it; it is only constructed from `src/opt/maxsmt.cpp:144`
  (`alloc(smt::theory_wmaxsat, ...)`), i.e. reachable only through the MaxSMT/optimization layer,

- **`theory_finite_set_size` is not a `theory` at all.** It has no `register_plugin` call
