# Part 3 — `src/sat/`: CDCL core and inprocessing


Repo: `/home/mjbommar/projects/personal/axeyum/references/z3`, HEAD `e18d63bda04fcab8240eb55314d567db3e43d540`.
Scope: `src/sat/` excluding `src/sat/smt/` (covered by another lane) and `src/sat/tactic/`.
All line numbers from `grep -n` against this exact commit; re-check before citing elsewhere.

Files that the brief names but that **do not exist anywhere in this repository's history**
(confirmed via `git log --oneline --all -- '<pattern>'` returning nothing): `sat_binspr.cpp`,
`sat_lut_finder.*`, `sat_cutset.*`, `sat_aig_cuts.*`. Treated as never-existed, not "removed."
`sat_prob.cpp` exists and is ProbSAT-style local search (class `prob`), distinct from
`sat_local_search.cpp` (WalkSAT-style, class `local_search`) and `sat_ddfw_wrapper.cpp`
(DDFW wrapper — the actual DDFW algorithm lives in `src/ast/sls/sat_ddfw.cpp`, **outside**
`src/sat/`, noted but not inventoried in depth here).

---

## CDCL core

### Two-watched-literal propagation
- **Where**: `src/sat/sat_solver.cpp:1013` (`solver::propagate`), core loop `src/sat/sat_solver.cpp:1041` (`solver::propagate_literal`)
- **Logics**: every logic that bottoms out in the SAT core (bit-blasted BV/array/etc. plus `sat.euf`)
- **Default**: on by default (not a param — this is the engine)
- **Does**: watch-list literal propagation with a blocking-literal optimization (`get_blocked_literal()`) to skip full clause scans
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Binary-clause special-casing in propagation
- **Where**: `src/sat/sat_watched.h:42-43` (`enum kind { BINARY = 0, CLAUSE, EXT_CONSTRAINT }`); handled inline at `src/sat/sat_solver.cpp:1050-1064`
- **Logics**: same as propagation
- **Default**: on by default, structural (not a param)
- **Does**: binary clauses are stored directly in the watch list as a literal, not as a clause pointer — no clause-array indirection on the hot path
- **Provenance**: [C]

### No dedicated ternary watch kind in the main solver
- **Where**: `src/sat/sat_watched.h:42-43` — only `BINARY`, `CLAUSE`, `EXT_CONSTRAINT`
- **Logics**: n/a (absence)
- **Default**: n/a
- **Does**: unlike some CDCL implementations, the *main* solver does not special-case ternary clauses in the watch representation; a size-3 clause is a `CLAUSE`. Ternary special-casing (`add_ternary`, `propagate_ternary`) exists only in the separate lookahead solver (`sat_lookahead.cpp:1223,1233,1263`), not in normal CDCL search.
- **Provenance**: [C][I] (structural inference from the enum + grep for `propagate_ternary`)

### Watch-list prefetching
- **Where**: `sat.propagate.prefetch` — read at `src/sat/sat_config.cpp:83` into `m_propagate_prefetch`; consumer not individually traced in this pass
- **Logics**: did not verify beyond config wiring
- **Default**: `on by default (sat.propagate.prefetch, default true)`
- **Does**: prefetches watch lists for assigned literals to hide memory latency
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Conflict analysis — 1st-UIP resolution
- **Where**: `src/sat/sat_solver.cpp:2477` (`solver::resolve_conflict_core`), main resolution loop `2540-2609`
- **Logics**: SAT core
- **Default**: on by default (the algorithm, not a switch)
- **Does**: resolves back along the implication graph, marking antecedents until exactly one literal at the conflict level remains (`num_marks > 0` loop) — classic 1-UIP
- **Provenance**: [C][P] (Marques-Silva & Sakallah / Zhang et al. 1-UIP scheme)

### Recursive (self-)minimization of learned clause
- **Where**: `src/sat/sat_solver.cpp:3333` (`solver::minimize_lemma`), driven by `implied_by_marked`
- **Logics**: SAT core
- **Default**: `on by default (sat.minimize_lemmas, default true)`
- **Does**: drops literals from the learned clause that are implied by the other literals (transitively, via marks) plus level-0 units
- **Provenance**: [C][P] (Sörensson & Biere minimization)

### Binary-resolution ("local") minimization
- **Where**: `src/sat/sat_solver.cpp:3299` (`solver::minimize_lemma_binres`)
- **Logics**: SAT core
- **Default**: called unconditionally alongside recursive minimization when `sat.minimize_lemmas` is on — did not verify a separate on/off flag for just this pass
- **Does**: uses binary clauses on the watch list of the asserting literal to drop further lemma literals subsumed by a binary
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Dynamic subsumption resolution (`dyn_sub_res`)
- **Where**: `src/sat/sat_solver.cpp:3423` (`solver::dyn_sub_res`), invoked at `2634`
- **Logics**: SAT core
- **Default**: `on by default (sat.dyn_sub_res, default true)`
- **Does**: after minimization, uses binary/ternary clauses to further subsume-resolve literals out of the new lemma
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Glue / LBD computation
- **Where**: `src/sat/sat_solver.cpp:2652` (`num_diff_levels` call inside `learn_lemma_and_backjump`), helper `num_diff_levels` at `3124`, bounded variant `num_diff_levels_below` at `3141`
- **Logics**: SAT core
- **Default**: always computed for every learned clause
- **Does**: counts distinct decision levels among the lemma's literals = glue/LBD; feeds fast/slow EMA and clause-DB tiering
- **Provenance**: [C][P] (Audemard & Simon glucose LBD)

### Backjumping vs. chronological backtracking
- **Where**: `src/sat/sat_solver.cpp:2648-2680` (`learn_lemma_and_backjump`), decision gate `use_backjumping` at `2683`, `allow_backtracking` at `2688`
- **Logics**: SAT core
- **Default**: `sat.backtrack.scopes (default 100)`, `sat.backtrack.conflicts (default 4000)` — chronological backtracking only kicks in after 4000 conflicts since init, and only for jumps of ≤100 scopes
- **Does**: chooses between standard 1-UIP backjump and cheaper chronological backtrack (Nadel & Ryvchin style) based on jump distance and conflict count
- **Provenance**: [C][P] (Nadel & Ryvchin, "Chronological Backtracking")

### Unique-max early pop (force_conflict_analysis path)
- **Where**: `src/sat/sat_solver.cpp:2506-2514`
- **Logics**: SAT core, notably interacts with `sat.euf`/external theories (comment: "external theories miss propagations that only get triggered after decisions")
- **Default**: on whenever `allow_backtracking()` holds and the conflict has a unique max level
- **Does**: instead of full resolution, pops straight to `conflict_lvl` and lets propagation re-derive the negated literal, forcing a second pass through `resolve_conflict_core`
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Restart — Luby
- **Where**: dispatch `src/sat/sat_solver.cpp:2436-2450` (`set_next_restart`, case `RS_LUBY`), sequence generator `#include "util/luby.h"` at line 25
- **Logics**: SAT core
- **Default**: `behind param sat.restart=luby (default is "ema", not luby)`; `sat.restart.initial (default 2)`
- **Does**: restart threshold = `restart.initial * luby(idx)`, idx incremented every restart
- **Provenance**: [C][P] (Luby, Sinclair, Zuckerman)

### Restart — geometric
- **Where**: `src/sat/sat_solver.cpp:2440-2442` (case `RS_GEOMETRIC`)
- **Default**: `behind param sat.restart=geometric`; factor `sat.restart.factor (default 1.5)`
- **Does**: threshold multiplied by `restart.factor` after every restart
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Restart — EMA (glue-based, default)
- **Where**: `should_restart` at `src/sat/sat_solver.cpp:2309-2317`; threshold reset `2443-2444`
- **Default**: `on by default (sat.restart = ema)`; `sat.restart.emafastglue (3e-2)`, `sat.restart.emaslowglue (1e-5)`, `sat.restart.margin (1.1)`, `sat.restart.fast (true)`
- **Does**: restarts when the fast glue EMA has caught up to/passed the search level and `margin * slow_glue_avg <= fast_glue_avg` — a Chanseok Oh-style glue-EMA restart, gated additionally by `m_case_split_queue` non-empty and scope depth
- **Provenance**: [C][P] (Biere & Fröhlich EMA restarts / glucose-style)

### Restart — static (never triggers early)
- **Where**: `src/sat/sat_solver.cpp:2445-2446` (case `RS_STATIC`: threshold unchanged, `break`)
- **Default**: `behind param sat.restart=static`
- **Does**: no growth of the restart threshold — effectively restarts on a fixed period set once at `restart.initial` and never adjusted by this switch statement
- **Provenance**: [C]

### `should_restart` gating conditions
- **Where**: `src/sat/sat_solver.cpp:2309-2317`
- **Does**: three independent gates before any restart strategy applies: conflict count since last restart must exceed threshold, scope depth must exceed search level by ≥2, and the decision queue must be non-empty
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Burst search (early fixed-budget bounded search before full restart policy)
- **Where**: `src/sat/sat_solver.cpp:1316-1327` inside `solver::check`
- **Default**: `sat.burst_search (default 100 conflicts)`
- **Does**: runs `bounded_search` with restart threshold pinned to `burst_search`, restart/simplify disabled (`bounded_search` sets `m_simplify_enabled=false`, `m_restart_enabled=false` — `1808-1810`), before falling into normal `search()`
- **Provenance**: [C]

### VSIDS decision heuristic
- **Where**: dispatch `src/sat/sat_solver.cpp:931-935`; activity bump `inc_activity` `src/sat/sat_solver.h:807-813`; decay `decay_activity` `src/sat/sat_solver.h:816-819`
- **Default**: `on by default (sat.branching.heuristic = vsids)`; decay `sat.variable_decay (default 110, i.e. /100 = 1.10x)`
- **Does**: classic VSIDS — bump on every clause literal, periodic multiplicative decay of the increment; rescale at `1<<24` (`rescale_activity`)
- **Provenance**: [C][P] (Moskewicz et al., Chaff)

### CHB decision heuristic (Liang/Ganesh/Poupart/Czarnecki AAAI'16)
- **Where**: dispatch `src/sat/sat_solver.cpp:1016-1018`, `2909-2917`; core update `update_chb_activity` `src/sat/sat_solver.cpp:3852`
- **Default**: `behind param sat.branching.heuristic=chb (default vsids)`; step-size params hardcoded in `sat_config.cpp:214-218` (`m_step_size_init=0.40`, `m_step_size_dec=1e-6`, `m_step_size_min=0.06`) — **not exposed via `sat_params.pyg`** (no `sat.step_size.*` entry exists), only the top-level `branching.heuristic` symbol is public
- **Does**: Conflict-History-Based branching; reward = 1/(conflicts since var last touched), activity is an exponential moving average of reward with a decaying step size
- **Provenance**: [C][P]

### LRB-style "reasoned" side bookkeeping under VSIDS
- **Where**: `src/sat/sat_solver.cpp:3361-3363` (`reset_lemma_var_marks` calls `update_lrb_reasoned()` when `m_config.m_branching_heuristic == BH_VSIDS`), impl `3373-3413`
- **Default**: runs unconditionally whenever VSIDS is selected (i.e., by default), no separate switch found
- **Does**: walks the justification graph of the learned clause and bumps activity for every literal touched during conflict analysis (`m_reasoned[v]++`, `inc_activity(v)`) — a Learning-Rate-Based-style side channel layered under nominal "VSIDS" mode
- **Provenance**: [C][I] (naming/behavior matches LRB reasoned-clause bumping from Liang et al.'s LRB paper, but the branch is unconditionally taken under `BH_VSIDS` rather than a separate `BH_LRB` mode — surprising)

### Anti-exploration heuristic for branching
- **Where**: param wiring `src/sat/sat_config.cpp:222` (`m_anti_exploration = p.branching_anti_exploration()`); consumer not traced further in this pass
- **Default**: `behind param sat.branching.anti_exploration (default false)`
- **Does**: per doc string, biases branch selection away from over-explored variables (MAB-style)
- **Provenance**: [C] (wiring only) / did not verify consumer logic

### Phase saving (basic)
- **Where**: `updt_phase_of_vars` `src/sat/sat_solver.cpp:2944-2964`
- **Default**: on whenever phase strategy ≠ `PS_FROZEN` (default phase strategy is `caching` = `PS_SAT_CACHING`, which is not frozen, so this runs by default)
- **Does**: on conflict, randomizes the phase of every variable popped off the trail above the conflict level (forgets old phase), then updates "best phase" bookkeeping if a new longest sat-prefix trail is found
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Phase strategy: always_true / always_false
- **Where**: `do_rephase` cases `PS_ALWAYS_TRUE`/`PS_ALWAYS_FALSE`, `src/sat/sat_solver.cpp:3009-3016`
- **Default**: `behind param sat.phase in {always_true, always_false}` (default `caching`)
- **Does**: forces every variable's saved phase to true/false respectively at each rephase point
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Phase strategy: basic_caching (cyclic 4-way rephase)
- **Where**: `do_rephase` case `PS_BASIC_CACHING`, `src/sat/sat_solver.cpp:3021-3040`
- **Default**: `behind param sat.phase=basic_caching`
- **Does**: cycles through {random, all-false, flip-all, no-op} every 4th rephase round based on `m_rephase.count % 4`
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Phase strategy: caching / sticky (default) + best-phase rephasing
- **Where**: `do_rephase` case `PS_SAT_CACHING`, `src/sat/sat_solver.cpp:3041-3044`; "sticky" flag `sat.phase.sticky (default true)` wired at `sat_config.cpp:76`
- **Default**: `on by default (sat.phase = caching)`
- **Does**: on rephase, if search state is `s_sat`, overwrite current phase with the recorded best phase (`m_best_phase`)
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Phase strategy: random
- **Where**: `do_rephase` case `PS_RANDOM`, `src/sat/sat_solver.cpp:3045-3049`
- **Default**: `behind param sat.phase=random`
- **Does**: every rephase, randomize all saved phases
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Phase strategy: local_search-seeded rephasing
- **Where**: `do_rephase` case `PS_LOCAL_SEARCH`, `src/sat/sat_solver.cpp:3050-3057`; invokes `bounded_local_search` (`src/sat/sat_solver.cpp:1376`)
- **Default**: `behind param sat.phase=local_search`
- **Does**: when search state is sat, with 50% probability run a bounded local-search pass (DDFW by default — `alloc(ddfw_wrapper)` at `1391`) to seed a better phase assignment, then copy its best model into saved phases
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Rephase scheduling (frequency)
- **Where**: `should_rephase` `src/sat/sat_solver.cpp:3002-3005`; increment schedule `3105-3107`
- **Default**: `sat.rephase.base (default 1000 conflicts)`, with `m_rephase_inc` growing by `rephase_base` each round (arithmetic backoff, not geometric)
- **Does**: gates rephasing to roughly every `rephase.base` conflicts, growing the gap linearly over the run
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Two-phase search-state toggling (SAT-mode / UNSAT-mode)
- **Where**: `should_toggle_search_state` `src/sat/sat_solver.cpp:2966-2972`, `do_toggle_search_state` `2974-2994`
- **Default**: `sat.search.unsat.conflicts (400)`, `sat.search.sat.conflicts (400)`
- **Does**: alternates the solver between an "unsat-seeking" and "sat-seeking" mode using a trail-length-vs-average heuristic; on toggle it swaps in a backed-up pair of fast/slow glue EMAs and grows the conflict budget for the mode just exited (a form of search-state diversification, cf. "two-phase" solvers like Cadical's "unsat/sat" stability toggling)
- **Provenance**: [C][I] (mechanism resembles CaDiCaL's stabilization toggling in spirit, not confirmed as a direct port)

### Random decisions
- **Where**: consumed in `decide()`, `src/sat/sat_solver.cpp:1698-1790` (guess/`m_rand`), param wiring `sat_config.cpp:87-88`
- **Default**: `sat.random_freq (default 0.01)`, `sat.random_seed (default 0)`
- **Does**: with frequency `random_freq`, pick a random unassigned variable/phase instead of the activity-ranked next var
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Random reorder / activity reshuffle (softmax)
- **Where**: `should_reorder` `src/sat/sat_solver.cpp:3061-3063`, `do_reorder` `3065-3103`
- **Default**: `sat.reorder.base (default UINT_MAX ⇒ effectively disabled by default)`, `sat.reorder.itau (4.0)`, `sat.reorder.activity_scale (100)`
- **Does**: periodically reassigns every unassigned variable's activity via a softmax over random logits (inverse-temperature `itau`) — a randomized diversification of the VSIDS order; a dead `#if 0` alternate branch does plain uniform shuffle instead (`3086-3096`, compiled out)
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Clause DB garbage collection — dispatch + scheduling
- **Where**: `should_gc`/`do_gc` `src/sat/sat_gc.cpp:31-70`
- **Default**: `sat.gc = glue_psm (default)`; `sat.gc.initial (20000)`, `sat.gc.increment (500)`
- **Does**: gates GC on conflict count since last GC exceeding a growing threshold (except `GC_DYN_PSM`, which additionally requires being at base level); dispatches to one of 5 strategies
- **Provenance**: [C]

### GC strategy: glue
- **Where**: `gc_glue`, referenced via `glue_lt` comparator `src/sat/sat_gc.cpp:76-81`; strategy enum `GC_GLUE` `sat_config.h:46`
- **Default**: `behind param sat.gc=glue`
- **Does**: sorts learned clauses by (glue, size) and deletes the worst tier
- **Provenance**: [C][P] (glue/LBD-based clause deletion, Audemard & Simon)

### GC strategy: psm
- **Where**: `psm_lt` comparator `src/sat/sat_gc.cpp:84-89`; `GC_PSM`
- **Default**: `behind param sat.gc=psm`
- **Does**: sorts by (PSM = "phase saving match" score, size) instead of glue
- **Provenance**: [C]

### GC strategy: glue_psm (default) / psm_glue
- **Where**: `glue_psm_lt` `src/sat/sat_gc.cpp:93-101`, `psm_glue_lt` `104-112`
- **Default**: `on by default (sat.gc = glue_psm)`
- **Does**: lexicographic combination of glue and PSM (order depends on which of the two strategies)
- **Provenance**: [C]

### GC strategy: dyn_psm (tiered, k-round survival)
- **Where**: dispatch `src/sat/sat_gc.cpp:57-65` (`GC_DYN_PSM` case, `gc_dyn_psm`)
- **Default**: `behind param sat.gc=dyn_psm`; `sat.gc.small_lbd (3)`, `sat.gc.k (7)`
- **Does**: clauses with LBD ≤ `gc.small_lbd` are never deleted; others survive up to `gc.k` inactive GC rounds before permanent deletion — only applied when not tracking assumptions and at base level (falls back to `gc_glue_psm` otherwise, `62-64`)
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Clause DB defragmentation
- **Where**: `should_defrag`/`defrag_clauses`, invoked at `src/sat/sat_gc.cpp:67-69`
- **Default**: `sat.gc.defrag (default true)`; threshold `m_defrag_threshold` init 2 (`sat_solver.cpp:2043`)
- **Does**: compacts the clause allocator after GC frees enough clauses
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Eager GC on initialization
- **Where**: `src/sat/sat_solver.cpp:1296-1299` (`m_config.m_gc_burst`)
- **Default**: `behind param sat.gc.burst (default false)`
- **Does**: forces one GC pass right after `init_search`, before any conflicts occur
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Activity rescaling
- **Where**: `inc_activity`, `src/sat/sat_solver.h:807-813`, `rescale_activity` declared `822`
- **Default**: triggers automatically once activity exceeds `1<<24`
- **Does**: prevents overflow of the fixed-point/unsigned activity counters by rescaling all activities down
- **Provenance**: [C]

---

## Inprocessing / preprocessing

### Overall inprocessing schedule (per `do_simplify` call)
- **Where**: `src/sat/sat_solver.cpp:2064-2160`
- **Does**, in order: `m_cleaner` (tautology/unit cleanup) → `m_scc()` (equivalent-literal substitution) → `m_ext->pre_simplify()` (theory hook) → `m_simplifier(false)` (subsumption/BCE/BVE over non-learned) → `m_simplifier(true)` if any learned clauses exist → `sort_watch_lits()` → `m_ext->simplify()` → `m_probing()` (failed-literal probing) → `m_asymm_branch(false)` (ALA/UHTE/UHLE) → optional `lookahead::simplify` if `sat.lookahead_simplify` → `reinit_assumptions()` → schedule next simplify → optional `anf_simplifier` if `sat.anf` and past `anf.delay` rounds → optional dump-and-exit if `sat.inprocess.out` set.
- **Provenance**: [C]

### `do_simplify` scheduling (when it fires)
- **Where**: `should_simplify` `src/sat/sat_solver.cpp:2058-2060`; next-round computation `2126-2136`
- **Default**: gated by `m_conflicts_since_init >= m_next_simplify`; first threshold `sat.simplify.delay` (default 0) or the **undocumented** hidden param `next_simplify` (default 90000, read via raw `get_uint`, not in `sat_params.pyg`); subsequent thresholds grow as `conflicts_since_init * simplify_mult2` (hidden, default 1.5x) capped by `conflicts_since_init + simplify_max` (hidden, default 1,000,000)
- **Does**: geometric-ish backoff on how often full inprocessing runs, similar in spirit to restart backoff but on a much coarser timescale
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Pre-simplify before bounded search
- **Where**: `src/sat/sat_solver.cpp:1301-1304`
- **Default**: `behind param sat.enable_pre_simplify (default false)`
- **Does**: runs one `do_simplify()` pass before entering `bounded_search`/`search`, i.e. even before the first conflict
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Cap on total inprocessing rounds
- **Where**: `should_cancel` check `src/sat/sat_solver.cpp:1351-1355`
- **Default**: `sat.inprocess.max (default UINT_MAX ⇒ unbounded)`
- **Does**: aborts the whole search (`l_undef`, reason `sat.max.inprocess`) once `m_simplifications` reaches this cap
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Inprocess-and-dump-and-exit
- **Where**: `src/sat/sat_solver.cpp:2143-2148`
- **Default**: `behind param sat.inprocess.out (default empty ⇒ off)`
- **Does**: after one inprocessing pass, dumps the current CNF to the named file and throws to abort — a debugging/benchmarking utility, not part of normal solving
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Subsumption
- **Where**: `simplifier::subsume` `src/sat/sat_simplifier.cpp:851`; back-subsumption helpers `subsumes0`/`subsumes1` at `398`, `515`
- **Default**: `on by default (sat.subsumption, default true)`; `sat.subsumption.limit (100,000,000 literals)`
- **Does**: removes clauses subsumed by a shorter clause (both non-binary-vs-binary via `subsume_with_binaries` at `727`, and general occurrence-list subsumption)
- **Provenance**: [C][P] (standard SAT preprocessing, cf. SatELite)

### Self-subsuming resolution
- **Where**: `subsumes1` `src/sat/sat_simplifier.cpp:398` (returns the literal removable via self-subsumption), applied through `back_subsumption1`/`collect_subsumed1` (`426-511`)
- **Default**: on whenever `sat.subsumption` is on (same pass as subsumption, not independently switched)
- **Does**: when `c1` almost-subsumes `c2` except for one complementary literal, removes that literal from `c2`
- **Provenance**: [C][P] (SatELite-style SSR)

### Binary-clause duplicate elimination
- **Where**: `simplifier::elim_dup_bins` `src/sat/sat_simplifier.cpp:790-826`
- **Default**: part of the standard subsumption pass, no separate flag found
- **Does**: removes duplicate binary clauses, counting each toward `m_num_subsumed`
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Variable elimination / bounded variable elimination (BVE)
- **Where**: entry `simplifier::elim_vars` `src/sat/sat_simplifier.cpp:2083`; per-var attempt `try_eliminate` at `1917`; resolvent counting `elim_var_report`-adjacent code near `1993-2058`
- **Default**: `on by default (sat.elim_vars, default true)`, gated additionally by `elim_vars_enabled()` (`src/sat/sat_simplifier.cpp:113-115`: disabled in incremental mode, when tracking assumptions, or when multi-threaded)
- **Does**: classic DP-style resolution-based variable elimination; cutoffs `resolution.occ_cutoff*`/`resolution.lit_cutoff*`/`resolution.cls_cutoff*` tune how aggressive elimination is based on problem size (see Parameters section)
- **Provenance**: [C][P] (Eén & Biere, SatELite BVE)

### Variable-elimination ordering heuristic
- **Where**: `simplifier::order_vars_for_elim` `src/sat/sat_simplifier.cpp:1759`
- **Default**: always applied when elimination runs
- **Does**: orders candidate variables for BVE (typically by occurrence count) to eliminate cheap variables first
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Asymmetric Tautology Elimination (ATE)
- **Where**: gate `ate_enabled` `src/sat/sat_simplifier.cpp:107`; applied inside `elim_blocked_clauses` dispatch, `src/sat/sat_simplifier.cpp:1057-1059`
- **Default**: `on by default (sat.ate, default true)`; delayed by `sat.bce_delay (default 2 rounds)` — reuses the BCE delay counter
- **Does**: removes clauses that become tautological under unit propagation of their own negated literals (asymmetric tautology detection), a cheap subset of BCE-family checks
- **Provenance**: [C][P] (Järvisalo, Heule, Biere "unhiding"/BCE family)

### Blocked Clause Elimination (BCE)
- **Where**: gate `bce_enabled` `src/sat/sat_simplifier.cpp:108`; entry `elim_blocked_clauses` `1723`; dispatch to BCE proper `1066-1068`
- **Default**: `behind param sat.bce (default false)`; also triggered once at round `sat.bce_at (default 2)` even if `bce` itself is false (see `bce_enabled` combining `m_bce || m_bce_at == m_num_calls || ...`); `sat.bce_delay (default 2)`, `sat.blocked_clause_limit (100,000,000)`
- **Does**: removes clauses containing a literal such that every resolvent on that literal with clauses containing its negation is a tautology
- **Provenance**: [C][P] (Järvisalo, Biere, Heule 2010)

### Asymmetric BCE (ABCE)
- **Where**: gate `abce_enabled` `src/sat/sat_simplifier.cpp:111`; dispatch `1063-1065`
- **Default**: `behind param sat.abce (default false)`
- **Does**: BCE strengthened with asymmetric-literal-addition reasoning (checks blockedness after adding implied literals)
- **Provenance**: [C][P]

### Covered Clause Elimination (CCE) / Asymmetric CCE (ACCE)
- **Where**: gates `cce_enabled`/`acce_enabled` `src/sat/sat_simplifier.cpp:109-110`; dispatch `1054-1062`
- **Default**: `behind params sat.cce (false)`, `sat.acce (false)`
- **Does**: generalizes BCE — a clause is "covered" (removable) if a fixed-point extension procedure over its literals never derives a non-tautological resolvent; ACCE adds asymmetric literals first
- **Provenance**: [C][P] (Heule, Järvisalo, Biere "covered clause elimination")

### Blocked Clause Addition (BCA)
- **Where**: gate `bca_enabled` `src/sat/sat_simplifier.cpp:112`; dispatch `1069-1071`
- **Default**: `behind param sat.bca (default false)`
- **Does**: adds blocked *binary* clauses (the inverse operation of BCE — strengthens the formula with redundant-but-safe clauses)
- **Provenance**: [C][P]

### Retain blocked clauses as lemmas / model reconstruction stack
- **Where**: `sat.retain_blocked_clauses (default true)`; consumer in `model_converter` entry kinds `BCE/CCE/ACCE/ABCE/ATE` `src/sat/sat_model_converter.h:67`
- **Default**: on by default
- **Does**: eliminated/blocked clauses are pushed onto the model-converter elimination stack (`add_elim_stack`) so a model over the reduced formula can be lifted back to the original
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Failed-literal probing
- **Where**: `probing::operator()` `src/sat/sat_probing.cpp:229`; single-literal trial `try_lit` `66`; per-var driver `process`/`process_core` `192`/`123`
- **Default**: `on by default (sat.probing, default true)`; `sat.probing_limit (5,000,000 probe calls)`, `sat.probing_binary (true)`
- **Does**: tentatively assigns each candidate literal, propagates, and if it fails (conflict) asserts its negation; also derives implied binary clauses (double-lookahead style) between surviving probes
- **Provenance**: [C][P] (failed-literal rule, standard SAT preprocessing)

### Probing binary-clause caching
- **Where**: `probing::cache_bins`/`reset_cache` `src/sat/sat_probing.cpp:36-64`
- **Default**: `sat.probing_cache (true)`, `sat.probing_cache_limit (1024, memory-based)`
- **Does**: caches binary implications discovered during probing as lemmas, bounded by an overall memory-usage cap
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Equivalent-literal substitution via SCC (binary implication graph)
- **Where**: `scc::operator()` `src/sat/sat_scc.cpp:225`; root extraction not individually line-checked beyond call site `237` (`extract_roots`)
- **Default**: `on by default (sat.scc, default true)`
- **Does**: computes strongly connected components of the binary-clause implication graph; each SCC's literals are provably equivalent, so all but one representative ("root") are eliminated via `elim_eqs`
- **Provenance**: [C][P] (Van Gelder; standard equivalence-literal-substitution preprocessing)

### Equivalence elimination (`elim_eqs`, the actual substitution engine)
- **Where**: `elim_eqs::operator()` `src/sat/sat_elim_eqs.cpp:277`; clause rewriting `cleanup_clauses` `98`, binary-watch cleanup `cleanup_bin_watches` `42`
- **Default**: invoked unconditionally by SCC when `scc` finds equivalences; also usable standalone
- **Does**: rewrites every clause and watch list to replace each equivalence-class member with its chosen root literal, recording the substitution on the model-converter elimination stack (`save_elim`, `224`)
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Transitive reduction of the binary implication graph
- **Where**: `scc::reduce_tr` `src/sat/sat_scc.cpp:252` and `259`, using `big::reduce_tr` (in `sat_big.cpp`, not individually line-checked here)
- **Default**: `on by default (sat.scc.tr, default true)`, applied only if `m_scc` itself is enabled
- **Does**: removes binary clauses that are redundant given other (longer) implication paths in the BIG
- **Provenance**: [C][P] (transitive-reduction on the implication graph, standard technique)

### Binary Implication Graph (BIG) construction
- **Where**: `big::init` `src/sat/sat_big.cpp:29`; DFS numbering `init_dfs_num` `109`
- **Default**: constructed on demand by consumers (SCC's `reduce_tr`, asymmetric-branch's sampled mode, lookahead)
- **Does**: builds an adjacency structure over binary-clause implications plus DFS pre/post numbers to answer reachability queries used by UHTE/UHLE and transitive reduction
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Asymmetric branching / ALA (Added Literal Analysis)
- **Where**: `asymm_branch::operator()` `src/sat/sat_asymm_branch.cpp:160`; per-clause driver `process(clause&)` `438`; literal-flip trial `flip_literal_at` `355`
- **Default**: `on by default (sat.asymm_branch, default true)`; `sat.asymm_branch.rounds (2)`, `sat.asymm_branch.delay (1)`, `sat.asymm_branch.limit (100,000,000 literals)`
- **Does**: for each clause, tentatively assumes all-but-one literal false and propagates; if a further literal is falsified, it can be dropped from the clause (asymmetric literal addition / ALA), shrinking clauses without changing satisfiability
- **Provenance**: [C][P] (Heule, Järvisalo, Biere "asymmetric branching")

### Unhiding: UHTE (Unhiding Transitive Edge elimination)
- **Where**: `asymm_branch::uhte` `src/sat/sat_asymm_branch.cpp:261`
- **Default**: part of the same `sat.asymm_branch` pass, no separate flag found
- **Does**: uses the BIG's DFS stamps to detect and remove hidden tautologies/transitive edges cheaply, without full propagation
- **Provenance**: [C][P] (Heule, Järvisalo, Biere "Efficient CNF Simplification based on Binary Implication Graphs" — the "unhiding" family, UHTE/UHLE)

### Unhiding: UHLE (Unhiding Literal Elimination)
- **Where**: `asymm_branch::uhle` (two overloads) `src/sat/sat_asymm_branch.cpp:282`, `314`
- **Default**: same pass as UHTE
- **Does**: removes literals from a clause that are provably redundant given BIG reachability (hidden literal elimination), cheaper than a full propagation-based ALA check
- **Provenance**: [C][P] (same "unhiding" paper as UHTE)

### Sampling-based asymmetric branching over the BIG
- **Where**: `asymm_branch::process_sampled` `src/sat/sat_asymm_branch.cpp:427`
- **Default**: `on by default (sat.asymm_branch.sampled, default true)`
- **Does**: samples candidate literals from the BIG rather than exhaustively trying every clause literal, trading completeness for speed; `sat.asymm_branch.all (default false)` switches to exhaustive per-literal branching on every clause instead
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Lookahead-based inprocessing simplification
- **Where**: invoked at `src/sat/sat_solver.cpp:2118-2122`; core `lookahead::simplify` `src/sat/sat_lookahead.cpp:2375`
- **Default**: `behind param sat.lookahead_simplify (default false)`; `sat.lookahead_simplify.bca (default true, only relevant when the pass runs)`
- **Does**: runs one pass of the lookahead solver purely for simplification (deriving units/binaries via lookahead reasoning), not for full search
- **Provenance**: [C]

### AIG-based rewriting/simplification (finder + ANF pipeline)
- **Where**: `aig_finder::operator()` `src/sat/sat_aig_finder.cpp:25`; consumed from `anf_simplifier::compile_aigs` `src/sat/sat_anf_simplifier.cpp:330`
- **Default**: only reachable through `sat.anf (default false)`; delay `sat.anf.delay (default 2 rounds)`
- **Does**: detects AND/ITE gate structure in the clause set and compiles it into the ANF/PDD representation for algebraic simplification
- **Provenance**: [C]

### XOR extraction
- **Where**: `xor_finder::operator()` `src/sat/sat_xor_finder.cpp:27`; extraction core `extract_xor` `56`; consumed from `anf_simplifier::compile_xors` `src/sat/sat_anf_simplifier.cpp:301`
- **Default**: reachable only via `sat.anf (default false)`
- **Does**: detects clause patterns encoding an XOR/parity constraint and extracts them for algebraic (Gröbner/ANF-style) handling
- **Provenance**: [C]

### ANF (Algebraic Normal Form) simplification
- **Where**: `anf_simplifier::operator()` `src/sat/sat_anf_simplifier.cpp:43`; invoked from `sat_solver.cpp:2145-2151`
- **Default**: `behind param sat.anf (default false)`; `sat.anf.delay (2)`, `sat.anf.exlin (false, extended-linear simplification sub-option)`
- **Does**: converts a subset of clauses to ANF/polynomial form (via a PDD solver), simplifies algebraically, and translates results back to clauses/phase hints (`anf2clauses`, `anf2phase`)
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Blocked Clause Decomposition (BCD, candidate-equivalence finder)
- **Where**: `bcd::operator()` `src/sat/sat_bcd.cpp:55`,`64`
- **Default**: **not reachable from any production code path** — see Dead/unreachable section
- **Does**: per its own header comment, finds candidate equivalent literals via blocked-clause decomposition (Biere & Heule, LPAR-19)
- **Provenance**: [C][P] — code exists and is well-formed, but see dead-code note

### NPN3 gate finder (mux/maj/or-and/xor 3-input gate patterns)
- **Where**: `npn3_finder::operator()` `src/sat/sat_npn3_finder.cpp:63`; per-gate finders `find_mux`(208)/`find_maj`(238)/`find_orand`(282)/`find_xor`(311)
- **Default**: **not reachable from any production code path** — see Dead/unreachable section
- **Does**: identifies clause patterns matching common 3-input NPN-equivalence-class gates (MUX, majority, OR-AND, XOR) for structural rewriting
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Cleaner (unit propagation / tautology cleanup)
- **Where**: `m_cleaner(...)` called at `src/sat/sat_solver.cpp:2089` and inside `simplifier::initialize` (`sat_simplifier.cpp:175`); implementation in `sat_cleaner.cpp` (outside the explicitly-named file list, but in `src/sat/`)
- **Default**: `sat.force_cleanup (default false)` controls whether cleanup is forced even without pending work; the pass itself always runs at the top of every simplify/inprocess cycle
- **Does**: removes satisfied clauses, falsified literals, and level-0-implied tautologies before other inprocessing passes run
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Parallel share/exchange during inprocessing
- **Where**: `src/sat/sat_solver.cpp:2141-2144` (`m_par->from_solver`/`to_solver`)
- **Default**: only active when `sat.threads > 1` (see Alternative-search section)
- **Does**: after each inprocessing round, exchanges learned clauses/units with sibling portfolio solvers
- **Provenance**: [C]

## Alternative search

### Lookahead solver — core search
- **Where**: `lookahead::search` `src/sat/sat_lookahead.cpp:2056`; variable choice `choose`/`choose_base` `2351`/`2355`
- **Default**: activated for cube generation and via `sat.lookahead_simplify`; not the default top-level search strategy
- **Does**: full lookahead-based DPLL-style search with autarky/preselection pruning, used both to generate cubes and (optionally) as the simplification engine
- **Provenance**: [C][P] (March-style lookahead solving, Heule & van Maaren)

### Lookahead preselection
- **Where**: `lookahead::pre_select` `src/sat/sat_lookahead.cpp:209`
- **Default**: `behind param sat.lookahead.preselect (default false)`
- **Does**: restricts the candidate set of branching variables considered at each lookahead node, to control cost
- **Provenance**: [C][P]

### Lookahead reward functions
- **Where**: dispatch `init_pre_selection` `src/sat/sat_lookahead.cpp:487-526`; implementations `heule_schur_scores`(528), `heule_unit_scores`(561), `march_cu_scores`(583)
- **Default**: `sat.lookahead.reward = march_cu (default)`; alternatives `ternary`, `heule_schur`, `heuleu`, `unit` (see `sat_params.pyg` doc block for exact formulas)
- **Does**: scores each literal by a reward heuristic (ternary-clause-based, Heule-Schur, Heule-unit, or March's default `march_cu`) to pick the next lookahead branching variable
- **Provenance**: [C][P] (March solver reward heuristics, Heule/Knuth 2018 "Schur Number 5" AAAI paper for `heule_schur`)

### Double lookahead
- **Where**: `lookahead::double_look` `src/sat/sat_lookahead.cpp:1901`; toggled at `1885-1899`
- **Default**: `on by default (sat.lookahead.double, default true)`; intensity via `sat.lookahead.delta_fraction (1.0)`
- **Does**: on promising branches, performs a second level of lookahead propagation to find more units before committing
- **Provenance**: [C][P] (March "double lookahead")

### Lookahead SCC (on the fly, within lookahead propagation)
- **Where**: `lookahead::get_scc`/`init_scc`/`found_scc` `src/sat/sat_lookahead.cpp:646,656,758`
- **Default**: internal to the lookahead engine, always active when lookahead runs
- **Does**: detects equivalent-literal SCCs during lookahead propagation to strengthen unit derivation
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Lookahead global autarky preference
- **Where**: `src/sat/sat_lookahead.cpp:321` (`get_config().m_lookahead_global_autarky`)
- **Default**: `behind param sat.lookahead.global_autarky (default false)`
- **Does**: prefers branching on variables that occur in clauses reduced by the current partial assignment (autarky-guided variable choice)
- **Provenance**: [C][P]

### Cube generation (cube-and-conquer front end)
- **Where**: `lookahead::cube` `src/sat/sat_lookahead.cpp:2145`
- **Default**: cutoff strategy `sat.lookahead.cube.cutoff = depth (default)`, options `depth/freevars/psat/adaptive_freevars/adaptive_psat`; `sat.lookahead.cube.depth (1)`, `sat.lookahead.cube.fraction (0.4)`, `sat.lookahead.cube.freevars (0.8)`, `sat.lookahead.cube.psat.*`
- **Does**: recursively splits the formula into cubes (partial assignments) up to a cutoff criterion, for cube-and-conquer style parallel/incremental solving
- **Provenance**: [C][P] (Heule, Kullmann, Wieringa, Biere "cube and conquer")

### `lookahead_scores` DIMACS utility
- **Where**: param `sat.lookahead_scores (default false)`, doc string: "utility that can only be used from the DIMACS front-end"
- **Default**: off by default, and only meaningful from the DIMACS CLI path, not the general API
- **Does**: exposes raw lookahead reward scores for external analysis/tuning
- **Provenance**: [C] (wiring only, did not trace consumer)

### DDFW (Divide-and-Distribute Fixed Weights) local search — wrapper
- **Where**: wrapper `ddfw_wrapper::check` `src/sat/sat_ddfw_wrapper.cpp:16`; real algorithm lives **outside `src/sat/`**, in `src/ast/sls/sat_ddfw.cpp`
- **Default**: `behind param sat.ddfw_search (default false)`; also the default engine for `sat.phase=local_search`-triggered rephasing (`sat_solver.cpp:1391`); `sat.ddfw.init_clause_weight (8)`, `sat.ddfw.use_reward_pct (15)`, `sat.ddfw.restart_base (100000)`, `sat.ddfw.reinit_base (10000)`, `sat.ddfw.threads (0)`
- **Does**: weighted local search over clause weights, redistributing weight from satisfied to unsatisfied clauses on a flip (DDFW algorithm)
- **Provenance**: [C][P] (Ishtaiwi, Thornton, Anbulagan, Sattar, Pham "Adaptive clause weight redistribution")

### WalkSAT-style local search
- **Where**: `local_search::walksat` `src/sat/sat_local_search.cpp:434`; flip selection `pick_flip_walksat` `562`, `pick_flip_lookahead` `537`
- **Default**: `behind param sat.local_search (default false)`; mode `sat.local_search_mode = wsat (default)`; **note**: doc string says the alternative mode is `qsat` but the code (`sat_config.cpp:100-104`) only special-cases the literal `"gsat"` (falling back to `wsat` for anything else, including `"qsat"`) — the enum in `sat_config.h:82-84` is literally `{gsat, wsat}`. Passing `sat.local_search_mode=qsat` as documented silently does nothing (falls through to wsat). Doc/code mismatch.
- **Does**: WalkSAT-family flip search with pseudo-boolean/cardinality constraint support (`add_pb`, `add_cardinality`)
- **Provenance**: [C][I] (mismatch confirmed by direct code read, not inferred)

### ProbSAT-style local search (`sat_prob.cpp`, the file the brief marked "(?)")
- **Where**: `prob::check` `src/sat/sat_prob.cpp:32`; flip `prob::flip` `45`/`71`; variable pick `pick_var` `53`
- **Default**: `behind param sat.prob_search (default false)`
- **Does**: ProbSAT-style probabilistic local search (break-count-weighted variable selection), a separate engine from both WalkSAT (`sat_local_search.cpp`) and DDFW
- **Provenance**: [C][P] (Balint & Schöning, ProbSAT)

### Local-search thread pool
- **Where**: config `sat.local_search_threads (default 0)`; consumer `src/sat/sat_solver.cpp` around `do_local_search`/`invoke_local_search` (`1463`, `1443`)
- **Default**: off (0 threads) by default
- **Does**: runs multiple local-search workers in parallel looking for a satisfying assignment while (optionally) CDCL search proceeds
- **Provenance**: [C] (wiring), did not trace full concurrency model

### Parallel / portfolio solving
- **Where**: `class parallel` `src/sat/sat_parallel.cpp` (`init_solvers` `101`, `share_clause` `145`/`158`, `exchange` `125`)
- **Default**: `sat.threads (default 1)`; `sat.ddfw.threads (default 0)` for DDFW-specific parallel workers
- **Does**: portfolio parallel solving — multiple solver instances share learned clauses/units through a shared clause-vector pool (`vector_pool`)
- **Provenance**: [C][P] (standard portfolio/clause-sharing parallel SAT, cf. Plingeling/Treengeling lineage in spirit)

### Bounded local search seeding from CDCL (`bounded_local_search`)
- **Where**: `src/sat/sat_solver.cpp:1376-1442`
- **Default**: invoked from `PS_LOCAL_SEARCH` rephasing (`sat.phase=local_search`) and from `check()` when `sat.phase=local_search` and an extension is present (`1310-1313`)
- **Does**: runs a resource-limited local-search sub-solve (DDFW by default, or `m_ext->local_search` if an extension provides one) purely to obtain a better phase, then discards the local-search state and resumes CDCL
- **Provenance**: [C]

## Proofs and cores

### DRAT/DRUP proof emission
- **Where**: `drat::dump` `src/sat/sat_drat.cpp:65`; binary variant `bdump` `129`; top-level `add`/`append` overloads `627-` and `182,200,232`
- **Default**: `m_drat` computed at `sat_config.cpp:189-196` — true when `sat.threads==1` **and** (SMT lemma logging OR `sat.drat.check_unsat` OR `sat.drat.file` set OR SMT proof-log path set OR `sat.smt.proof.check` OR `sat.drat.check_sat`); `sat.drat.disable (default false)` force-overrides everything off; `sat.drat.file` (empty by default), `sat.drat.binary (false)`
- **Does**: emits a DRAT (or DRUP) proof trace of every added/deleted clause during solving, in ASCII or binary format
- **Provenance**: [C][P] (Heule, Hunt, Wetzler DRAT format)

### DRAT proof logging is disabled under multi-threading
- **Where**: `sat_config.cpp:189` (`p.threads() == 1` conjunct)
- **Default**: structural — `sat.threads > 1` silently disables DRAT regardless of the other flags
- **Does**: portfolio/parallel mode cannot produce a checkable proof trace with this build
- **Provenance**: [C]

### On-the-fly DRAT/DRUP checking during search
- **Where**: `drat::verify` `src/sat/sat_drat.cpp:402`; DRUP check `is_drup` `306`/`329`; DRAT check `is_drat` `353`/`380`
- **Default**: `behind param sat.drat.check_unsat (default false)`
- **Does**: as each learned clause is added, attempts to verify it is DRUP-implied (unit-propagation check) or DRAT-implied (resolution-with-negation check) against the current clause database *during* the search itself, not just as a post-hoc external check; on failure hits `UNREACHABLE()` (i.e., treated as an internal bug, not a soft failure)
- **Provenance**: [C][P]

### Model checking on the fly during SAT search
- **Where**: `sat.drat.check_sat (default false)`, consumed inside `drat::contains` `src/sat/sat_drat.cpp:436` gating on `m_check_sat`
- **Default**: off by default
- **Does**: builds up an internal trace and checks the satisfying model against it
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Variable-activity dump alongside DRAT
- **Where**: `sat.drat.activity (default false)`; `drat::dump_activity` `src/sat/sat_drat.cpp:122`
- **Default**: off
- **Does**: dumps VSIDS/branching activities into the proof stream, presumably for replaying solver behavior, not for checking correctness
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Proof trimming (LRAT-style trim/replay)
- **Where**: `proof_trim::trim` `src/sat/sat_proof_trim.cpp:30`; conflict-driven core extraction `conflict_analysis_core` `176`; trail pruning `prune_trail` `85`
- **Default**: standalone utility class, not wired into the default `sat.*` search-time flow — invoked externally (e.g. tooling/CLI), did not trace every caller
- **Does**: replays a DRAT/LRAT-style proof backward, keeping only clauses whose deletion would break the derivation (proof minimization/trimming), producing a smaller justified proof
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Minimal Unsat Core extraction — deletion-based (MUS1)
- **Where**: `mus::mus1` `src/sat/sat_mus.cpp:57`
- **Default**: entry point `mus::operator()` `47` always calls `mus1` (no alternate dispatch to `mus2` observed at the top level in this pass — worth a closer look if MUS work matters)
- **Does**: iteratively drops assumptions from the current unsat core and re-checks SAT to shrink it to a minimal unsatisfiable subset
- **Provenance**: [C][P] (standard deletion-based MUS extraction)

### `mus2` (present but not observed dispatched from `operator()`)
- **Where**: `mus::mus2` `src/sat/sat_mus.cpp:137`
- **Default**: did not verify a caller of `mus2` outside its own file in this pass — flagging for the dead-code check below rather than asserting it's unreachable
- **Does**: per name, an alternate MUS-extraction strategy
- **Provenance**: [I] (function exists; reachability not fully confirmed)

### QuickXplain-style MUS support (`qx`)
- **Where**: `mus::qx` `src/sat/sat_mus.cpp:147`
- **Default**: did not verify caller
- **Does**: per name, QuickXplain-style divide-and-conquer conflict/core minimization
- **Provenance**: [P][I] (QuickXplain algorithm, Junker) — existence confirmed, integration not traced

### Core minimization (partial)
- **Where**: `sat.core.minimize (default false)`, `sat.core.minimize_partial (default false)`; consumed in `mus::operator()` `src/sat/sat_mus.cpp:27-28` (`m_max_num_restarts` capped when partial minimize is on)
- **Default**: off by default; when on, `core.minimize_partial` bounds the minimization to ~10 restarts' worth of work rather than running to completion
- **Does**: trades MUS-extraction completeness for bounded cost
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Model converter — elimination-stack replay
- **Where**: `model_converter::operator()` `src/sat/sat_model_converter.cpp:61`; stack processing `process_stack` `45`; entry kinds `ELIM_VAR, BCE, CCE, ACCE, ABCE, ATE` `src/sat/sat_model_converter.h:67`
- **Default**: always active — every model returned by the solver goes through this to lift a model over the simplified formula back to the original
- **Does**: replays, in reverse order, every variable-elimination/blocked-clause/covered-clause/equivalence step recorded during inprocessing, reconstructing values for eliminated variables so the model satisfies the *original* clause set
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Model converter self-check
- **Where**: `solver::mk_model` `src/sat/sat_solver.cpp:2185-2245`, guarded by `m_clone`/`m_config.m_drat` (`m_drat.check_model` at `2219`)
- **Default**: only runs when a clone solver exists (debug/check mode) or when DRAT is active
- **Does**: re-checks the lifted model against the clause set (and, if cloned, against the pre-simplification clause set too), throwing `solver_exception("check model failed")` on mismatch
- **Provenance**: [C]

Transcribed verbatim from `src/params/sat_params.pyg` (main file) plus the four sibling `.pyg`
files under `src/sat/` that are pulled in by the classes in this area. All defaults as literally
written in the source at HEAD `e18d63b`.

### `src/params/sat_params.pyg` (module `sat`)
| name | default | one line |
|---|---|---|

| phase.sticky | true | use sticky phase caching |
| search.unsat.conflicts | 400 | period for solving for unsat (in number of conflicts) |
| search.sat.conflicts | 400 | period for solving for sat (in number of conflicts) |
| rephase.base | 1000 | number of conflicts per rephase |
| reorder.base | UINT_MAX | number of conflicts per random reorder |
| reorder.itau | 4.0 | inverse temperature for softmax |
| reorder.activity_scale | 100 | scaling factor for activity update |
| propagate.prefetch | true | prefetch watch lists for assigned literals |
| restart | `ema` | restart strategy: static, luby, ema or geometric |
| restart.initial | 2 | initial restart (number of conflicts) |
| restart.max | UINT_MAX | maximal number of restarts |
| restart.fast | true | use fast restart approach only removing less active literals |
| restart.factor | 1.5 | restart increment factor for geometric strategy |
| restart.margin | 1.1 | margin between fast and slow restart factors (EMA) |
| restart.emafastglue | 3e-2 | ema alpha factor for fast moving average |
| restart.emaslowglue | 1e-5 | ema alpha factor for slow moving average |

## Dead or unreachable

### `sat_bcd.cpp` / `class bcd` — compiled, never instantiated
- **Where**: `src/sat/sat_bcd.cpp` (whole file), built via `src/sat/CMakeLists.txt:7`; only external reference anywhere in the tree is the `friend class bcd;` declaration in `src/sat/sat_solver.h:218`
- **Verification**: `grep -rn "sat::bcd\|sat_bcd.h\"" src/` (excluding `sat_bcd.*` itself) returns nothing — no `#include "sat/sat_bcd.h"` outside its own `.cpp`, no construction site found anywhere, including tests
- **Does**: blocked-clause-decomposition candidate-equivalence finder (per its header doc, Biere & Heule LPAR-19), fully implemented but orphaned
- **Provenance**: [C][I] (absence confirmed by grep across the whole `src/` tree, not just `src/sat/`)

### `sat_npn3_finder.cpp` / `class npn3_finder` — reachable only from a test
- **Where**: `src/sat/sat_npn3_finder.cpp`, built via `CMakeLists.txt:23`; sole external user is `src/test/finder.cpp` (a unit test), plus the `friend class npn3_finder;` in `sat_solver.h:237`
- **Verification**: `anf_simplifier` (the pass that hosts `xor_finder` and `aig_finder` in production, `sat_anf_simplifier.cpp:22-23`) does **not** include or use `npn3_finder` at all — confirmed by grep of both `#include` and identifier usage in that file
- **Does**: NPN-equivalence-class gate detection (mux/maj/or-and/xor); implemented and unit-tested, but never wired into any solving/simplification pass that a user's query would hit

### `do_reorder`'s uniform-shuffle alternative — compiled out
- **Where**: `src/sat/sat_solver.cpp:3072` `#if 1` / `3096` `#else` / `3100` `#endif`, inside `solver::do_reorder`

### `restart_level`'s "pop trail from top" branch — compiled out

# Part 4 — `src/sat/smt/`: the second SMT core

## `src/sat/smt/` — the second SMT core (`sat.euf`)

Z3 contains a **complete second SMT solver**, built as a set of theory plugins
over the CDCL SAT core, structurally unrelated to `src/smt/`. It duplicates
arithmetic, bit-vectors, arrays, datatypes, FPA, quantifiers, recursive
functions, PB, and special relations.

### When does `sat.euf` run at all?
- **Where**: switch at `src/tactic/portfolio/smt_strategic_solver.cpp:131`–`:137`; internalizer gate at `src/sat/tactic/goal2sat.cpp:96` (`m_euf = sp.euf() || sp.smt()`); params at `src/params/sat_params.pyg:50` (`smt`, BOOL, False) and `:63` (`euf`, BOOL, False, *"this feature is preliminary and not ready for general consumption"*).
- **Answer**: **no SMT-LIB logic gets it by default.** It is reachable only by setting `sat.euf=true` or `sat.smt=true`, or through the `fd_solver` / `sat` tactic paths that construct `mk_inc_sat_solver` and then internalize non-Boolean atoms. The exception worth naming: `QF_BV`'s *incremental* solver is `mk_inc_sat_solver` by default (`smt_strategic_solver.cpp:144`, because `hi_div0` defaults true), and `goal2sat` will instantiate the EUF extension there if the goal is not purely propositional.
- **Provenance**: `[C]`

### `euf::solver` — the core
- **Where**: `src/sat/smt/euf_solver.cpp:44`; theory dispatch `get_solver` at `:120`
- **Default**: only under `sat.euf` / `sat.smt`
- **Does**: owns the e-graph, dispatches internalization by family id, and plays the role `smt::context` plays in the classic core.
- **Provenance**: `[C]`

### Theory-plugin registry (exactly which theories the EUF core has)
- **Where**: `src/sat/smt/euf_solver.cpp:138`–`:161`
- **Does**: `pb::solver` (`:139`), `bv::solver` (`:142`), `intblast::solver` (`:146`), `array::solver` (`:151`), `fpa::solver` (`:153`), `arith::solver` (`:155`), `dt::solver` (`:157`), `recfun::solver` (`:159`), `specrel::solver` (`:161`); `q::solver` for quantifiers at `:114`; `sls::solver` added in `init_search` at `:192` when `m_sls_enable`; `user_solver::solver` at `:1273`.
- **The gap this makes visible**: **there is no sequence/string theory plugin.** `src/sat/smt/` has no `seq_solver`. Strings, regexes and sequences exist ONLY in the classic `src/smt/theory_seq.cpp`. An unhandled family id falls to `unhandled_function` (`:180`). `[C]`
- **Provenance**: `[C]`

### `sat.bv_solver` — three bit-vector engines, one of which throws
- **Where**: `src/sat/smt/euf_solver.cpp:141`–`:148`; param `smt.bv.solver` UINT default 0 at `src/params/smt_params_helper.pyg:63`
- **Does**: `0` = bit-blasting (`bv::solver`), `1` = **polysat — `throw default_exception("polysat solver is not integrated")`**, `2` = intblast (`intblast::solver`, translate BV to unbounded integers). Value 1 is a live, documented, user-settable parameter whose only implementation is a throw.
- **Provenance**: `[C]`

### `intblast::solver` — bit-vectors as integers
- **Where**: `src/sat/smt/intblast_solver.cpp:29`; translation `:378`; bound axioms `:99`; predicate axioms `:118`; bv2int axioms `:136`; sub-solver check `:171`, `:240`
- **Default**: `smt.bv.solver=2`, requires `sat.smt=true`
- **Does**: translates bit-vector constraints into unbounded integer arithmetic with modular bound axioms and discharges them on a nested solver, instead of bit-blasting.
- **Provenance**: `[C]`

### `bv::solver` — lazy/delayed bit-vector internalization
- **Where**: `src/sat/smt/bv_delay_internalize.cpp:23` (`check_delay_internalized`), `:45` (`should_bit_blast`), `:81` (`check_lazy_mul`), `:124` (`check_mul`), `:196` (`check_mul_invertibility`), `:232` (`check_mul_zero`), `:276` (`check_mul_one`), `:335` (`check_umul_no_overflow`), `:300`/`:316` (`encode_msb_tail`/`encode_lsb_tail`)
- **Default**: `smt.bv.delay` BOOL False (`smt_params_helper.pyg:61`)
- **Does**: refuses to bit-blast multiplication and overflow predicates up front; instead evaluates them against the candidate model and adds invertibility / zero / one / no-overflow lemmas lazily. This is a *distinct procedure*, not an optimization: the blasted circuit is never built for a `bvmul` that the model already satisfies.
- **Provenance**: `[C]`

### Named BV internalization procedures
- **Where**, all `src/sat/smt/bv_internalize.cpp`: `internalize_circuit` `:156`, `internalize_num` `:389`, `internalize_mkbv` `:410`, `internalize_bv2int` `:416` + `assert_bv2int_axiom` `:425`, `internalize_int2bv` `:445` + `assert_int2bv_axiom` `:468`, `internalize_le` `:495`, `internalize_carry` `:511`, `internalize_xor3` `:525`, `internalize_udiv_i` `:541` + `assert_bv_divrem_bound_axiom` `:550`, `internalize_overflow` `:638`, `internalize_concat` `:734`, `internalize_sub` `:744`, `internalize_extract` `:755`, `register_true_false_bit` `:299`, `propagate_bits` (`bv_solver.cpp:585`).
- **Default**: only under `sat.euf`/`sat.smt`
- **Provenance**: `[C]`

### `bv_ackerman` / `euf_ackerman` — dynamic Ackermannization inside the EUF core
- **Where**: `src/sat/smt/euf_ackerman.cpp:95` (`enable_cc`), `:109` (`enable_eq`), `:115` (`cg_conflict_eh`), `:132` (`used_eq_eh`), `:144` (`used_cc_eh`), `:156` (`gc`), `:170` (`propagate`), `:191` (`add_cc`), `:212` (`add_eq`); `src/sat/smt/bv_ackerman.cpp` for the BV-specific variant
- **Default**: did not verify (the classic-core analogue is `smt.dack`, default 1)
- **Does**: watches which congruences participate in conflicts and lazily instantiates Leibniz/congruence axioms for the frequent ones, with its own garbage collection.
- **Provenance**: `[C]`

### Arithmetic axiom families (EUF core)
- **Where**, all `src/sat/smt/arith_axioms.cpp`: `mk_div_axiom` `:25`, `mk_to_int_axiom` `:34`, `mk_abs_axiom` `:52`, `mk_power0_axioms` `:61`, `mk_power_axiom` `:69`, `mk_is_int_axiom` `:94`, `mk_idiv_mod_axioms` `:103`, `mk_rem_axiom` `:223`, `mk_bv_axiom` `:354` (BV terms inside arithmetic, gate `check_bv_term` `:236` / `check_bv_terms` `:343`), `mk_bound_axioms` `:401`, `add_farkas_clause` `:447`, `mk_bound_axiom` `:458`, `mk_diseq_axiom` `:585`, `assert_idiv_mod_axioms` `:630`, `check_idiv_bounds` `:656`, `fixed_var_eh` `:723`.
- **Default**: only under `sat.euf`/`sat.smt`
- **Provenance**: `[C]`

### Arithmetic propagation procedures (EUF core)
- **Where**, `src/sat/smt/arith_solver.cpp`: `propagate_basic_bounds` `:121`, `propagate_bound` `:137`, `propagate_bounds_with_lp_solver` `:212`, `propagate_lp_solver_bound` `:230`, `is_bound_implied` `:274`, `refine_bound` `:370`, `propagate_eqs` `:428`, `flush_bound_axioms` `:470`, `report_equality_of_fixed_vars` `:729`, `get_phase` `:841` (theory-driven phase hint into the SAT solver).
- **Provenance**: `[C]`

### Array axiom families (EUF core)
- **Where**, all `src/sat/smt/array_axioms.cpp`: `assert_default` `:77`, `assert_select` `:92`, `assert_store_axiom` `:132`, `assert_select_store_axiom` `:151`, `assert_select_const_axiom` `:238`, `assert_extensionality` `:256`, `assert_select_map_axiom` `:284`, `assert_select_as_array_axiom` `:322`, `assert_default_map_axiom` `:367`, `assert_default_const_axiom` `:382`, `assert_default_store_axiom` `:401`, `assert_select_lambda_axiom` `:457`, `assert_choice_axiom` `:471`, `assert_congruent_axiom` `:495`, `has_unitary_domain` `:521`, `has_large_domain` `:533`, `add_delayed_axioms` `:565`, `add_as_array_eqs` `:604`, `add_interface_equalities` `:628`, `check_lambdas` `:684`; upward-propagation policy `array_solver.cpp:248`–`:281`.
- **Note**: `assert_select_lambda_axiom` and `check_lambdas` mean this array solver handles **lambda terms as arrays** — beta-reduction is a first-class array operation here (`is_beta_redex`, `euf_internalize.cpp:436`). `[C]`
- **Provenance**: `[C]`

### Datatype solver (EUF core)
- **Where**, `src/sat/smt/dt_solver.cpp`: `assert_eq_axiom` `:104`, `assert_is_constructor_axiom` `:121`, `assert_accessor_axioms` `:141`, `sign_recognizer_conflict` `:157`, `assert_update_field_axioms` `:177`, `mk_split` `:240`, `mk_enum_split` `:279`, `propagate_recognizer` `:412`, and the **occurs check** for acyclicity: `oc_mark_on_stack` `:83`, `oc_mark_cycle_free` `:89`, `occurs_check_explain` `:600`, `occurs_check_enter` `:624`, `occurs_check` `:687`.
- **Default**: only under `sat.euf`/`sat.smt`; laziness governed by `smt.dt_lazy_splits` (UINT 1, `smt_params_helper.pyg:163`)
- **Provenance**: `[C]`

### Quantifier machinery (EUF core, `q_*`)
- **`q::solver`** — `src/sat/smt/q_solver.cpp:32`; `instantiate` `:122`, `skolemize` `:143`, `specialize` `:154`, `flatten` `:179`, `expand` `:244` (miniscoping/expansion), unit inference `init_units` `:208` / `get_unit` `:223`.
- **`q::ematch`** — `src/sat/smt/q_ematch.cpp:52`; `nnf_skolem` `:147`, `on_merge` `:193`, `add_watch` `:216`, `init_watch` `:223`/`:244`, `alloc_binding` `:293`, `on_binding` `:327`, `propagate` `:345`/`:380`, `instantiate` `:409`/`:425`, `clausify` `:489`, `clausify_literal` `:521`. Note this core **clausifies quantifiers** before e-matching, which the classic core does not.
- **`q::eval`** — `src/sat/smt/q_eval.cpp:39`, `:79`; `compare` `:84`, `compare_rec` `:142`. Evaluates a clause under a binding to decide whether an instance is useful before creating it.
- **`q::queue`** — `src/sat/smt/q_queue.cpp:27`; `get_cost` `:111`, `get_new_gen` `:118`, `propagate` `:182`, `lazy_propagate` `:221`. Cost-function-driven instantiation queue (`smt.qi.cost`, default `(+ weight generation)`).
- **`q::mbqi`** — `src/sat/smt/q_mbi.cpp:34`; `check_forall` `:153`, `check_forall_default` `:196`, `check_forall_subst` `:207`, `restrict_to_universe` `:90`, `replace_model_value` `:113`, `choose_term` `:135`, `solver_project` `:312`, `add_domain_eqs` `:386`, `add_domain_bounds` `:447`, `eliminate_nested_vars` `:479`, `quick_check` `:555`.
- **`q::model_fixer`** — `src/sat/smt/q_model_fixer.cpp:60`; `add_projection_functions` `:113`/`:132`, `add_projection_function` `:175`, `collect_partial_functions` `:241`, `invert_app` `:252`, `invert_arg` `:275`, `restrict_arg` `:328`. Builds finite projections so a model satisfies the quantifiers.
- **Default**: all only under `sat.euf`/`sat.smt`
- **Provenance**: `[C]`

### `recfun::solver` — recursive function unfolding with guard blocking
- **Where**: `src/sat/smt/recfun_solver.cpp:29`; `assert_macro_axiom` `:66`, `assert_case_axioms` `:86`, `assert_guard` `:122`, `block_core` `:129`, `disable_guard` `:140`, `assert_body_axiom` `:161`, `add_assumptions` `:282`, `should_research` `:299`
- **Does**: unfolds `define-fun-rec` bodies lazily under assumption literals, and re-searches when the core contains a guard.
- **Provenance**: `[C]`

### `specrel::solver` — special relations
- **Where**: `src/sat/smt/specrel_solver.cpp:28`
- **Logics / entry point**: API-only: `Z3_mk_partial_order`, `Z3_mk_linear_order`, `Z3_mk_tree_order`, `Z3_mk_piecewise_linear_order`, `Z3_mk_transitive_closure` (`src/api/z3_api.h`)
- **Does**: decides partial/linear/tree/piecewise-linear orders and transitive closure as a theory rather than by axiom instantiation.
- **Provenance**: `[C]`

### `user_solver::solver` — the user propagator
- **Where**: `src/sat/smt/user_solver.cpp`; constructed at `src/sat/smt/euf_solver.cpp:1273`
- **Logics / entry point**: API only (`Z3_solver_propagate_*`)
- **Does**: lets an external program register fixed/eq/final callbacks and inject clauses — a programmable theory solver.
- **Provenance**: `[C]`

### `sls::solver` — local search as a co-processor to CDCL(T)
- **Where**: `src/sat/smt/sls_solver.cpp:115`; added in `euf_solver.cpp:192`
- **Default**: `smt.sls.enable` BOOL **False** (`src/params/smt_params_helper.pyg:156`); `smt.sls.parallel` BOOL True (`:157`)
- **Does**: runs the `src/ast/sls/` local-search engine alongside the systematic search, exchanging phases, values and units in both directions.
- **Provenance**: `[C]`

### Proof checkers inside the EUF core
- **Where**: `src/sat/smt/euf_proof_checker.cpp:217` (plugin registry), `src/sat/smt/arith_theory_checker.h`, `bv_theory_checker.cpp`, `q_theory_checker.cpp`, `tseitin_theory_checker.cpp`, `distinct_theory_checker.h`; emission in `euf_proof.cpp` (`on_clause` `:370`, `on_lemma` `:423`, `on_instantiation` `:441`, `log_rup` `:130`)
- **Default**: behind `sat.drat.*` / `smt.clause_proof`
- **Does**: per-theory checkers that validate the hints attached to emitted lemmas — Z3's route to a checkable proof for non-propositional reasoning.
- **Provenance**: `[C]`

### `xor_solver` — present in the tree, excluded from the build
- **Where**: `src/sat/smt/xor_solver.cpp`, `.h`, and a stray `src/sat/smt/xor_solver.d`
- **Default**: **dead.** `xor_solver.cpp` does not appear in `src/sat/smt/CMakeLists.txt` (only `bv_solver.cpp` matched a `grep -n "xor_solver\|bv_solver"` of that file), nothing in `src/` references `xr::solver`, and `src/sat/sat_config.cpp:251` hardcodes `m_xor_solver = false; // prevent users from playing with this option` — overriding the `cardinality_solver`-adjacent parameter unconditionally.
- **Does**: would decide XOR constraints by Gaussian elimination.
- **Provenance**: `[C]`

# Part 5 — `src/ast/simplifiers/` and `src/ast/rewriter/`: preprocessing


Repo: `references/z3`, HEAD `e18d63b`. Read-only inventory, area = PREPROCESSING
(`src/ast/simplifiers/`, `src/ast/rewriter/`, `src/ast/normal_forms/`,
`src/ast/macros/`, plus how a pass gets wired into a pipeline). All line
numbers below are from `grep -n` against this checkout unless the entry says
"did not verify".

## Registration mechanism — how a pass reaches a query [C]

Two independent registries exist, and they answer different questions.

1. **Tactic/simplifier-combinator registry** — a header calls the
   `ADD_SIMPLIFIER(name, help, ctor-expr)` macro (see
   `src/cmd_context/simplifier_cmds.h:28-40`, `src/api/api_tactic.cpp:533`).
   Every hit is a name reachable from `(simplify ...)` in the SMT-LIB front
   end, `Z3_mk_simplifier`, or `and-then`/`using-params` combinators
   (`src/cmd_context/simplifier_cmds.cpp:29-74`). Full list, `grep -rn
   "ADD_SIMPLIFIER" src`, 38 hits:

   `bit2int` `bit-blast` `bv1-blast` `cheap-fourier-motzkin` `elim-term-ite`
   `lambda-macros` `leibniz-instantiation` `max-bv-sharing`
   `pull-nested-quantifiers` `push-app-ite-conservative` `push-app-ite`
   `ng-push-app-ite-conservative` `ng-push-app-ite` `randomizer`
   `refine-injectivity` `simplify` `qe-light` `card2bv` `factor`
   `propagate-ineqs` `bvarray2uf` `propagate-bv-bounds` `bv-divrem-bounds`
   `bv-slice` `blast-term-ite` `cofactor-term-ite` `demodulator` `der`
   `distribute-forall` `dom-simplify` `elim-predicates` `elim-unconstrained`
   `fold-unfold` `injectivity` `propagate-values` `reduce-args` `solve-eqs`
   `special-relations` `euf-completion`.

   Four of these (`special-relations`, `bvarray2uf`, `cofactor-term-ite`,
   `qe-light`) are *registered* in `src/tactic/**` / `src/qe/lite/**` but
   their pass classes live outside `src/ast/{simplifiers,rewriter}` —
   out of scope for this inventory, noted here only for completeness of the
   registry.

2. **`init_preprocess` — the actual default pipeline** at
   `src/solver/solver_preprocess.cpp:48-84`. Called from
   `src/solver/simplifier_solver.cpp:226` **exactly when the caller passes a
   null `simplifier_factory`** (`simplifier_solver.cpp:220-225`) — which is
   the case for `mk_sat_smt_solver` (`src/sat/sat_solver/sat_smt_solver.cpp:
   728-730`, the SAT-core-backed default solver). This is the closest thing
   to "on by default for a plain `(check-sat)`". Its passes are gated one by
   one on `smt_params`/`preprocessor_params` booleans
   (`src/params/preprocessor_params.h:31-49`, `src/params/bit_blaster_params.h`,
   `src/params/qi_params.h:45`); defaults transcribed below.

   | flag | default | pass added |
   |---|---|---|
   | (unconditional) | — | `rewriter_simplifier` |
   | `m_propagate_values` | **true** | `propagate_values` |
   | `m_solve_eqs` | **true** | `euf::solve_eqs` |
   | `m_elim_unconstrained` | **true** | `elim_unconstrained` |
   | `m_nnf_cnf` | **true** | `cnf_nnf_simplifier` |
   | `m_macro_finder \|\| m_quasi_macros` | both **false** | `eliminate_predicates` |
   | `m_qe_lite` | **false** | qe-lite simplifier |
   | `m_pull_nested_quantifiers` | **false** | `pull_nested_quantifiers_simplifier` |
   | `m_max_bv_sharing` | **true** | `max_bv_sharing` |
   | `m_refine_inj_axiom` | **true** | `refine_inj_axiom_simplifier` |
   | `m_bv_size_reduce` | **false** | `bv::slice` |
   | `m_distribute_forall` | **false** | `distribute_forall_simplifier` |
   | `m_bound_simplifier` | **true** | `bound_simplifier` (+ nested `rewriter_simplifier`/`propagate_values`/`solve_eqs`, wrapped in `if_change_simplifier`) |
   | `m_eliminate_bounds` | **false** | `elim_bounds_simplifier` |
   | `m_simplify_bit2int` | **false** | `bit2int_simplifier` |
   | `m_bb_quantifiers` | **false** | `bv::elim_simplifier` |
   | `m_eliminate_term_ite && lift_ite != LI_FULL` | eliminate_term_ite **false** | `elim_term_ite_simplifier` |
   | `lift_ite != LI_NONE` | **LI_NONE** | `push_ite_simplifier` |
   | `ng_lift_ite != LI_NONE` | **LI_NONE** | `ng_push_ite_simplifier` |
   | (unconditional) | — | `flatten_clauses` |

   So in this pipeline the passes that are genuinely **on** with stock
   parameters are: rewriter_simplifier, propagate_values, solve_eqs,
   elim_unconstrained, cnf_nnf, max_bv_sharing, refine_inj_axiom,
   bound_simplifier, flatten_clauses. Everything else needs a param flip.

3. **`mk_fd_simplifier`** (`src/tactic/fd_solver/fd_simplifier.cpp:322-330`)
   is a second, separate default pipeline used by `mk_fd_solver`
   (finite-domain / QF_FD / PB logics): unconditionally chains
   `bounded_int2bv_simplifier` (local to that file) → `card2bv` → a local
   `enum2bv_simplifier` wrapping `enum2bv_rewriter`. This is the only route
   by which `card2bv` and array/enum-to-bv reach a query *by default* — it
   is otherwise only reachable as the standalone `card2bv` tactic.

4. **Legacy pipeline**: `src/solver/assertions/asserted_formulas.cpp` /
   `.h` is a *second, older* preprocessing framework (`simplify_fmls`
   combinators, not `dependent_expr_simplifier`), out of the named area but
   worth flagging because it is the sole includer of
   `src/ast/rewriter/distribute_forall.h` (see Dead-code section) — it is a
   parallel world to the `then_simplifier`/`dependent_expr` framework, not
   fully traced here. `did not verify` how/whether it is still reachable
   from a default `(check-sat)` path vs. only from older entry points.

---

## 1. `src/ast/simplifiers/` — the dependent_expr passes

### bit2int_simplifier
- **Where**: `src/ast/simplifiers/bit2int.h:21` (class), name `"bit2int"` at `:30`

- **Does**: rewrites `int2bv`/`bv2int` idioms back into plain integer arithmetic where legal

- **Does**: sequences a list of simplifiers (`and-then`); `if_change_simplifier` runs a sequence only until nothing further changes / picks the branch that changed something

## 2. `src/ast/rewriter/` — term rewriters

### th_rewriter — the top-level dispatcher
- **Where**: `src/ast/rewriter/th_rewriter.h:29` (class), `src/ast/rewriter/th_rewriter.cpp` (`mk_app_core` dispatch table `:213-246`, `:676`)
- **Logics**: all — this is the rewriter every `dependent_expr`'s `rewriter_simplifier`/`simplify` step calls
- **Default**: on by default (see `rewriter_simplifier` above)
- **Does**: dispatches each application to the matching per-theory rewriter (`bool_rewriter` `m_b_rw`, `arith_rewriter` `m_a_rw`, `bv_rewriter` `m_bv_rw`, `array_rewriter` `m_ar_rw`, `datatype_rewriter` `m_dt_rw`, `fpa_rewriter` `m_f_rw`, `dl_rewriter` `m_dl_rw`, `pb_rewriter` `m_pb_rw`, `seq_rewriter` `m_seq_rw`, `char_rewriter` `m_char_rw`, `recfun_rewriter` `m_rec_rw`, `finite_set_rewriter` `m_fs_rw`) plus the generic `mk_simplified_app`/AC-sharing machinery. Governed by `rewriter_params.pyg`: `push_ite_arith`(false), `push_ite_bv`(false), `pull_cheap_ite`(false), `bv_ineq_consistency_test_max`(0), `unfold_recursive_functions`(false), `cache_all`(false), `enable_der`(true), `rewrite_patterns`(false), `ignore_patterns_on_ground_qbody`(true). Has an `#if 0` block at `th_rewriter.cpp:525` and a dead-condition `if (false && k == OP_AND)` at `:196`.
- **Provenance**: [C]

### bv_rewriter
- **Where**: `src/ast/rewriter/bv_rewriter.h:52` (class, extends `poly_rewriter<bv_rewriter_core>`, core at `:27`); dispatch `src/ast/rewriter/bv_rewriter.cpp:54`
- **Logics**: QF_BV / bit-vectors
- **Default**: on by default (reached through `th_rewriter`)

### seq_rewriter
- **Where**: `src/ast/rewriter/seq_rewriter.h:44`; dispatch has 69 `mk_*` methods in `seq_rewriter.cpp` (did not enumerate all individually — exhaustive count confirmed by `grep -c`)
- **Logics**: strings/sequences/regular expressions, plus `char_rewriter` companion
- **Default**: on by default
- **Tunable params** (`src/params/seq_rewriter_params.pyg`): `coalesce_chars`(true), `max_power_expansion`(2)
- **Provenance**: [C]. `#if 0` at `:4654`, `:5271`; two `if (false && re().is_intersection(...))` dead branches at `:3003`, `:3007`.
- Satellite files under the same theory, each a distinct named component:
  - **seq_derive** (`src/ast/rewriter/seq_derive.h:60`, class `derive`) — symbolic Brzozowski/Antimirov derivative computation for regexes, with a nested `cofactor_cache`(`:61`)
  - **seq_range_collapse** (`src/ast/rewriter/seq_range_collapse.h`) — recognizes regexes that are Boolean combinations of character-class primitives and collapses them to canonical multi-range form
  - **seq_range_predicate** (`src/ast/rewriter/seq_range_predicate.h:49`, class `range_predicate`) — smart-constructor side conditions/range algebra over `[0, max_char]`
  - **seq_regex_bisim** (`src/ast/rewriter/seq_regex_bisim.h:63`, class `regex_bisim`) — union-find bisimulation algorithm to prove two regex-derivative states equivalent
  - **seq_regex_witness** (`src/ast/rewriter/seq_regex_witness.h:42`, class `regex_witness`) — extracts a non-emptiness witness string for a regex
  - **seq_subset** (`src/ast/rewriter/seq_subset.h:21`) — heuristic regex-subset checks used by `seq_rewriter`
  - **guard_set** (`src/ast/rewriter/guard_set.h:34`) — a conjunction of derivative cofactor guards over the element variable, used by the derivative machinery

- **Where**: `src/ast/macros/macro_manager.h:37`

- **Where**: `src/ast/macros/quasi_macros.h:29`

- **Does**: finds "quasi-macros" — equalities that are macro-shaped after a restricted generalization (weaker precondition than a full macro)
- **Provenance**: [C]
