# Part 6 — the tactic library


Repo: `/home/mjbommar/projects/personal/axeyum/references/z3`, HEAD `e18d63b` ("Remove build_z3.bat (#10754)").
Read-only inventory. All `path:line` citations below come from an actual
`grep -n` run against this checkout; anything not directly grepped is marked
"did not verify" rather than guessed.

Registration mechanism (verified): `ADD_TACTIC(...)` / `ADD_PROBE(...)`
strings are not C++ calls — they are Python-syntax expressions that
`scripts/mk_genfile_common.py` (`ADD_TACTIC_DATA`/`ADD_PROBE_DATA`, around
line 681-699) text-scans out of every `.h`/`.hpp` file under the configured
source dirs and `eval`s to build the tactic/probe command tables. This is
why some entries use Python single-quotes (e.g. `nlsat_tactic.h:45`) and why
`solve_eqs_tactic.h`'s `ADD_TACTIC` line sits inside a `/* */` C++ comment
(`solve_eqs_tactic.h:80-83`) — a C++ comment does not hide it from the
generator, so it is a live registration, not dead code.

---

## Per-logic default strategy

Transcribed from the actual `and_then(...)` / `or_else(...)` / `cond(...)`
chains in each `mk_<logic>_tactic` function. "Preamble" = the shared
simplification/preprocessing chain each pipeline builds before dispatching to
a decision procedure.

### QF_BV — `src/tactic/smtlogics/qfbv_tactic.cpp`

`mk_qfbv_tactic` (`qfbv_tactic.cpp:126-131`), preamble at `qfbv_preamble`
(`qfbv_tactic.cpp:37-81`), main pipeline `mk_qfbv_tactic(m,p,sat,smt)`
(`qfbv_tactic.cpp:92-123`):

1. `simplify` (flat_and_or=false)
2. `propagate-values` (flat_and_or=false)
3. `solve-eqs` (conservative: solve_eqs_max_occs=2)
4. `elim-uncnstr`
5. `bv-divrem-bounds`
6. `reduce-bv-size` — only `if_no_proofs(if_no_unsat_cores(...))`
7. `simplify` (som=true, pull_cheap_ite=true, push_ite_bv=false, local_ctx=true, flat=true, hoist_mul=false)
8. `simplify` (hoist_mul=true, som=false) — "Z3 can solve a couple of extra benchmarks... but SMT-COMP timeout is too small" (comment)
9. `max-bv-sharing`
10. `ackermannize_bv` — only `if_no_proofs(if_no_unsat_cores(...))`
11. `cond(is-qfbv-eq, then A, else cond(is-qfbv, then B, else smt))`:
    - **A** (=/extract/concat-only fragment): `bv1-blast` → `smt` (with `preprocess=false`)
    - **B** (general QF_BV): `bit-blast` → `when(memory < 300MB, simplify+solve-eqs (local_ctx) → aig (if_no_proofs))` → `sat`
    - **else** (still has uninterpreted symbols, e.g. `HI_DIV0=false`): `smt`
12. Top-level `sat` is itself `cond(produce-proofs, simplify+smt, psat)` (parallel SAT) — chosen in the public `mk_qfbv_tactic(m,p)` wrapper (`qfbv_tactic.cpp:126-131`).

### QF_LIA — `src/tactic/smtlogics/qflia_tactic.cpp`

`mk_qflia_tactic` (`qflia_tactic.cpp:210-239`):

1. `mk_preamble_tactic` (`qflia_tactic.cpp:187-208`): `simplify` → `propagate-values` → `ctx-simplify` (max_depth=30) → `simplify` (pull_cheap_ite, hoist_ite) → `solve-eqs` → `lia2card` (max_range=1, max_ite_nesting=1) → `elim-uncnstr`
2. `simplify` (arith_lhs=true)
3. `or_else` of four strategies, first to decide wins:
   - `mk_ilp_model_finder_tactic` — only if unbounded ILP: `propagate-ineqs` → `or_else(try_for(no-cut-smt seed100,2s), add-bounds[-16,15]+try_for(lia2sat,5s), try_for(no-cut-smt seed200,5s), add-bounds[-32,31]+try_for(lia2sat,10s))` → `fail-if-undecided`
   - `mk_pb_tactic` — only if `is-pb` and not proof/unsat-core mode: `or_else(fail_if(size>=80000)+fail_if_not(is-ilp)+fail-if-undecided, pb2bv→fail_if_not(is-qfbv)→bit-blast pipeline→sat)`
   - `and_then(fail_if_not(is-quasi-pb), lia2sat[lia2pb_max_bits=64], fail-if-undecided)`
   - `mk_bounded_tactic` — only if bounded: `or_else(try_for(no-cut-smt seed100,5s), try_for(no-cut-no-relevancy-smt seed200,5s), try_for(no-cut-smt seed300,15s))` → `fail-if-undecided`
   - `smt` (final fallback)

`lia2sat` = `propagate-ineqs → normalize-bounds → lia2pb → pb2bv → fail_if_not(is-qfbv) → bv2sat`; `bv2sat` = `simplify → propagate-values → solve-eqs → max-bv-sharing → bit-blast → aig → sat`.

### QF_LRA — `src/tactic/smtlogics/qflra_tactic.cpp`

`mk_qflra_tactic` (`qflra_tactic.cpp:29-88`):

1. `using_params(smt, {arith.greatest_error_pivot=true})`

That is the *entire live* pipeline — one call straight to `smt`. Two
alternative pipelines are dead code behind `#if 0`:
- `qflra_tactic.cpp:50-68`: a MIP-based pipeline (recover-01, ctx-simplify,
  elim-uncnstr, solve-eqs, then `try_for(mk_mip_tactic(m), 30000)`) —
  `mk_mip_tactic` is not defined anywhere in this tree (see Dead code below).
- `qflra_tactic.cpp:74-83`: a 3-way `par` over three simplex strategies.

### QF_NIA — `src/tactic/smtlogics/qfnia_tactic.cpp`

`mk_qfnia_tactic` (`qfnia_tactic.cpp:126-134`):

1. `mk_qfnia_preamble` (`qfnia_tactic.cpp:61-92`): `simplify` → `propagate-values` → `solve-eqs` → `ctx-simplify` (max_depth=30) → `simplify` (pull_cheap_ite, local_ctx) → `elim-uncnstr` → `lia2card` (max_range=1) → `card2bv` → `skip_if_failed(cofactor-term-ite)`
2. `or_else`:
   - `mk_qfnia_sat_solver` (`qfnia_tactic.cpp:94-105`): `simplify`(hoist_mul) → `nla2bv`(max_bv_size=64) → `skip_if_failed(mk_qfnia_bv_solver)` → `fail-if-undecided`, where `mk_qfnia_bv_solver` = `simplify → propagate-values → simplify(local_ctx) → max-bv-sharing → bit-blast(max_conflicts=500) → sat`
   - `try_for(mk_qfnia_smt_solver, 2000ms)` — `simplify(som=true) → smt`
   - `mk_qfnia_nlsat_solver` — `simplify(som=true,factor=false) → try_for(qfnra-nlsat, 3000ms) → fail-if-undecided`
   - `mk_qfnia_smt_solver` (unbounded retry)

### QF_NRA — `src/tactic/smtlogics/qfnra_tactic.cpp`

`mk_qfnra_tactic` (`qfnra_tactic.cpp:323-329`):

1. `simplify`
2. `propagate-values`
3. `mk_qfnra_mixed_solver` (`qfnra_tactic.cpp:302-321`) — `cond` chain on the
   `memory` probe against five size tiers (VERY_SMALL<30MB, SMALL<80MB,
   MIDDLE<300MB, LARGE<600MB, else VERY_LARGE), each tier a large `or_else`
   of `qfnra-nlsat` runs at different `variable_ordering_strategy` values
   (1–5) and time budgets, a `simple_check` pre-pass, a multilinear
   local-search pass (`mk_multilinear_ls_tactic` = `smt` with `use_ls=true`),
   an `smt`-with-`greatest_error_pivot` pass, and (for the two smallest
   tiers) a loop of 200/100 randomly-reseeded+shuffled `qfnra-nlsat` retries
   before a final unconditional `qfnra-nlsat`.
   Selection is purely by **memory footprint at the time the probe runs**,
   not by problem size/degree.

### QF_IDL — `src/tactic/smtlogics/qfidl_tactic.cpp`

`mk_qfidl_tactic` (`qfidl_tactic.cpp:38-111`):

1. `cond(num-consts < 5000 AND NOT produce-proofs AND NOT produce-unsat-cores, A, smt)`
   - **A**: preamble (`simplify → fix-dl-var → propagate-values → elim-uncnstr` then `solve-eqs → simplify(arith_lhs) → propagate-values → normalize-bounds → solve-eqs`) → `or_else(diff-neq[diff_neq_max_k=25], try2bv, smt)`
   - `try2bv` = `lia2pb[max_bits=4] → propagate-ineqs → pb2bv[clauses_limit=8] → fail_if_not(is-qfbv) → bv_solver`, `bv_solver` = `simplify → propagate-values → solve-eqs → max-bv-sharing → bit-blast → aig → sat`

### QF_UF — `src/tactic/smtlogics/qfuf_tactic.cpp`

`mk_qfuf_tactic` (`qfuf_tactic.cpp:27-38`):

1. `simplify` → `propagate-values` → `solve-eqs` → `simplify`(pull_cheap_ite, local_ctx) → `symmetry-reduce` (only `if_no_proofs(if_no_unsat_cores(...))`) → `smt`

### QF_UFBV — `src/tactic/smtlogics/qfufbv_tactic.cpp`

`mk_qfufbv_tactic` (`qfufbv_tactic.cpp:180-196`):

1. `mk_qfufbv_preamble` (`qfufbv_tactic.cpp:166-178`): `simplify` → `propagate-values` → `solve-eqs` → `elim-uncnstr` → `reduce-args` (guarded) → `reduce-bv-size` (guarded) → `max-bv-sharing` → `ackermannize_bv` (guarded)
2. `cond(is-qfbv, mk_qfbv_tactic, smt)`

A **separate** entry point `mk_qfufbv_ackr_tactic` (`qfufbv_tactic.cpp:198-204`,
registered as tactic `qfufbv_ackr`) uses a different preamble
(`mk_qfufbv_preamble1`, `qfufbv_tactic.cpp:138-164`, which additionally runs
`bv_bound_chk`) and dispatches to the `qfufbv_ackr_tactic` C++ class
(`qfufbv_tactic.cpp:45-136`), which runs lazy Ackermannization (`lackr`) with
either `mk_qfbv_tactic`/`mk_inc_sat_solver` or `mk_qfaufbv_tactic` as its
UF-free backend depending on params. **This is not the tactic reached by
`(check-sat)` under `QF_UFBV`** — `mk_tactic_for_logic` only ever calls
`mk_qfufbv_tactic`, never `mk_qfufbv_ackr_tactic`
(`smt_strategic_solver.cpp:88-89`).

### QF_AUFBV — `src/tactic/smtlogics/qfaufbv_tactic.cpp`

`mk_qfaufbv_tactic` (`qfaufbv_tactic.cpp:55-68`); also reached for `QF_ABV`
(`smt_strategic_solver.cpp:86-87`):

1. `mk_qfaufbv_preamble` (`qfaufbv_tactic.cpp:32-53`): `simplify` → `propagate-values` → `solve-eqs` → `elim-uncnstr` → `bv-divrem-bounds` → `reduce-bv-size` (guarded) → `simplify`(som,pull_cheap_ite,local_ctx) → `max-bv-sharing` → `ackermannize_bv` (guarded)
2. `cond(is-qfbv, mk_qfbv_tactic, smt)`

### QF_AUFLIA — `src/tactic/smtlogics/qfauflia_tactic.cpp`

`mk_qfauflia_tactic` (`qfauflia_tactic.cpp:27-48`):

1. `simplify → propagate-values → solve-eqs → elim-uncnstr → simplify` (preamble, params `elim_and=true, som=true, sort_store=true`)
2. `smt` (with `array.simplify=false`)

### NRA — `src/tactic/smtlogics/nra_tactic.cpp`

`mk_nra_tactic` (`nra_tactic.cpp:30-50`):

1. `simplify` → `propagate-values` → `qe-light` → `simplify`
2. `cond(is-qfnra, or_else(try_for(qfnra-nlsat,5s), try_for(qfnra-nlsat seed11 factor=false,10s), qfnra-nlsat seed13 factor=false), or_else(nlqsat, smt))`

### Quantified logics — `src/tactic/smtlogics/quant_tactics.cpp`

Shared `mk_quant_preprocessor(disable_gaussian)` (`quant_tactics.cpp:31-57`):
`simplify → propagate-values → ctx-simplify(max_depth=30) → simplify(pull_cheap_ite) → [solve-eqs guarded by NOT has-patterns, or skip if disable_gaussian] → elim-uncnstr → simplify`.

- **UFNIA** (`mk_ufnia_tactic`, `:63-69`): `mk_quant_preprocessor(disable_gaussian=true)` → `qe-light` → `smt`
- **UFLRA** (`mk_uflra_tactic`, `:71-76`): `mk_quant_preprocessor(false)` → `smt`
- **AUFLIA** (`mk_auflia_tactic`, `:78-89`): `mk_quant_preprocessor(true)` → `or_else(fail_if(num-exprs>128)+using_params(smt,{qi.cost=0})+fail-if-undecided, smt)`
- **AUFLIRA** (`:91-96`) / **AUFNIRA** (`:98-103`): `mk_quant_preprocessor(false) → smt`
- **LRA** (`mk_lra_tactic`, `:105-115`): `mk_quant_preprocessor(false)` → `qe-light` → `cond(has-quantifiers, cond(is-lira, or_else(qsat, smt), smt), smt)`
- **LIA** (`:117-119`) and **LIRA** (`:121-123`) are **literal aliases**: `mk_lia_tactic` and `mk_lira_tactic` both just `return mk_lra_tactic(m, p);` — same code path, no LIA/LIRA-specific logic exists.

## Registered tactics

Ground truth: 118 `ADD_TACTIC(...)` registrations found via
`grep -rn "ADD_TACTIC" src/` (dump saved at time of writing, one match per
tactic command — some names, e.g. `ufbv-rewriter`, alias another tactic's
implementation; see Dead/unreachable section). Each entry's `Does` text is
copied verbatim from the registration's description string, so it carries
`[C]` provenance directly from source. `Logics`/`Default` reflect what I
traced through the per-logic pipelines above plus a whole-tree grep for
external callers of each `mk_*` factory function; where I did not trace a
call site, the field says so explicitly rather than guessing.

### ackermannize_bv
- **Where**: `src/ackermannization/ackermannize_bv_tactic.h:53`
- **Logics**: QF_BV, QF_AUFBV, QF_UFBV, QF_FP
- **Default**: in default strategy for QF_BV/QF_AUFBV/QF_UFBV preambles and qffp preamble
- **Does**: A tactic for performing full Ackermannization on bv instances.
- **Provenance**: [C] (`mk_ackermannize_bv_tactic(m, p)`, description string from the ADD_TACTIC registration itself)

### subpaving
- **Where**: `src/math/subpaving/tactic/subpaving_tactic.h:29`
- **Logics**: n/a
- **Default**: available but not in any default pipeline — explicitly a test tactic ("tactic for testing subpaving module")
- **Does**: tactic for testing subpaving module.
- **Provenance**: [C] (`mk_subpaving_tactic(m, p)`, description string from the ADD_TACTIC registration itself)

### horn
- **Where**: `src/muz/fp/horn_tactic.h:49`
- **Logics**: HORN (Datalog/CHC)
- **Default**: in default strategy for HORN (smt_strategic_solver.cpp:115)
- **Does**: apply tactic for horn clauses.
- **Provenance**: [C] (`mk_horn_tactic(m, p)`, description string from the ADD_TACTIC registration itself)

### horn-simplify
- **Where**: `src/muz/fp/horn_tactic.h:54`
- **Logics**: HORN
- **Default**: available but not in any default pipeline (horn_tactic.h:49-54) — separate simplify-only entry point, not wired into mk_tactic_for_logic
- **Does**: simplify horn clauses.
- **Provenance**: [C] (`mk_horn_simplify_tactic(m, p)`, description string from the ADD_TACTIC registration itself)

### nlsat
- **Where**: `src/nlsat/tactic/nlsat_tactic.h:45`
- **Logics**: QF_NRA/NRA (nonlinear real, standalone)
- **Default**: reached only through qfnra_nlsat_tactic.cpp, not directly from smt_strategic_solver.cpp
- **Does**: (try to) solve goal using a nonlinear arithmetic solver.
- **Provenance**: [C] (`mk_nlsat_tactic(m, p)`, description string from the ADD_TACTIC registration itself)

### qfnra-nlsat
- **Where**: `src/nlsat/tactic/qfnra_nlsat_tactic.h:47`
- **Logics**: QF_NRA, QF_NIA, NRA
- **Default**: in default strategy for QF_NRA (qfnra_tactic.cpp mixed_solver), QF_NIA fallback (qfnia_nlsat_solver), and NRA (nra_tactic.cpp)
- **Does**: builtin strategy for solving QF_NRA problems using only nlsat.
- **Provenance**: [C] (`mk_qfnra_nlsat_tactic(m, p)`, description string from the ADD_TACTIC registration itself)

### qe-light

## Tactical combinators

These are not `ADD_TACTIC`-registered names (they are not directly
invocable from an SMT-LIB `(apply ...)` script by these C++ names — the
script-level combinator syntax is separate, parsed by
`cmd_context`/`sexpr2tactic`); they are the C++ builder functions every
pipeline above is written with. Source: `src/tactic/tactical.h` (declarations)
and `src/tactic/tactical.cpp` (implementations), confirmed by `grep -n`.

### and_then
- **Where**: `src/tactic/tactical.cpp:231` (2-ary; overloads up to 11-ary through `:271`, plus an `unsigned num, tactic* const*` array form)
- **Does**: sequential composition — run `t1`, then run `t2` on every resulting subgoal; fails if any step fails.
- **Provenance**: [C]

### or_else
- **Where**: `src/tactic/tactical.cpp:406` (2-ary; overloads through `:451`, plus array form at `:402`)
- **Does**: try `t1`; if it fails, try `t2` on the original goal (first success wins).
- **Provenance**: [C]

### par
- **Where**: `src/tactic/tactical.cpp:582` (array form), `:588`-`:603` (2/3/4-ary); backing class `par_tactical`, `src/tactic/tactical.cpp:465`
- **Does**: run all given tactics concurrently on the same goal; take the first to finish (successfully). This is Z3's "parallel or" — there is no separately-named `par_or`.
- **Provenance**: [C]

### par_and_then
- **Where**: `src/tactic/tactical.cpp:606` (2-ary), `:851` (array form); backing class `par_and_then_tactical`, `src/tactic/tactical.cpp:611`
- **Does**: run `t1`, then run `t2` on the resulting subgoals **in parallel**.
- **Provenance**: [C]

### repeat
- **Where**: `src/tactic/tactical.cpp:998`; backing class `repeat_tactical`, `src/tactic/tactical.cpp:899`
- **Does**: apply `t` repeatedly until it stops changing the goal or `max` iterations (default `UINT_MAX`) are reached.
- **Provenance**: [C]

### try_for
- **Where**: `src/tactic/tactical.cpp:1078`; backing class `try_for_tactical`, `src/tactic/tactical.cpp:1048`
- **Does**: run `t` with a wall-clock timeout in milliseconds; treated as "undecided"/fails past the deadline rather than crashing. Used everywhere in the nonlinear (`qfnra`, `qfnia`) pipelines to budget many alternative strategies.
- **Provenance**: [C]

### using_params
- **Where**: `src/tactic/tactical.cpp:1112`; backing class `using_params_tactical`, `src/tactic/tactical.cpp:1082`
- **Does**: run `t` with a modified/overriding `params_ref`. This is the single most-used combinator in every pipeline transcribed above — nearly every preamble step wraps its tactic in `using_params` to set one or two flags (`local_ctx`, `flat_and_or`, `som`, `elim_and`, ...) without disturbing the ambient params.
- **Provenance**: [C]

### annotate_tactic
- **Where**: `src/tactic/tactical.cpp:1145`; backing class `annotate_tactical`, `src/tactic/tactical.cpp:1116`
- **Does**: give an inner (usually anonymous, composed-inline) tactic a name for tracing/statistics — e.g. `"no-cut-smt-tactic"`, `"pb-tactic"`, `"lia2sat-tactic"` in `qflia_tactic.cpp`.
- **Provenance**: [C]

### cond
- **Where**: `src/tactic/tactical.cpp:1180`; backing class `cond_tactical`, `src/tactic/tactical.cpp:1149`
- **Does**: `if p(goal) then t1 else t2` — the probe-driven branch every per-logic dispatcher above is built from (`default_tactic.cpp`'s whole probe cascade is nested `cond`s).
- **Provenance**: [C]

### when
- **Where**: `src/tactic/tactical.cpp:1184`
- **Does**: `cond(p, t, skip)` — run `t` only if probe `p` holds, otherwise no-op. Implemented literally as `cond(p, t, mk_skip_tactic())`.
- **Provenance**: [C]

### fail_if / fail_if_not
- **Where**: `src/tactic/tactical.cpp:1215` / `:1219`
- **Does**: fail immediately if probe `p` is (resp. is not) true — used as a cheap guard at the head of an `and_then`/`or_else` branch (e.g. `fail_if_not(mk_is_pb_probe())` gating `mk_pb_tactic`).
- **Provenance**: [C]

### fail_if_branching
- **Where**: `src/tactic/tactical.cpp:1023`; backing class `fail_if_branching_tactical`, `src/tactic/tactical.cpp:1002`
- **Does**: fail if applying `t` would split the goal into more than `threshold` subgoals.
- **Provenance**: [C]

### if_no_proofs / if_no_unsat_cores / if_no_models
- **Where**: `src/tactic/tactical.cpp:1281` / `:1285` / `:1289`; backing classes at `:1223`, `:1242`, `:1260`
- **Does**: run `t` only when proof/unsat-core/model production is *not* requested — the standard guard wrapping every "unsound-under-proof-tracking" simplification (`bv_size_reduction`, `ackermannize_bv`, `symmetry-reduce`, ...) throughout the pipelines above. `if_no_proofs(if_no_unsat_cores(t))` is the near-universal double-guard idiom.
- **Provenance**: [C]

### skip_if_failed
- **Where**: `src/tactic/tactical.cpp:1294`
- **Does**: `or_else(t, mk_skip_tactic())` — run `t`, but treat a failure as a no-op rather than propagating failure. Used in QF_NIA's preamble around `cofactor-term-ite` and around the bit-blasting BV sub-solver.
- **Provenance**: [C]

### clean
- **Where**: `src/tactic/tactical.cpp:1044`; backing class `cleanup_tactical`, `src/tactic/tactical.cpp:1027`
- **Does**: run `t`, then garbage-collect/compact the resulting goal's AST references. Did not verify a call site in the pipelines read above.
- **Provenance**: [C]

### mk_lazy_tactic
- **Where**: `src/tactic/tactic.h:138`
- **Does**: defer construction of the inner tactic (via a `std::function` factory) until the tactic actually runs, rather than at pipeline-build time — used throughout `default_tactic.cpp` (every `cond` branch) and `qfnra_tactic.cpp`'s size-tiered solvers so unreached alternatives are never even built.
- **Provenance**: [C]

### mk_report_verbose_tactic / mk_trace_tactic
- **Where**: `src/tactic/tactic.h:146` / `:147`
- **Does**: side-effecting no-op tactics that print a verbosity-gated message or a `TRACE` line — used as pipeline checkpoints (e.g. `"(qfnia-tactic)"`, `"(qfnia-sat)"` in `qfnia_tactic.cpp`; `"ufbv_pre"`/`"ufbv_post"` in `ufbv_tactic.cpp:43,60`). Purely diagnostic, no effect on the goal.
- **Provenance**: [C]

### mk_skip_tactic / mk_fail_tactic / mk_fail_if_undecided_tactic
- **Where**: registered as ordinary tactics too — see `skip`, `fail`, `fail-if-undecided` in the flat inventory above (`src/tactic/tactic.h:135-137,141-143`).
- **Does**: `skip` = always-succeed no-op (the identity of `or_else`/target of `when`'s else-branch); `fail` = always-fail; `fail-if-undecided` = fail unless the goal has been fully decided (sat/unsat) — used as the terminal guard closing off many `or_else` chains above (QF_LIA, QF_NIA, QF_NRA, AUFLIA).
- **Provenance**: [C]

### Probe algebra: mk_not / mk_and / mk_or / mk_implies / mk_eq / mk_neq / mk_le / mk_lt / mk_ge / mk_gt / mk_add / mk_sub / mk_mul / mk_div / mk_const_probe
- **Where**: `src/tactic/probe.h:56` (`mk_const_probe`), `:101-114` (the boolean/arithmetic algebra over probes)
- **Does**: build compound probes by combining primitive ones (e.g. `mk_and(mk_is_ilp_probe(), mk_is_unbounded_probe())` in `qfidl_tactic.cpp:98-100`, `mk_lt(mk_memory_probe(), mk_const_probe(300))` gating QF_BV's AIG sub-pass). `mk_or`, `mk_implies`, `mk_eq`, `mk_neq`, `mk_le`, `mk_add`, `mk_sub`, `mk_mul`, `mk_div` — did not verify a call site in the pipelines read above (only `mk_not`, `mk_and`, `mk_lt`, `mk_ge`, `mk_gt` were directly observed in use).
- **Provenance**: [C]

## Dead or unreachable

### `ufbv-rewriter` registration calls the wrong function — [C], high confidence
`src/tactic/ufbv/ufbv_rewriter_tactic.h:28`:
```
ADD_TACTIC("ufbv-rewriter",  "Applies UFBV-specific rewriting rules, mainly demodulation.", "mk_quasi_macros_tactic(m, p)")
```
The command string is `mk_quasi_macros_tactic(m, p)`, not
`mk_ufbv_rewriter_tactic(m, p)`. So `(apply ufbv-rewriter)` from a script (or
any consumer of the tactic-command table, e.g. the Python/`.NET` API's tactic
listing) actually constructs and runs the quasi-macros tactic, not the UFBV
rewriter. This looks like a copy-paste bug (the adjacent `quasi-macros`
registration in `quasi_macros_tactic.h` correctly calls
`mk_quasi_macros_tactic`). The real `mk_ufbv_rewriter_tactic` is still
reachable, but only by its C++ name directly from `ufbv_tactic.cpp:56`
(`mk_ufbv_preprocessor_tactic`), not through the `ufbv-rewriter` command name.
Confirmed by reading the exact registration line; did not check whether this
also affects the Python/OCaml/.NET binding generators that consume the same
table.

### `mk_mip_tactic` does not exist anywhere in this tree — [C]
`grep -rn "mk_mip_tactic" src/` finds only three commented-out call sites
(`qflia_tactic.cpp:115,159,166`, all prefixed `//`) and one call inside a
`#if 0` block (`qflra_tactic.cpp:66`). There is no declaration or definition
of `mk_mip_tactic` anywhere in `src/`. A Mixed-Integer-Programming tactic was
apparently once planned/prototyped for both QF_LIA and QF_LRA and then
removed, leaving only the dead references behind.

### `mk_is_mip_probe` is defined and registrable-looking but only called from dead code — [C]
`src/tactic/arith/probe_arith.cpp:422` defines `mk_is_mip_probe()`; its
declaration is at `probe_arith.h:39`. Its **only** call site in `src/` is
`qflra_tactic.cpp:65`, inside the same `#if 0` block as `mk_mip_tactic`
above. It has no `ADD_PROBE` registration (not in the 42-entry list), so it
is doubly unreachable: not scriptable, and its one C++ caller is compiled
out.

### QF_LRA's live pipeline is a single line — [C]
`mk_qflra_tactic` (`qflra_tactic.cpp:85`) is, after the two `#if 0` blocks
are excluded, just `using_params(using_params(mk_smt_tactic(m), pivot_p), p)`
— i.e. QF_LRA has **no dedicated preprocessing pipeline** in the shipped
build; every QF_LRA problem goes straight to the general `smt` tactic with
one param (`arith.greatest_error_pivot=true`) set. This contrasts sharply
with QF_LIA/QF_NIA/QF_NRA, which each have substantial dedicated preambles
and multi-strategy `or_else` chains.

### `default_tactic.cpp`'s QF_UFNRA branch is commented out — [C]
`src/tactic/portfolio/default_tactic.cpp:51`:
```
//cond(mk_is_qfufnra_probe(), mk_qfufnra_tactic(m, p),
```
`mk_qfufnra_tactic` does not exist anywhere in `src/` (`grep -rn
"mk_qfufnra_tactic" src/` finds only this commented line). The probe
`mk_is_qfufnra_probe`/`is-qfufnra` **is** implemented and `ADD_PROBE`-registered
(`probe_arith.h:68`, `probe_arith.cpp:704-746`) but its only consumer is this
dead line — so `is-qfufnra` is a live, scriptable probe with zero effect on
the default dispatcher. A QF_UFNRA goal run through `mk_default_tactic`
falls through every other `cond` and lands on plain `smt`.

### `qfufbv_ackr` is registered but never reached by `(check-sat)` under any logic — [C] + [I]
`mk_tactic_for_logic` (`smt_strategic_solver.cpp:88-89`) maps `QF_UFBV` to
`mk_qfufbv_tactic` only. `mk_qfufbv_ackr_tactic` (the eager/lazy
Ackermannization variant, `qfufbv_tactic.cpp:198-204`) is registered as the
tactic command `qfufbv_ackr` and is exercised by unit tests presumably (did
not verify), but a user who just sets `(set-logic QF_UFBV)` never runs it —
they would have to explicitly `(apply qfufbv_ackr)` or select it through
some other non-default path.

### `smtfd` tactic command is bypassed by its own logic dispatch — [C]
`mk_smtfd_tactic` exists (`smtfd_solver.cpp:2105-2106`, registered as tactic
`smtfd`), but `smt_strategic_solver.cpp:126-127`
(`mk_special_solver_for_logic`) intercepts `logic=="SMTFD"` and returns
`mk_smtfd_solver(...)` (a `solver*`) directly, before `mk_tactic_for_logic`
(and therefore the `smtfd` tactic command) is ever consulted. The registered
tactic is reachable only via explicit `(apply smtfd)`, never via
`(set-logic SMTFD)`.

### `mk_lia_tactic` and `mk_lira_tactic` are literal aliases of `mk_lra_tactic` — [C]
`quant_tactics.cpp:117-123`:
```cpp
tactic * mk_lia_tactic(ast_manager & m, params_ref const & p) {
    return mk_lra_tactic(m, p);
}
tactic * mk_lira_tactic(ast_manager & m, params_ref const & p) {
    return mk_lra_tactic(m, p);
}
```
The `LIA` and `LIRA` SMT-LIB logics (quantified linear integer / mixed
arithmetic) run byte-identical code to `LRA`. Not a bug — the underlying
`qe-light`+`qsat`+`smt` pipeline is genuinely logic-agnostic here — but
worth knowing before attributing any LIA/LIRA-specific behavior to a
dedicated strategy that does not exist.

### `mk_qffpbv_tactic` and `mk_qffplra_tactic` are aliases of `mk_qffp_tactic` — [C]
`qffp_tactic.cpp:107-109` (`mk_qffpbv_tactic`) and `qffplra_tactic.cpp:23-27`
(`mk_qffplra_tactic`) both just call and return `mk_qffp_tactic(m, p)`. All
three registered tactic commands (`qffp`, `qffpbv`, `qffplra`) run the same
pipeline; only the routing probe that selects among them
(`is-qffp`/`is-qffpbv`/`is-qffplra`) differs.

### `mk_is_qfufbv_probe` exists in C++ but has no `ADD_PROBE` registration — [C]
Declared `src/tactic/probe.h:119`, used internally by
`qfufbv_tactic.cpp:203` to gate `mk_qfufbv_ackr_tactic`'s fallback to `smt`.
Its sibling probes `is-propositional`, `is-qfbv`, `is-qfaufbv` are all
`ADD_PROBE`-registered a few lines below it (`probe.h:122-124`), but
`is-qfufbv` itself is not in that block — so it cannot be named from a
script even though the C++ pipeline depends on it.

### A large population of tactics is registered but consulted by no default pipeline — [C] + [I]
Cross-checking every `ADD_TACTIC` factory function
(`mk_<name>_tactic`) for callers outside its own defining file (via
`grep -rn "mk_<fn>(" src/`) turned up roughly three dozen tactics with **zero**
external callers anywhere in `src/` — meaning they are compiled, registered,
invocable one at a time via `(apply <name>)` or the tactic-combinator DSL,
but not woven into `mk_qf*_tactic`, `quant_tactics.cpp`, `default_tactic.cpp`,
or `smt_strategic_solver.cpp`:
`subpaving`, `horn-simplify`, `qe`, `qe_rec`, `sat-preprocess`,
`ctx-solver-simplify`, `psmt`, `fm`, `bvarray2uf`, `propagate-bv-bounds`,
`propagate-bv-bounds2`, `bv-slice`, `elim-small-bv`, `blast-term-ite`,
`collect-statistics`, `demodulator`, `dom-simplify`, `elim-predicates`,
`elim-uncnstr2`, `fold-unfold`, `injectivity`, `occf`, `pb-preprocess`,
`propagate-values2`, `reduce-args2`, `special-relations`, `split-clause`,
`euf-completion`, `solver-subsumption`, `qfbv-sls`, `sls-smt`, `eq2bv`,
`dt2bv`, `purify-arith`, `qe2`. (Per-entry citations are in the flat
inventory above; this list is a summary, not independently re-verified line
by line here.) Several of these are plausibly exercised by test suites
(`src/test/`) or the Python/opt front ends instead of the tactic pipelines —
did not verify test coverage for any of them.

### A parallel population of probes is registered but consulted by no default pipeline — [C]
By the same method: `ackr-bound-probe`, `arith-max-deg`, `arith-avg-deg`,
`arith-max-bw`, `arith-avg-bw`, `is-qflira`, `is-nia`, `is-nira`, `is-lia`,
`depth`, `size`, `num-bool-consts`, `num-arith-consts`, `num-bv-consts`,
`produce-model` have no caller outside their own registration file. Most of
these read as pure introspection/diagnostic surface for `(apply ... using
...)`, `(check-sat-using ...)`, or external tooling, not orphaned mistakes —
except `is-qfufnra` (above), which was clearly meant to drive a `cond` that
got commented out.

### `#if 0` / commented dead blocks found by pattern search
`grep -rn "#if 0" src/tactic/` (restricted to the tactic library, not the
whole repo) turns up:
- `src/tactic/arith/bv2int_rewriter.cpp:259,590`
- `src/tactic/bv/bv_size_reduction_tactic.cpp:115,178,326`
- `src/tactic/core/cofactor_elim_term_ite.cpp:626,648`
- `src/tactic/goal.cpp:596`
- `src/tactic/smtlogics/qflra_tactic.cpp:50,74` (both covered above)

I read and characterized the two `qflra_tactic.cpp` blocks; the other six did
not verify — flagged only by the grep pattern, not read in full.

# Part 7 — `src/nlsat/` and `src/math/`: the arithmetic engines


Repo: /home/mjbommar/projects/personal/axeyum/references/z3, HEAD e18d63b. Read-only inventory,
no build performed. Line numbers are from `grep -n` against the checked-out tree; "did not verify"
marks anything not confirmed by reading the body. Provenance: `[C]` = read from code in this
session, `[I]` = inferred from signature/comment/context without reading the full call graph,
`[P]` = attributed to a paper/algorithm name in a comment (not independently checked against the
paper).

---

## src/nlsat/

The model-constructing CDCL(T) solver for nonlinear real arithmetic (QF_NRA/NRA), also driven
bounded/incrementally from `nla_core` (`src/math/lp/nla_core.cpp`) as a fallback oracle for
`arith.nl` in QF_NIA/QF_NRA/NIA/NRA. 12,751 total lines across 11 .cpp/.h pairs plus levelwise.cpp.

### explain (conflict-driven projection, nlsat_explain.cpp)

Implements a CAD-lite projection/explanation operator: given a conflicting set of literals over
the just-eliminated variable, produces an equisatisfiable literal set not mentioning that
variable (McCallum-style projection with PSC chains), used both for full learned-clause conflict
explanation and single-variable elimination (`project`).

### `psc_chain`
- **Where**: `src/nlsat/nlsat_explain.cpp:138`
- **Logics**: QF_NRA/NRA
- **Default**: on by default
- **Does**: computes the principal subresultant coefficient chain of two polynomials in a variable — the core projection primitive.
- **Provenance**: [C]

### `collect_polys`
- **Where**: `src/nlsat/nlsat_explain.cpp:148`
- **Does**: gathers the polynomials occurring (as atoms) in a literal set, feeding projection.
- **Provenance**: [C]

### `find_cell_roots`
- **Where**: `src/nlsat/nlsat_explain.cpp:207`
- **Does**: isolates roots of a polynomial set at the current sample to build a CAD cell description.
- **Provenance**: [C]

### `add_zero_assumption`
- **Where**: `src/nlsat/nlsat_explain.cpp:294`
- **Does**: records that a polynomial vanishes identically at the sample as a projection-set assumption (gated by `zero_disc` param).
- **Provenance**: [C]

### `elim_vanishing` (poly and poly-vector overloads)
- **Where**: `src/nlsat/nlsat_explain.cpp:340`, `:391`
- **Does**: strips leading coefficients that vanish at the sample so projection uses a "live" polynomial.
- **Provenance**: [C]

### `normalize` (literal, and literal-vector overload)
- **Where**: `src/nlsat/nlsat_explain.cpp:431`, `:523`
- **Does**: puts a literal/clause into a canonical sign-normalized form before core conflict processing.
- **Provenance**: [C]

### `insert_fresh_factors_in_todo`
- **Where**: `src/nlsat/nlsat_explain.cpp:591`
- **Does**: pushes newly-produced factors of a polynomial back onto the projection worklist.
- **Provenance**: [C]

### `add_lcs`
- **Where**: `src/nlsat/nlsat_explain.cpp:623`
- **Does**: adds leading-coefficient nonvanishing conditions for a set of polynomials in a variable (McCallum projection operator, leading-coefficient part).
- **Provenance**: [C]/[P] (McCallum 1998 projection)

### `psc_resultant_sample`, `psc`, `psc_discriminant`, `psc_resultant`
- **Where**: `src/nlsat/nlsat_explain.cpp:644`, `:663`, `:717`, `:738`
- **Does**: compute (sampled) PSC chains and discriminant/pairwise-resultant projection sets — the two other McCallum projection operators (discriminant, resultant).
- **Provenance**: [C]/[P]

### `test_root_literal`, `add_root_literal`
- **Where**: `src/nlsat/nlsat_explain.cpp:751`, `:759`
- **Does**: builds/tests a `root[i]` atom (i-th real root of `p`) used for linear/quadratic root literal construction.
- **Provenance**: [C]

### `mk_linear_root`, `mk_plinear_root`, `mk_quadratic_root`
- **Where**: `src/nlsat/nlsat_explain.cpp:776`, `:790`, `:821`
- **Does**: rewrites a root atom into an explicit linear or quadratic inequality when the defining polynomial has low enough degree (avoids keeping the literal as an opaque root object).
- **Provenance**: [C]

### `ensure_sign`, `ensure_sign_quad`
- **Where**: `src/nlsat/nlsat_explain.cpp:856`, `:868`
- **Does**: normalizes leading coefficient sign for linear/quadratic root construction.
- **Provenance**: [C]

### `cac_add_cell_lits`
- **Where**: `src/nlsat/nlsat_explain.cpp:906`
- **Does**: adds the literals describing the current single CAD cell (cylindrical algebraic coverings-style, "cac" = cylindrical algebraic covering) for a variable.
- **Provenance**: [C]/[P] (CAC / NuCAD-adjacent single-cell construction)

### `all_univ`, `levelwise_single_cell`, `extract_max_polys`
- **Where**: `src/nlsat/nlsat_explain.cpp:928`, `:944`, `:974`
- **Does**: checks whether all polys are univariate in `x`; dispatches to the `levelwise.cpp` single-cell projection when applicable; extracts the maximal-level polynomials to project.
- **Provenance**: [C]

### `project` (poly-vector form, and literal-vector public entry)
- **Where**: `src/nlsat/nlsat_explain.cpp:988`, `:1635`
- **Does**: top-level projection driver: eliminates variable `x` (or the max var) from a polynomial/literal set, producing the projection-set literals.
- **Provenance**: [C]

- **Where**: `src/nlsat/nlsat_explain.cpp:1125`, `:1276`, `:1404`

- **Provenance**: [C]

### `main`, `process`, `process2`
- **Where**: `src/nlsat/nlsat_explain.cpp:1433`, `:1553`, `:1458`

- **Where**: `src/nlsat/nlsat_explain.cpp:1486`, `:1535`

## src/math/lp/ — simplex + LIA

`int_solver::check` (`src/math/lp/int_solver.cpp:432`, real work in the `imp::check` at `:272`) is
the LIA decision-procedure pipeline, run after the (real-relaxation) simplex is feasible. Read in
full — the **actual dispatch order**, gated by scheduling predicates, is:

```
1. gcd test (should_apply || dio ignoring terms)         -> m_gcd()
2. patch_basic_columns()                                  (try to round basic columns to int)
3. int_cube (should_find_cube, period m_int_find_cube_period)
4. find_lcube (should_find_lcube, "largest cube" test, exponential backoff m_lcube_period)
5. move_non_basic_columns_to_bounds()
6. hnf_cut() (should_hnf_cut, period hnf_cut_period, exponential backoff on failure)
7. solve_dioph_eq() (should_solve_dioph_eq, period dio_calls_period, adaptive)
8. gomory(lia).get_gomory_cuts(2) (should_gomory_cut, period m_int_gomory_cut_period)
9. int_branch(lia)() (branch-and-bound, the fallback of last resort)
```
- **Where**: `src/math/lp/int_solver.cpp:272`-`303`
- **Logics**: QF_LIA, LIA (and QF_UFLIA etc. via theory combination)
- **Default**: on by default; `dio()`/gomory/hnf/lcube individually toggleable, see Parameters
- **Provenance**: [C] (read in full)

### `dioph_eq` — Diophantine-equation handler (Griggio-style; file header credits Alberto Griggio)

### `check`, `explain`, `some_terms_are_ignored` (public API)
- **Where**: `src/math/lp/dioph_eq.h:32`-`34`; impl `src/math/lp/dioph_eq.cpp:2752`, `:2756`, `:2760`
- **Logics**: QF_LIA/LIA
- **Default**: on (`dio`=True in `lp_params_helper.pyg`)
- **Does**: maintains a certificate matrix (S-set of substituted-eliminated equalities, fresh-variable defs, F-set of not-yet-eliminated rows) over the integer equalities implied by tight rows, incrementally rewriting/eliminating to either prove integer infeasibility (`conflict`) or leave a simplified system.
- **Provenance**: [C]

### `eliminate_var_in_f`, `eliminate_var_in_f_with_term`, `fresh_var_step`, `rewrite_eqs`
- **Where**: `src/math/lp/dioph_eq.cpp:2299`, `:2354`, `:2549`, `:2643`
- **Does**: the elimination step of the Diophantine procedure — substitutes a variable using gcd-normalized row, possibly introducing a fresh existential variable when the gcd doesn't divide evenly (classical technique for solving linear Diophantine systems).
- **Provenance**: [C]/[P] (integer Gaussian-elimination-with-fresh-variables, cf. Pugh's Omega test family)

### `normalize_e_by_gcd`, `gcd_of_coeffs`
- **Where**: `src/math/lp/dioph_eq.cpp:1390`, `:1348`
- **Does**: gcd-normalizes an equation row; a non-1 gcd not dividing the constant is itself a conflict (implicit gcd test inside the Diophantine solver).
- **Provenance**: [C]

### `tighten_terms_with_S`, `tighten_on_espace`, `tighten_bounds_for_term_column`, `tighten_bounds_for_non_trivial_gcd`, `tighten_bound_kind`
- **Where**: `src/math/lp/dioph_eq.cpp:1660`, `:1791`, `:1818`, `:1939`, `:1966`
- **Does**: derives tightened variable bounds from the eliminated-equation set (e.g. `x = 2y+1` tightens parity/bound on `x`), feeding back into the LP relaxation.
- **Provenance**: [C]

## src/math/lp/ — NLA (nla_solver / nla_core)

`core::check(unsigned level)` (`src/math/lp/nla_core.cpp:881`) is the full nonlinear-arithmetic
decision pipeline invoked from `theory_lra`'s final-check when the LP relaxation is feasible but
some monomial constraint (`m = Πxᵢ`) is violated by the current point. **Read in full** — the
pipeline, in order, with early-exit `no_effect()` gating after each stage:

```
1. m_patcher.patch_monomials()                — try to nudge point onto exact monomial values
2. m_monomial_bounds.generate_lemmas()         — interval-derived monomial bound lemmas
3. refine_pseudo_linear()                      — incremental-linearization axioms for pseudo-linear monomials (params().arith_nl_reduce_pseudo_linear)
4. m_squeeze_schedule: m_monomial_bounds.optimize_nl_bounds()   — LP-based bound optimization, eager or on horner cadence
5. weighted choice among: {1: horner_lemmas, 1: grobner(), 1: add_bounds}   (check_weighted, arith.nl.horner / arith.nl.grobner / arith_nl_branching)
6. m_nra.check_assignment()                    — call into nlsat as an assignment-checking oracle (arith.nl.nra_check_assignment)
7. bounded_nlsat()                             — bounded call into full nlsat (should_run_bounded_nlsat, arith.nl.delay)
8. m_basics.basic_lemma(true) then (false)     — sign/zero/neutral-element lemmas, derived-mode then model-based-mode
9. m_divisions.check()
10. weighted choice among: {6: order_lemma, 2: monotonicity_lemma, 1: tangent_lemma}
```
- **Where**: `src/math/lp/nla_core.cpp:881`-`980` (weights at `:928`, `:975`)
- **Logics**: QF_NIA/QF_NRA/NIA/NRA (also drives `int_solver`'s LIA loop for the integer parts)
- **Default**: on, `arith.nl`=True (relevant only when `arith.solver`=6, the default `lra` solver)
- **Provenance**: [C] (read in full)

### `check_weighted`
- **Where**: `src/math/lp/nla_core.cpp:826`
- **Does**: round-robins among a small set of alternative lemma-generation strategies by integer weight, so e.g. order lemmas (weight 6) are tried roughly 6x as often as tangent lemmas (weight 1) — did not verify the exact selection algorithm (weighted round-robin vs. weighted random) from this excerpt alone.
- **Provenance**: [C] signature+call sites / did not verify internal selection policy

### `check_power`
- **Where**: `src/math/lp/nla_core.cpp:847` (`:853` calls into `powers::check`)
- **Does**: entry point for `x^y` (real/integer power) reasoning, delegated to `nla_powers`.
- **Provenance**: [C]

### horner (Horner-form interval evaluation heuristic)

### `horner_lemmas`, `lemmas_on_row`, `row_is_interesting`, `lemmas_on_expr`, `row_has_monomial_to_refine`
- **Where**: `src/math/lp/horner.cpp:102`, `:77`, `:41`, `:70`, `:31`
- **Logics**: QF_NIA/QF_NRA/NIA/NRA
- **Default**: `arith.nl.horner`=True, period `arith.nl.horner_frequency` (4), rows longer than `arith.nl.horner_row_length_limit` (10) skipped
- **Does**: evaluates each interesting row's polynomial in Horner form using interval arithmetic (via `nla_intervals`), looking for a row whose interval doesn't contain 0 (or straddles a needed sign) — a cheap alternative to full Gröbner-basis or cross-nested reasoning, tried before Gröbner in the weighted round-robin.
- **Provenance**: [C]

### cross_nested — common-factor extraction for Horner-style nonlinear expressions

### `run`, `extract_common_factor`, `has_common_factor`, `proceed_with_common_factor`
- **Where**: `src/math/lp/cross_nested.h:65`, `:87`, `:108`, `:126`
- **Does**: rewrites a sum-of-products expression by pulling out a common variable factor across terms (a Horner-like nested factoring), enabling `horner`'s interval bound to be tighter.
- **Provenance**: [C]

### `explore_expr_on_front_elem`, `explore_expr_on_front_elem_vars`, `explore_of_expr_on_sum_and_var`, `calc_occurences`, `split_with_var`
- **Where**: `src/math/lp/cross_nested.h:251`, `:167`, `:284`, `:200`, `:435`
- **Does**: the recursive search over which variable to factor out next, driven by an occurrence-count heuristic (`calc_occurences`) with random tie-breaking (`m_random_bit`).
- **Provenance**: [C]

## src/math/ other subdirectories

### polynomial/ — multivariate polynomial manager + algebraic numbers (live, used throughout nlsat/lp)

### `polynomial::manager::gcd`, `derivative`, `square_free`/`is_square_free`, `exact_pseudo_remainder`/`pseudo_remainder`, `quasi_resultant`, `resultant`, `discriminant`, `psc_chain`, `factor`
- **Where**: `src/math/polynomial/polynomial.h:443`, `:695`, `:700/705/710/715`, `:782/793`, `:859`, `:865`, `:871`, `:876`, `:993`
- **Logics**: QF_NRA/NRA (via nlsat), QF_NIA/QF_NRA/NIA/NRA (via nla_grobner/horner's polynomial layer)
- **Does**: the multivariate polynomial-manager primitives (gcd, derivative, square-free part, pseudo-remainder, resultant, discriminant, PSC chain, full factorization) that `nlsat_explain.cpp` and `nla_core`'s Gröbner/Horner paths are built on.
- **Provenance**: [C] (signatures) / did not verify each implementation body

### `algebraic_numbers::manager::isolate_roots` (3 overloads), `mk_root` (2 overloads), `eval_sign_at`, `compare`
- **Where**: `src/math/polynomial/algebraic_numbers.h:160/171/184/189`, `:201/213`, `:320`, `:266`
- **Does**: real-root isolation of a univariate polynomial (used as `anum` — algebraic number — construction), root selection by index, sign evaluation of a multivariate polynomial at an algebraic-number point assignment, and total-order comparison between two algebraic numbers — this is the number type nlsat's model is built from.
- **Provenance**: [C] (signatures)

### `upolynomial`: `gcd`/`euclid_gcd`/`subresultant_gcd`, `ext_gcd`, `sturm_seq`/`sturm_tarski_seq`, `descartes_bound`, `refine`, `factor`/`factor_core`, `zp_factor_square_free_berlekamp`, `hensel_lift`
- **Where**: `src/math/polynomial/upolynomial.h:369-376` (gcd variants), `:454` (ext_gcd), `:835/845` (Sturm), `:627` (Descartes), `:868` (refine), `:908` (factor); `upolynomial_factorization.h:54` (Berlekamp), `:74` (Hensel lift)
- **Does**: the univariate-polynomial toolkit backing root isolation — three GCD algorithms (naive Euclid, subresultant PRS, and a mod-p variant), Sturm/Sturm-Tarski sequences for real root counting, Descartes' rule of signs bound, interval refinement, and full integer factorization via Berlekamp (mod p) + Hensel lifting + search.
- **Provenance**: [C] (signatures) / [P] for the named classical algorithms (Sturm, Descartes, Berlekamp, Hensel) — did not independently verify against the papers

### grobner/ — TWO parallel Gröbner-basis implementations

- `src/math/grobner/grobner.h`/`.cpp` (ast-`expr*`-based, old): reachable **only** from `src/smt/theory_arith.h`. See Dead-code section — `theory_arith` is never instantiated in the live solver setup path.
- `src/math/grobner/pdd_solver.h`/`.cpp` (pdd-based, current): reachable from `nla_grobner.{h,cpp}`, `nla_core.cpp`, `nra_solver.h`, and independently from `src/sat/sat_anf_simplifier.cpp` (ANF/ XOR-heavy CNF simplification in the SAT preprocessor).
- **Does** (pdd_solver): `add`/`add_subst` register polynomial equations (as `dd::pdd`s) with a dependency tag; `simplify`/`saturate` run Buchberger-style S-polynomial reduction to a (partial) Gröbner basis; `step`/`pick_next` are the internal work-queue primitives.
- **Where**: `src/math/grobner/pdd_solver.h:143-165`
- **Provenance**: [C]

### interval/ — `dep_intervals`: dependency-tracked rational interval arithmetic
- **Where**: `src/math/interval/dep_intervals.h` (interval struct + `set_lower`/`set_upper`/`is_zero`/`add_deps` at `:58`-`106`)
- **Does**: the concrete interval-arithmetic engine `nla_intervals` is built on — every bound carries an optional dependency (`u_dependency*`) so a derived `[lo,hi]` can be explained back to the LP-column bounds that produced it.
- **Provenance**: [C]

### dd/ — BDDs and PDDs (live, used by NLA Gröbner + SAT ANF)
- `dd_bdd.h/.cpp`: `apply`, `mk_quant`, `mk_not_rec`, `mk_ite_rec` (`src/math/dd/dd_bdd.h:139-150`) — a standard reduced-ordered BDD package; did not verify which of the arithmetic engines (vs. `muz`/`sat`) actually calls it directly — `pdd_solver.h` is the confirmed NLA-facing consumer, not `dd_bdd.h` itself.
- `dd_pdd.h/.cpp`: the "poly decision diagram" (PDD) representation — `reduce`, `try_spoly` (S-polynomial existence test) per header comment at `:17`-`29` — the polynomial datatype `nla_grobner`'s `pdd_solver` operates on.
- **Provenance**: [C] header comments / did not verify `dd_bdd` call sites into nlsat/lp directly

### realclosure/ — real closed field numeral package
- **Where**: `src/math/realclosure/realclosure.h` (`isolate_roots` at `:102`, `mk_transcendental`/`mk_pi`/`mk_e` at `:85`-`96`, `mk_infinitesimal` at `:72`)
- **Reachability**: only from `src/api/api_rcf.cpp` (the `Z3_rcf_*` C API) and `src/test/rcf.cpp`. **Not used by nlsat or nla_core** — nlsat's `anum`/`anum_manager` typedefs resolve to `algebraic_numbers::anum`/`manager` in `polynomial/algebraic_numbers.h` (confirmed: `src/nlsat/nlsat_types.h:194-195`), a separate, simpler algebraic-number implementation without realclosure's infinitesimal/transcendental extensions.
- **Does**: a richer real-closed-field numeral supporting infinitesimals and named transcendentals (π, e, arbitrary user transcendentals via an interval-refinement callback) — exposed to API clients but architecturally parallel to, not integrated with, the nlsat/NLA solving path.

- **Provenance**: [C] (reachability grep) / did not verify the command's own behavior

### hilbert/ — Hilbert basis computation for linear Diophantine systems

## Parameters

### `nlsat_params.pyg` (module `nlsat`) — all params, verbatim defaults
- **Where**: `src/nlsat/nlsat_params.pyg`
- `simple_check` BOOL **False** — precheck via `simple_checker` before full search.
- `variable_ordering_strategy` UINT **0** — 0 none, 1 BROWN, 2 TRIANGULAR, 3 ONLYPOLY (mapping to comparator functors in `nlsat_variable_ordering_strategy.cpp` not independently verified).
- `lazy` UINT **0** — "how lazy the solver is" (did not verify semantics).
- `reorder` BOOL **True** — allow variable reordering during search.
- `log_lemmas`/`log_lemma_smtrat`/`dump_mathematica` BOOL **False** — debug lemma dumping formats.
- `check_lemmas` BOOL **False** — re-check every lemma with an independent nlsat instance.
- `simplify_conflicts` BOOL **True** — simplify conflicts with equalities before resolution.
- `minimize_conflicts` BOOL **False** — shrink learned conflict cores.
- `randomize` BOOL **True** — randomize witness selection.
- `max_conflicts` UINT **UINT_MAX** — conflict budget.
- `shuffle_vars` BOOL **False** — fully random variable order.
- `inline_vars` BOOL **False** — inline isolable equation variables (incompatible with incremental mode per comment).
- `seed` UINT **0**.
- `factor` BOOL **True** — factor polynomials during conflict resolution.
- `add_all_coeffs` BOOL **False** — add all coefficients during projection (more complete, more expensive).
- `zero_disc` BOOL **False** — add zero-assumption for vanishing discriminants.
- `known_sat_assignment_file_name` STRING **""** — debug-only known-solution file.
- `lws` BOOL **True** — use the `levelwise.cpp` single-cell projection.
- `lws_spt_threshold` UINT **4** — min both-side poly count to use the spanning-tree optimization (<2 disables it).
- `lws_witness_subs_lc`/`lws_witness_subs_disc` BOOL **True**/**True** — witness substitution by leading coeff / discriminant.
- `canonicalize` BOOL **True**.
- **Provenance**: [C] (file read in full)

### `lp_params_helper.pyg` (module `lp`) — all params, verbatim defaults
- **Where**: `src/math/lp/lp_params_helper.pyg`
- `dio` BOOL **True** — enable the Diophantine-equality handler.
- `dio_branching_period` UINT **100**.
- `dio_cuts_enable_gomory` BOOL **False** — run Gomory alongside dio (only via this flag, unless the dio-unproductive-streak controller in `int_solver.cpp` starts it).
- `dio_gomory_enable_period` UINT **16** — consecutive unproductive dio calls before auto-starting Gomory+gcd.
- `dio_cuts_enable_hnf` BOOL **True**.
- `dio_ignore_big_nums` BOOL **True**.
- `dio_calls_period` UINT **1**, `dio_calls_period_decrease` UINT **2** — adaptive scheduling of the dio handler.
- `dio_run_gcd` BOOL **False** — only meaningful if `dio` is on.
- `dio_undo_max_work` UINT **1000000** — work budget for incremental pop of dio's certificate matrix before falling back to full rebuild.
- `lcube` BOOL **True** — enable the largest-cube integer feasibility test.
- `lcube_flips` UINT **16** — max coordinate flips repairing a rounded cube center.
- `int_hammer_period` UINT **4** — period for cube/hnf/gomory heuristics.
- `random_hammers` BOOL **True** — draw periodic-heuristic gates at random (1/period rate) instead of deterministic modulus (explicitly to avoid resonance with the search, per the comment at `int_solver.cpp:203-208`).
- **Provenance**: [C] (file read in full)

### `smt_params_helper.pyg` — `arith.*` section (selected; full section is ~60 lines starting at line 64)
- **Where**: `src/params/smt_params_helper.pyg:64`-`123` (did not read past 123; more `arith.*` entries may exist below)
- `arith.solver` UINT **6** — 0 none, 1 Bellman-Ford (diff-logic), 2 simplex+nl, 3 Floyd-Warshall (diff-logic), 4 UTVPI, 5 infinitary LRA, 6 LRA solver (the default, `lar_solver`-based).
- `arith.nl` BOOL **True** — relevant only when `arith.solver=2` per the doc text (note: this reads as inconsistent with `arith.nl.*` sub-params below being described as relevant when `arith.solver=6` — **did not resolve this apparent doc inconsistency**, flagging rather than guessing).
- `arith.nl.nra` BOOL **True** — fall back to `nra_solver`/nlsat when incremental linearization stalls.
- `arith.nl.nra_check_assignment` / `_max_fail` BOOL **True** / UINT **7**.
- `arith.nl.branching` BOOL **True** — branch on integer vars in nonlinear clusters.
- `arith.nl.rounds` UINT **1024**.
- `arith.nl.order` BOOL **True**, `.order.binomial_sign` BOOL **True**.
- `arith.nl.tangents` BOOL **True**, `.tangents.box_corners` BOOL **False**.
- `arith.nl.horner` BOOL **True**, `.horner_subs_fixed` UINT **2**, `.horner_frequency` UINT **4**, `.horner_row_length_limit` UINT **10**.
- `arith.nl.grobner` BOOL **True** plus 10 sub-tuning params (`grobner_row_length_limit`=10, `grobner_frequency`=4, `grobner_eqs_growth`=10, `grobner_expr_size_growth`=2, `grobner_expr_degree_growth`=2, `grobner_max_simplified`=10000, `grobner_cnfl_to_report`=1, `grobner_propagate_quotients`=True, `grobner_gcd_test`=True, `grobner_exp_delay`=True, `grobner_adaptive`=False, `grobner_subs_fixed`=1, `grobner_expand_terms`=True, `grobner_perfect_squares`=True).
- `arith.nl.monomial_sandwich` BOOL **False** (+ `.max_fanout` UINT 0) — off by default; "derive bound on a monomial factor by pairing two LP rows that share the other factor".
- `arith.nl.monomial_binomial_sign` BOOL **False** — off by default; explicitly documented as a deterministic replacement for `order_lemma_on_binomial_sign`.
- `arith.nl.reduce_pseudo_linear` BOOL **True**.
- `arith.nl.delay` UINT **10** — final-check calls before invoking bounded nlsat.
- `arith.nl.propagate_linear_monomials` BOOL **True**, `.linearize_violated_monomials` BOOL **True**.
- `arith.nl.optimize_bounds` BOOL **True**, `.optimize_bounds_lp_max_vars` UINT **120**.
- `arith.nl.cross_nested` BOOL **True**.
- `arith.nl.log` BOOL **False**.
- `arith.propagate_eqs` BOOL **True**, `.epsilon` DOUBLE **1.0**, `.propagation_mode` UINT **1**, `.branch_cut_ratio` UINT **2**, `.int_eq_branch` BOOL **False**, `.ignore_int` BOOL **False**, `.eager_eq_axioms` BOOL **True**, `.auto_config_simplex` BOOL **False**, `.validate` BOOL **False**.

## Dead or unreachable

### Old ast-based Gröbner basis (`src/math/grobner/grobner.{h,cpp}`) — dead in practice
- **Finding**: its only in-repo consumer is `src/smt/theory_arith.h`. `theory_arith<C>` is
  explicitly template-instantiated in `src/smt/theory_arith.cpp:24-29` (keeping it compiling) but
  **never allocated/registered** anywhere: `grep -rn "alloc(smt::theory_arith\|new theory_arith\|theory_arith<"` across all `.cpp` outside `theory_arith.cpp` itself returns nothing, and
  `src/smt/smt_setup.cpp:605` registers only `smt::theory_lra`. So the entire legacy arithmetic
  theory plugin — and by extension this old Gröbner module — is compiled-but-unreachable dead
  code in the live solving path.
- **Provenance**: [C] (grep across `src/smt/*.cpp` + read of `smt_setup.cpp`)

### `subpaving/` — reachable only as an interactive shell command, not a solving path
- **Finding**: the only consumer outside `src/math/subpaving/` is `src/cmd_context/extra_cmds/subpaving_cmds.{h,cpp}`, installed unconditionally by `smtlib_frontend.cpp:167`. No tactic, theory, or default pipeline references it. Did not verify whether the installed command is invoked by any `.smt2` script directive short of a bespoke command name.
- **Provenance**: [C] (grep)

### `realclosure/` — reachable only via the C API, architecturally parallel to nlsat, not used by it
- **Finding**: `algebraic_numbers::anum`/`manager` (from `polynomial/`) is nlsat's numeral type
  (`nlsat_types.h:194-195`), not `rcf_manager`. `realclosure.h` is consumed only by
  `api/api_rcf.cpp` and `test/rcf.cpp`.
- **Provenance**: [C] (grep + type-alias read)

### `hilbert/` — reachable only from the Datalog/CHC engine (`muz`), not the SMT arithmetic core
- **Finding**: sole consumers are `src/muz/rel/karr_relation.h` and
  `src/muz/transforms/dl_mk_karr_invariants.h`. No reference from `nlsat`, `math/lp`, or `smt`.
- **Provenance**: [C] (grep)

### `nla_coi::init()` — did not verify it is called from `nla_core::check`
- **Finding**: the pipeline read in full at `nla_core.cpp:881-980` never mentions `m_coi` or
  `coi.init()`. Either it's invoked elsewhere in the 1150+ remaining lines of `nla_core.cpp` not
  read in this session, or it is unused/vestigial. **Flagging as unverified, not asserting dead.**
- **Provenance**: [C] partial-file read / did not verify

### `src/math/automata/`, `src/math/bigfix/` — do not exist in this codebase
- **Finding**: `find src -iname "*automata*" -o -iname "*bigfix*"` returns nothing anywhere in
  the repository, and `ls src/math` (9 entries: dd, grobner, hilbert, interval, lp, polynomial,
  realclosure, simplex, subpaving) confirms no such subdirectories. These were named in the task
  brief but are not present at HEAD e18d63b — possibly confused with another project or a
  different Z3 branch/fork.
- **Provenance**: [C] (directory listing + repo-wide filename search)

### `arith.nl` doc-text inconsistency — flagged, not resolved
- **Finding**: `smt_params_helper.pyg:66` says `arith.nl` is "relevant only if smt.arith.solver=2"
  while the surrounding `arith.nl.*` sub-parameters' own doc strings and the `nla_core.cpp` code
  path both indicate they matter when `arith.solver=6` (the default `lra` solver, which is what
  `nla_core`/`theory_lra` actually use). Did not determine whether this is a stale comment or a
  real second, unverified `arith.solver=2` code path.
- **Provenance**: [C] (param file text) / did not verify against `arith.solver=2`'s code path

### Old vs. new Gröbner naming collision — a documentation trap, not dead code
- **Finding**: two live "grobner" concepts coexist and are easy to conflate: the smt_params
  doc-strings under `arith.nl.grobner*` describe `nla_grobner` (pdd-based, live); the *file*
  `math/grobner/grobner.{h,cpp}` (ast-based) is the dead one above. Anyone searching the codebase
  by the literal string "grobner" will find both; only reachability analysis (not naming)
  distinguishes them.
- **Provenance**: [I] (synthesized from the two findings above)

# Part 8 — `src/ast/sls/` and other `src/ast/` subsystems


Repo: `/home/mjbommar/projects/personal/axeyum/references/z3`, HEAD `e18d63b`. Read-only.
Provenance tag on every entry: `[C]` = read from code (grep -n / file read), `[P]` = paper/doc claim, `[I]` = inference from code shape (not a direct statement in code).

---

## How is SLS reached?

**There are TWO independent SLS implementations in this tree**, easy to conflate:

1. **Legacy engine — `sls_engine`** (`src/ast/sls/sls_bv_engine.{h,cpp}`, global namespace, `bv_util`/`mpz`-based). Constructed only by the `qfbv-sls` tactic.
   - `sls_engine::sls_engine` ctor — `src/ast/sls/sls_bv_engine.cpp:33`. `[C]`
   - Tactic wrapper `sls_tactic` allocs it — `src/tactic/sls/sls_tactic.cpp:157` (`m_engine = alloc(sls_engine, m, p);`). `[C]`
   - `mk_qfbv_sls_tactic` — `src/tactic/sls/sls_tactic.cpp:260`; gated `and_then(fail_if_not(mk_is_qfbv_probe()), …)` at `:225-227` — **QF_BV only**. `[C]`
   - Also reused by MaxSMT/optimization: `bvsls_opt_engine` (`src/ast/sls/bvsls_opt_engine.cpp:22` ctor) is constructed at `src/opt/opt_sls_solver.h:212` inside `opt::sls_solver::bvsls_opt(...)`. `[C]`
   - Tactic registration: `ADD_TACTIC("qfbv-sls", …)` — `src/tactic/sls/sls_tactic.h:29`. `[C]`

2. **New engine — `sls::` namespace** (`sls_context`, `sls_smt_solver`, `sls_smt_plugin`, plugin architecture: `arith_plugin`, `bv_plugin`, `euf_plugin`, `array_plugin`, `basic_plugin`, `datatype_plugin`, `seq_plugin`). Three call paths:
   - **(a) Standalone tactic `sls-smt`.** `sls_smt_tactic` allocs `sls::smt_solver` — `src/tactic/sls/sls_tactic.cpp:44` (`m_sls = alloc(sls::smt_solver, m, p);`), `mk_sls_smt_tactic` at `src/tactic/sls/sls_tactic.cpp:267`. Not logic-gated the way qfbv-sls is (no `mk_is_qfbv_probe` guard) — handles general SMT via `sls::context` plugin dispatch. Tactic name registered `ADD_TACTIC("sls-smt", …)` — `src/tactic/sls/sls_tactic.h:30`. `[C]`
   - **(b) SAT-solver-embedded co-processor, new `sat/smt/euf_solver` stack.** `sat::smt::sls_solver` (in `src/sat/smt/sls_solver.cpp`) allocs `sls::smt_plugin` at `src/sat/smt/sls_solver.cpp:115` inside `init_search()`. It is itself added as a sub-solver ONLY when `get_config().m_sls_enable` is true — `src/sat/smt/euf_solver.cpp:191-192` (`if (get_config().m_sls_enable) add_solver(alloc(sls::solver, *this));`). `[C]`
   - **(c) Legacy `smt::context` stack.** `theory_sls` (`src/smt/theory_sls.cpp`) allocs `sls::smt_plugin` at `src/smt/theory_sls.cpp:96`, itself registered by `setup::setup_sls()` — `src/smt/smt_setup.cpp:773-776` — ONLY when `m_params.m_sls_enable` is true. `[C]`
   - **(d) Parallel/portfolio worker.** `parallel::sls_worker::sls_worker` allocs `sls::smt_solver` directly — `src/smt/smt_parallel.cpp:888` (confirmed at this exact line). One portfolio thread runs pure SLS regardless of the `sls.enable` param (this path does not gate on it — it's a fixed portfolio member). `[C]`

**The `m_sls_enable` param** is `smt.sls.enable`, default **`False`** — `src/params/smt_params_helper.pyg:156`. Companion `smt.sls.parallel` (run SLS concurrently with CDCL vs. sequential), default **`True`** — `src/params/smt_params_helper.pyg:157`. Both flow into `smt_params.h:119-120` / `smt_params.cpp:56-57` and are read at `theory_sls.cpp:207` (`m_parallel_mode = p.m_sls_parallel`).

**Third, unrelated, SLS-flavored code: DDFW at the SAT level.** `sat::ddfw_wrapper` (`src/sat/sat_ddfw_wrapper.{h,cpp}`) wraps the DDFW core `sls::ddfw` — whose implementation, per the task brief, physically lives in `src/ast/sls/sat_ddfw.{h,cpp}` (namespace `sls`, class `ddfw`) even though it is a bare SAT-level local-search core, not the term-level SLS engine above. It is allocated inside `sat::solver` at `src/sat/sat_solver.cpp:1391` and `:1472` (`m_local_search = alloc(ddfw_wrapper);`), gated by `sat_params`/`sat_config`: `m_config.m_local_search` (param `sat.local_search`, `BOOL`, default **`False`** — `src/params/sat_params.pyg:71`) and used for the `local_search` case-split phase (`PS_LOCAL_SEARCH`, `sat_config.h:31,82`). `[C]`

**Summary of defaults**: NONE of the SLS/DDFW routes run by default for a plain `(check-sat)` on any logic.
- `qfbv-sls` / `sls-smt`: only if explicitly invoked as a tactic.
- `sat.local_search` (DDFW inside the CDCL SAT solver): off by default (`False`).
- `smt.sls.enable` (SLS as SMT co-processor, both new and legacy stacks): off by default (`False`).
- Parallel portfolio's dedicated `sls_worker` thread: did not verify whether/when it's scheduled — grep only confirms its constructor exists and is unconditional within that struct; whether `parallel::` mode itself is on by default, and whether the sls_worker is always spawned inside it, is "did not verify".

`sls_params.pyg` module doc line confirms: *"Stochastic Local Search Solver (invoked by sls-qfbv and sls-smt tactics or enabled by smt.sls.enable=true)"* `[C]` (note: doc says `sls-qfbv`, actual registered tactic name is `qfbv-sls` — see `sls_tactic.h:29`; doc/name mismatch, not verified which is authoritative for the CLI).

## `static_features.cpp` — the feature vector

Confirmed as the input to `src/smt/smt_setup.cpp`'s configuration-selection logic: `setup::setup_unknown()` builds a `static_features st(m_manager); st.collect(...)` (`smt_setup.cpp:806-809`) and both the no-quantifier and quantified overloads of `setup_unknown` branch on its fields (e.g. `st.m_has_real`, `st.m_num_quantifiers`, and — in `setup_relevancy` — `st.m_has_bv` combined with `st.m_num_quantifiers == 0` to force `relevancy_lvl = 0`, `smt_setup.cpp:828-836`). `[C]`

Every field of the `static_features` struct (`src/ast/static_features.h:44-108`), one entry each — this is the enumerated "feature vector":

- `m_cnf` (bool) — whether the formula is already in CNF shape
- `m_num_exprs` — total expr count visited
- `m_num_roots` — number of top-level asserted roots
- `m_max_depth` — maximum term-tree depth
- `m_num_quantifiers` — count of quantifiers
- `m_num_quantifiers_with_patterns` — quantifiers carrying an explicit pattern
- `m_num_quantifiers_with_multi_patterns` — quantifiers carrying a multi-pattern
- `m_num_clauses` — top-level clause count
- `m_num_bin_clauses` — binary-clause count
- `m_num_units` — unit-clause count
- `m_sum_clause_size` — sum of clause sizes (for average-size derivation)
- `m_num_nested_formulas` — formulas nested inside non-Boolean structure
- `m_num_bool_exprs` — Boolean-sorted expr count
- `m_num_bool_constants` — Boolean 0-arity constant count
- `m_num_ite_trees` — number of maximal ITE-tree groupings
- `m_max_ite_tree_depth` — deepest ITE-tree
- `m_sum_ite_tree_depth` — sum of ITE-tree depths
- `m_num_ands` — nested-AND count
- `m_num_ors` — nested-OR count
- `m_num_iffs` — iff count
- `m_num_ite_formulas` — ITEs at Boolean sort
- `m_num_ite_terms` — ITEs at non-Boolean sort
- `m_num_sharing` — DAG-sharing measure (subterm reuse count)
- `m_num_interpreted_exprs` — interpreted (theory) expr count excluding bool
- `m_num_uninterpreted_exprs` — uninterpreted expr count
- `m_num_interpreted_constants` — interpreted 0-arity constants excluding bool
- `m_num_uninterpreted_constants` — uninterpreted 0-arity constants
- `m_num_uninterpreted_functions` — uninterpreted functions of arity > 0
- `m_num_eqs` — equality atom count
- `m_has_rational` (bool) — any rational literal present
- `m_has_int` (bool) — any Int-sorted term
- `m_has_real` (bool) — any Real-sorted term
- `m_has_bv` (bool) — any bit-vector term
- `m_has_fpa` (bool) — any floating-point term
- `m_has_sr` (bool) — any special-relations term
- `m_has_str` (bool) — any String-sorted seq term
- `m_has_seq_non_str` (bool) — any non-String Sequence-sorted term
- `m_has_arrays` (bool) — any array term
- `m_has_ext_arrays` (bool) — uses extended (non-basic) array theory
- `m_arith_k_sum` (rational) — sum of numeral literals appearing in arithmetic atoms
- `m_num_arith_terms` — arithmetic-family term count
- `m_num_arith_eqs` — arithmetic equalities `t = k`
- `m_num_arith_ineqs` — arithmetic inequalities
- `m_num_diff_terms` — subset of arith terms recognized as difference-logic shape
- `m_num_diff_eqs` — difference-logic equalities
- `m_num_diff_ineqs` — difference-logic inequalities
- `m_num_simple_eqs` — equalities of form `x = k`
- `m_num_simple_ineqs` — inequalities of form `x <= k` / `x >= k`
- `m_num_non_linear` — nonlinear-arithmetic term count
- `m_num_apps` (per-decl vector) — application count per `func_decl` id
- `m_num_theory_terms` (per-family vector) — term count per theory family id
- `m_num_theory_atoms` (per-family vector) — atom count per theory family id
- `m_num_theory_constants` (per-family vector) — constant count per theory family id
- `m_num_theory_eqs` (per-family vector) — equality count per theory family id
- `m_num_aliens` — count of "alien" subterms (foreign-theory terms embedded inside another theory's term)
- `m_num_aliens_per_family` (per-family vector) — alien-term count broken out by owning family
- `m_num_theories` — count of distinct theory family ids touched (`mark_theory`, `static_features.h:126-131`)
- `m_theories` (bool vector) — which family ids were touched

Derived query methods (not raw fields, but part of the same "feature vector" surface): `is_diff_term`/`is_diff_atom` (difference-logic shape recognizers), `is_gate` (Boolean-gate recognizer), `get_feature_vector` (`static_features.cpp:666`, flattens the struct to a `vector<double>` for external consumption e.g. by a learned/portfolio selector — did not verify any actual consumer of this flattened form beyond `smt_setup.cpp`'s direct field access).

**Provenance**: `[C]` throughout — every field name read directly from `src/ast/static_features.h:44-108`; the two `setup_unknown` consumer call sites read directly from `src/smt/smt_setup.cpp`.

## Parameters

### `sls_params.pyg` — COMPLETE transcription (`src/params/sls_params.pyg`)

Module doc: *"Stochastic Local Search Solver (invoked by sls-qfbv and sls-smt tactics or enabled by smt.sls.enable=true)"*.

| name | type | default | description |
|---|---|---|---|
| (max_memory_param) | UINT | (shared macro) | shared memory-limit param |
| `max_restarts` | UINT | `UINT_MAX` | maximum number of restarts |
| `max_repairs` | UINT | `1000` | maximum number of repairs before restart |
| `walksat` | BOOL | `1` (True) | use walksat assertion selection (instead of gsat) |
| `walksat_ucb` | BOOL | `1` (True) | use bandit heuristic for walksat assertion selection (instead of random) |
| `walksat_ucb_constant` | DOUBLE | `20.0` | the ucb constant c in the term `score + c * f(touched)` |
| `walksat_ucb_init` | BOOL | `0` (False) | initialize total ucb touched to formula size |
| `walksat_ucb_forget` | DOUBLE | `1.0` | scale touched by this factor every base restart interval |
| `walksat_ucb_noise` | DOUBLE | `0.0002` | add noise `0 <= 256 * ucb_noise` to ucb score for assertion selection |
| `walksat_repick` | BOOL | `1` (True) | repick assertion if randomizing in local minima |
| `scale_unsat` | DOUBLE | `0.5` | scale score of unsat expressions by this factor |
| `paws_init` | UINT | `40` | initial/minimum assertion weights |
| `paws_sp` | UINT | `52` | smooth assertion weights with probability `paws_sp / 1024` |
| `wp` | UINT | `100` | random walk with probability `wp / 1024` |
| `vns_mc` | UINT | `0` | in local minima, try Monte Carlo sampling `vns_mc` many 2-bit-flips per bit |
| `vns_repick` | BOOL | `0` (False) | in local minima, try picking a different assertion (only for walksat) |
| `restart_base` | UINT | `100` | base restart interval given by moves per run |
| `restart_init` | BOOL | `0` (False) | initialize to 0 or random value (=1) after restart |
| `early_prune` | BOOL | `1` (True) | use early pruning for score prediction |
| `random_offset` | BOOL | `1` (True) | use random offset for candidate evaluation |
| `rescore` | BOOL | `1` (True) | rescore/normalize top-level score every base restart interval |
| `dt_axiomatic` | BOOL | `True` | use axiomatic mode or model reduction for datatype solver |
| `track_unsat` | BOOL | `0` (False) | keep a list of unsat assertions as done in SAT — currently disabled internally |
| `random_seed` | UINT | `0` | random seed |
| `arith_use_lookahead` | BOOL | `True` | use lookahead solver for NIRA |
| `arith_allow_plateau` | BOOL | `False` | allow plateau moves during NIRA solving |
| `arith_use_clausal_lookahead` | BOOL | `False` | use clause based lookahead for NIRA |
| `bv_use_top_level_assertions` | BOOL | `True` | use top-level assertions for BV lookahead solver |
| `bv_use_lookahead` | BOOL | `True` | use lookahead solver for BV |
| `bv_allow_rotation` | BOOL | `True` | allow model rotation when repairing literal assignment |
| `str_update_strategy` | UINT | `2` | string update candidate selection: 0 = single-char-based, 1 = subsequence-based, 2 = combined |

**Provenance**: `[C]` — full file transcribed verbatim from `src/params/sls_params.pyg`, read in one pass.

### `pattern_inference_params_helper.pyg` — COMPLETE transcription (`src/params/pattern_inference_params_helper.pyg`)

Module doc: *"pattern inference (heuristics) for universal formulas (without annotation)"*.

| name | type | default | description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `max_multi_patterns` | UINT | `0` | threshold on extra multi-patterns created when patterns aren't provided; by default creates at most one multi-pattern when there is no unary pattern |
| `block_loop_patterns` | BOOL | `True` | block looping patterns during pattern inference |
| `decompose_patterns` | BOOL | `True` | allow decomposition of patterns into multipatterns |
| `arith` | UINT | `1` | 0 = no arithmetic-term patterns, 1 = use them only if no other pattern exists, 2 = always use them |
| `use_database` | BOOL | `False` | use pattern database (i.e. gates `expr_pattern_match`) |
| `enabled` | BOOL | `True` | enable the heuristic pattern-inference pass when patterns aren't provided |
| `arith_weight` | UINT | `5` | default weight for quantifiers whose only pattern has nested arithmetic terms |
| `non_nested_arith_weight` | UINT | `10` | default weight for quantifiers whose only pattern has non-nested arithmetic terms |
| `pull_quantifiers` | BOOL | `True` | pull nested quantifiers if no pattern was found |
| `avoid_skolems` | BOOL | `True` | avoid skolem functions when computing patterns |
| `warnings` | BOOL | `False` | enable/disable warning messages in the pattern inference module |

**Provenance**: `[C]` — full file transcribed verbatim.

- **`euf/CMakeLists.txt`**: likewise every `.cpp` in the directory is listed; no absent-file signal. `[C]`
